**Consequence for us:** the interpreter's public `compute` is report-all outcomes and does **not** surface the eager operand-error inventory (a kernel result-envelope channel), but it matches the focused downstream witnesses — the direct consumer is CLEARED, the unrelated computation produces a VALUE, and each represented instance carries a single outcome (ERRORED, never also CLEARED). No interpreter change was needed for THIS entry's witnesses; the SPEC-2026-07-23-15 supplement below sharpens the index-field half (which indexes the inventory SELECTS, the shared default staging, and the preliminary index poison the -15 differential exposed on the interpreter). *(Locked: `adapter.laws.ComputationEagerPrepassLawsTest` — invalid-operand reporting + direct-consumer classification + continued processing + interpreter parity, required-empty silence, empty/non-unique index reporting, and errored-not-cleared; the five channels themselves by `adapter.laws.ComputeResultChannelsLawsTest`.)*

## §11 supplement — computation SELECTS operand index fields (direct + induced) and applies full validation's index-default staging (source + dual-strategy differential, 2026-07-23, SPEC-2026-07-23-15)

> Fold: folded → §11.

In an expanded A12 model, computation's eager formal-input inventory is a **checked-plan union across all computations** — directly referenced fields; every field contained by an **ordinarily referenced group** (e.g. a group predicate in a common/alternative precondition) or by a conditionally referenced ancestor group; and the **index fields required by semantic-index or parallel-iteration use** — traversing operations, preconditions, filters, and semantic-index key expressions, with computed fields then excluded from formal input checking (`CalculationOperandsCollector` → `CalculationDependencies.getExpandedOperandFieldsOfCalculations`; `CalculationCommand` filters the ingested data to that set). The generated **preliminary mandatory/uniqueness rules run ONLY for index fields retained by the inventory** (`CalculationCommand.preliminaryValidationOfIndexFields`), so:

- an **unused** bad index (its group untouched by every computation) is **silent** — no `formalErrorsInOperands` entry;
- a **selected** empty/duplicate index reports `mandatoryField`/`uniqueIndex` through `formalErrorsInOperands` — and the selection is broader than a read trace: **iterating an index group selects its index** even when no operand references it (a `[Qty] * 2` computation inside the indexed Items group still requires Sku — the target-scope iteration IS the parallel-iteration machinery), a group reference in a precondition selects the group's contained index, semantic-index and cross-group parallel-iteration references select the joined indexes, and a `Having`-filter-only reference selects too (differentially confirmed, both strategies);
- formal ingestion applies **the SAME full-call index-default staging as full validation** (the SPEC-2026-07-23-14 §12 supplement): an eligible clean-empty enum index receives its declared S-value BEFORE the preliminary rules, so it draws no `mandatoryField` and its consumer computes on the token (`[Sku]` echoes `STD`); an ineligible shape (multi-row, admitted explicit, mandatory) keeps its ordinary prepass error;
- the prepass-invalidated index cell **poisons a computation that READS it** (`MainValidatorController.preliminaryError` → `markFieldAsInvalidForValidation` → `convertInvalidFieldsForCalculation`): the kernel's change projection is silent for the never-source-filled consumer, the report-all interpreter documents the same instance as CLEARED — never a VALUE computed from an empty-as-"" or duplicated read. An ordinary required-empty operand stays empty-as-0 (the entry-12 silence above).

The inventory is neither a runtime read trace nor a dependency schedule — whether an invalid producer poisons a dependent remains decided by the reached reads (the poison findings above). The legacy unique-entity/H-model path (all-noncomputed input, early abort) is outside this contract.

**Interpreter reconciliation (SPEC-2026-07-23-15):** compute stages the same eligible index defaults as full validation (call-local, never mutating the document), and the compute path's formal gate now carries the kernel's preliminary index invalidation — a DUPLICATED index cell is invalid for a reading computation, and an EMPTY index cell poisons its reader (unlike an ordinary required-empty field) — the class the -15 differential exposed (two sibling instances: empty and duplicate; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS). The eager `formalErrorsInOperands` channel itself stays kernel-only (the report-all projection documents outcomes, not envelope channels). *(Locked: `adapter.laws.ComputationEagerPrepassLawsTest` — the SPEC-15 arms: selected-vs-unused, iteration/group-ref/semantic-index/parallel-iteration/filter induction, absent + present-empty default staging with value parity, ineligible multi-row/admitted-explicit controls, channel separation, and the empty/duplicate poison projections — dynamic-Groovy · static-Java · interpreter; `interpreter.ComputationIndexDefaultMultiplatformTest` — the pure engine on JVM + Node.)*

## §9/§11 supplement — computations PARALLEL-ITERATE over sibling indexed groups; an invalid index anywhere marks every instance incorrectly calculated (source + differential, both strategies, 2026-07-09)

> Fold: folded → §11.

A computation whose operands reference OUT-OF-SCOPE sibling repeatable groups (all joined groups carrying the same-named INDEX field) runs the §9 parallel iteration: the target row keyed K reads each sibling's row with the SAME key — join-by-key, never physical row position (differentially pinned with scrambled row orders). An **unmatched side reads EMPTY** (empty-as-0 for a missing key row and for a wholly-uninstantiated side group — the calc still computes). Two calc-specific rules: the calc **never creates a repetition** (`CalculationController.isWiederholungGesetzt` — "no additional repetitions should be created by calculation"; a side-only key computes nothing), and an **INVALID index in ANY joined group** (a duplicated or empty key cell, target or side) marks **EVERY instance** of the computation "incorrectly calculated" — `CalculationCache.markFieldsUsingInvalidIndexGroupsAsIncorrectlyCalculated`, deliberately *"marking more field instances as invalid than strictly necessary"* — so even clean-keyed instances CLEAR (and poison dependents). Both strategies agree (tri-checked). *(Interpreter: the compute path reuses the rule-side `parallelIterationContexts` anchored on the TARGET's scope + an `invalidIndexGroup` all-instances guard; locked `adapter.laws.ComputeParallelIterationDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter … ComputeParallelIterationTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF96.)*

## §11/§12 supplement — validateFull does NOT run computations — validate and compute are separate operations (runtime, 2026-06-09)

> Fold: folded → §11.

The kernel keeps **validation** (`IDocumentRtService.validateFull`) and **computation** (`compute`) as distinct, independently-invoked operations — confirmed in the kernel dev docs (`a12-kernel/documentation/_merged/kernel-dev.md` §"Validating a document / Computations on a document": *"the use of validation and computation is very similar, therefore only one is described"*, and the worked sequence `validateFull(doc)` … `compute(doc)` … `result.appliedTo(doc)`). There is **no combined call**: `validateFull` validates the document's values **as-is** and does **not** compute computed fields first, and `compute` returns a *result* (the input is never mutated — applying it back via `appliedTo`/`apply` is a separate, opt-in step the consumer orchestrates).

**`applyTo` semantics — the apply-computed-back step (source: `IDocumentComputationResult`).** The third operation is `result.applyTo(doc)` → the **updated document** (`old + computed values`). Its javadoc is precise: it **applies the `getComputedFieldInstancesWithChanges` values and CLEARS** the instances in **both** `getComputedFieldInstancesWithErrors` **and** `getClearedFieldInstances`. So an **errored** computed value is *emptied* on apply, not carried as the bad value — same as a cleared one; only a clean VALUE is set. (`applyTo(DocumentV2)` returns a new doc, original unchanged; the deprecated `applyTo(IDocument)` mutates in place.)

**The post-application cell STATE, per result kind (source + tri-differential, both strategies, 2026-07-17; IG72 → IF126).** What `applyTo(DocumentV2)` *leaves in the applied document* — the per-cell refinement of the paragraph above, and the contract behind the interpreter's `Document.cellStateAt`. Mechanism (source: `DocumentComputationResultImpl.applyTo(DocumentV2)` folds cleared → errored → changed instances through `ComputedFieldInstanceImpl.applyTo(DocumentV2)`, which writes via `DocumentV2.withFieldValue` → `GroupDocCommonImpl.updateNested`/`withDirectFieldValue`; strategy-independent — `DocumentDynamicRtService` and `DocumentStaticRtService` share `DocumentAbstractRtService.compute` → `LowKernelApiUtils.callCalculator` → the same result impl):

| result kind (target's input state) | applyTo action | post-application state |
|---|---|---|
| VALUE, typed-unequal (`WithChanges`) — target **absent** | `withFieldValue(ptr, v)`: `updateNested` **creates missing ancestor rows**, `FieldInstanceV2.ofValue(v)` creates the cell | **present-value(v)** |
| VALUE, typed-unequal — target present (empty or stale) | `field.withValue(v)` | **present-value(v)** |
| VALUE **typed-equal** to the stored one (`WithoutErrors` only) | **not applied** (only `WithChanges` is) — untouched | present-value (indistinguishable from an applied write) |
| CLEARED (minted **only** for an input-FILLED cell — `getFilledComputedFieldInstances`, `valueExists` = non-null and non-`""`) | unguarded `withFieldValue(ptr, null)` — the instance **stays, value nulled** | **present-empty** — a clear is never a removal |
| computed-nothing — target present-EMPTY | **no instance minted** (not input-filled) → untouched | present-empty |
| computed-nothing — target **absent** | no instance minted → untouched | **absent** (the Set Y receipt's silence, as a state) |
| ERRORED (unconditional; non-empty computed value + error message) — target present | **guarded** `withFieldValue(ptr, null)` | **present-empty** |
| ERRORED — target **absent** | the guard `document.field(ptr) != null` fails → document returned **unchanged** | **absent** |
| untouched cells | never visited | placement state preserved (a stored `\r\n` stays — the IF124 normalization is eval-cache-side only, never the store) |

The errored branch's guard exists precisely because `withFieldValue(ptr, null)` would otherwise *create* a present-empty instance (`withDirectFieldValue` creates even for a null value) — so "errored is emptied like cleared" (above) holds only for a *present* target; an absent one stays absent. (The deprecated V1 `applyTo(IDocument)` clears errored instances *unguarded* via `DocumentUtils.clearFieldInstanceValue` — a V1-route difference outside the supported `DocumentV2` contract.) *(Interpreter: `DmInterpreter.applyComputations` → `Document.withComputed` reproduces every row of the table with no internal-store change — a null override empties a present placement and no-ops on an absent one; read back via `Document.cellStateAt` (IF126). Locked: `adapter.laws.AppliedCellStateDiffTest` — tri-check groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter on all nine shapes + the CRLF raw read — + multiplatform `interpreter … CellStateAtTest`.)*

**Consequence for us:** a *bare* `validateFull` sees a **computed field** as whatever the instance supplies — **empty** if absent (verified: `FieldFilled(CoverageWindow)` does **not** fire when only the `DateRange` operands are present), so a required-or-referenced computed field false-fires `mandatoryField` / misses its value. The **form-engine** answer applies the computation first. So our runtime-validate verbs **compose by default**: `model eval` / `rule eval` run **compute → applyTo → validateFull** (the form-engine flow), and **`--no-computations`** drops to the bare `validateFull`. The interpreter mirrors `applyTo` as the clean-room `DmInterpreter.applyComputations(doc)` (VALUE set, ERRORED/CLEARED emptied — INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF45), the kernel side (the differential oracle) as `KernelAdapter.evaluateApplied`. `model compute` stays the pure `compute` (what does a field compute to?); the document file is never mutated by any verb (read-only — `compute`/eval report, never write `--instance`). The **engine-level** `DmInterpreter.validate == validateFull` parity stays foundational (the differentials rest on it); the *composed* path has its own differential. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.EvalDocSeamLawsTest.validationDoesNotRunComputations` (the engine split) + `adapter.laws.ComputeApplyDiffTest` (interp compute-then-validate == kernel `compute().applyTo().validateFull()`).)*

## §12 supplement — Full vs Partial validation — `model eval` is FULL; partial may skip `*`-all-reps + `Having` rules (BA appendix; filter skip source + differential locked 2026-07-21)

> Fold: folded → §12.

The kernel has two validation modes (BA "Full Validation and Partial Validation", L5303-5321). **Full validation** — our `model eval` = `validateFull` — formal-validates all fields and evaluates every rule. **Partial validation** is a page-level pre-check over an app-specified *relevant-field* set: it guarantees never to report an error fixable only *outside* that set, but **does NOT guarantee complete checking of the relevant fields** — some checks are skipped (performance/simplicity). Named skip examples: rules referencing **all repetitions** via `*` when only some are relevant (explicitly incl. `NotAllFieldsFilled`, `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled`), and **`Having`-filter** rules. The exact skip-set is an **unstable implementation detail** (may change any release; projects must not depend on it). **Consequence:** every law we lock and every catalog example we ship is *full-validation* behaviour — a rule that fires under full validation may *not* fire under a partial page pre-check. For the pinned kernel 30.8.1, the filter case is exact and executable: every rule containing a `Having` filter is skipped before iteration or condition evaluation, even when its error field and every referenced repetition are relevant (`PartialValidationFilterSkipDiffTest`). A future kernel upgrade must re-check the source templates and differential rather than treating this versioned skip as a permanent public promise. Related: the mandatory-"star" in a field's label (BA "the star method") is a *separate decidable heuristic* over a fixed allow-list of presence constructs — a UI affordance outside rulekit's scope.

## §3 supplement — a fired message's `getErrorText` is the *interpolated* end-user text (runtime, 2026-06-14)

> Fold: folded → §13.

`IMessage.getErrorText()` returns the message with its tokens already resolved against the evaluated document, not the authored template (`$<fieldname>$` → the display string, `$<field.value>$` → the display value; no other substitution). Only the runtime can produce it. Treatment: `KernelAdapter.evaluate` surfaces it per message; the CLI returns it as `data.messages[].message` / `data.message` (FEATURE-GAPS G1). *(Locked: `adapter.MessageInterpolationTest`; CLI surfacing by `cli.RuleTestVerbTest.firesOnAViolatingInstance`.)*

## §3 supplement — `$field$` is the field's *display name*, and the kernel sources it from a caller-supplied `ILabelProvider` (runtime, 2026-06-23)

> Fold: folded → §13.

The token resolves through a three-step cascade. The caller-registered `ILabelProvider` (`IDocumentDynamicServiceConfig.getLabelProvider()`) is consulted first, and a present result wins **exactly, including the empty string**. If the provider is absent or returns no result, a nonempty localized model label wins; only then does the kernel emit the resolved `fullName/idx.idx` path+index debug fallback. dmtool deliberately supplies its label-or-name provider profile; a standalone interpreter consumer may select a different profile or no provider. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.DisplayNameDiffTest`; multiplatform `MessageDisplayPolicyTest`; source-grounded provider/fallback cascade.)*

## §3 supplement — a formal error silently suppresses the author's rule (runtime, 2026-06-06)

> Fold: folded → §3.

All four claims are canonical in §3 ("Suppression is branch-scoped — and it reaches presence checks"); the evidence skeleton:

- **The silent suppression** — a malformed value raises only the kernel's own formal-error code; a referencing author rule never fires. Observed codes: `zahlFalscheAnzahlNachkommastellen`, `zahlHatUngueltigeZeichen`, `fuehrendesBlank` — German internal identifiers, not `MVK_*`, locked as the observed contract. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.FormalErrorLawsTest`.)*
- **The sentinel attribution** — a formal error reports `rulePath = "formalePruefung"`; an author rule's is its slash-path — the discriminator in a validation report. *(Verified 2026-06-09; locked: `cli.EvalDocVerbTest.aFormalErrorIsReportedAsAKernelCheck`.)*
- **Branch-scoped, not rule-scoped** (RT-semantics drain, probe-corrected 2026-06-10): `healthyTrue Or [broken] > 0` still fires; the `And` twin is absorbed. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.FormalErrorScopeLawsTest`.)*
- **Even a presence check is UNKNOWN on a not-check-relevant field** (fuzz-caught 2026-06-24): `isWertAngegeben` backs `FieldFilled`/`FieldNotFilled` AND the fill quantifiers, so a malformed operand makes them undecidable — the quantifier tally counts UNKNOWN in neither bucket. *(Interpreter fixed at the shared root — `filledTri` + the 3-state `quantCount`; locked: `adapter.laws.CurrentRepetitionDiffTest.malformedErrorFieldAtFiringRow` + `QuantifierMalformedDiffTest`.)*

## §3 supplement — two more NUMBER sign formal errors: signed-zero and leading-plus (runtime, 2026-06-23)

> Fold: folded → §3.

Surfaced by the **differential fuzz net** (`adapter.perf.InterpreterKernelFuzzTest` — random generated documents, assert interp == kernel), which fed a `-0`:

- **A signed zero is a formal error** — `-0`, `-0.0`, `-0.00` (any minus sign on a zero magnitude) → **`zahlNullEnthaeltVorzeichen`**. A non-zero negative (`-5`, `-0.01`) and a plain zero (`0`, `0.00`) are clean.
- **A leading plus is a formal error** — `+5`, `+0`, `+0.5` → **`zahlMitPlus`** (distinct from `zahlHatUngueltigeZeichen`; the explicit `+` is its own error, not "invalid characters").
- **Ordering: signed-zero is checked BEFORE the decimal-separator/scale checks.** `-0.0` on a **scale-0** field is `zahlNullEnthaeltVorzeichen`, *not* `zahlHatDezimalTrenner` — so a malformed value can match several checks and the kernel's precedence matters. (Leading-plus likewise wins over signed-zero: `+0` → `zahlMitPlus`.)

Both ride `formalePruefung` (the sentinel rule path) like the other formal errors, and suppress a referencing author rule (branch-scoped). *(Locked: `adapter.laws.NumberSignFormalDiffTest` — interp vs kernel over a scale-2 and a scale-0 field; the precedence is part of the lock.)*

## §3 supplement — BASELINE value checks run for EVERY kind before the type format: charset + leading/trailing blank (probe + source, 2026-06-26)

> Fold: folded → §3.

(The charset half reconciles with the "`modelConfig.supportedCharacters` is enforced at runtime" finding below: the *model-level* slot IS DM-JSON-declarable and runtime-enforced; what is not declarable is a *per-field* charset — the BMP default below is the no-slot case.) `FormalChecker.checkFieldValueFormat` runs three baseline checks on **every filled field value**, regardless of kind (string/number/date/…), **before** the type-specific format check — and they **pre-empt** it:

- **legal charset** → **`ZeichenNichtImZeichensatz`**. The legal set is `metaData.getLegalCodepoints()` — a **programming-language/codegen** config (`MetaModelImpl`, the codegen target's restricted charset; cf. the codegen `MVK_INVALID_CHAR_IN_RESTRICTED_CHARSET` "not permitted for the programming language"). It is **NOT a DM-JSON field/model property** (no `charset` key on `StringType`/`IStringType` in 30.8.1). With none configured — a plain DM-JSON model — the **default legal charset is the BMP** (`0x0000-0xFFFF`; `CharsetUtil`: "empty list ≡ 0x0000-0xFFFF"). So **any supplementary-plane character** (a UTF-16 surrogate, e.g. an emoji `😀` U+1F600) is illegal; BMP chars (`é`/`ü`/`€`/`™`/CJK) are legal. Probe-confirmed on a plain string field.
- **leading blank** → **`fuehrendesBlank`**; **trailing blank** → **`nachfolgendesBlank`** (distinct codes). Probe-confirmed across STRING, NUMBER, and DATE — and they pre-empt the type check (a ` 2024-01-01` DATE is `fuehrendesBlank`, not a date-format error).

Order: charset → leading → trailing → (type format). This **corrects** the earlier suspect §3 "string charset is not DM-JSON-declarable / no serialized key" note — the basis was about a per-field `StringType.charset` key (which indeed doesn't exist), but the runtime check fires anyway from the **default BMP** charset; the conclusion ("out of scope") was WRONG.

**Is the charset customizable? Yes — but at the CODEGEN/PROJECT level, not via the DM model.** A restricted charset *can* be declared (`KernelUtils.validateCharset`/`simplifyCharset` process a `restrictedCharset`), and the legal codepoints are **baked into the generated meta-model** by the codegen templates (`CodeGenMetaModel.st`) from the project's programming-language config — they do NOT travel with the DM-JSON model. The real-world case is a standard restricted set such as **DIN 91379** (the EU public-administration / e-government character set for names & addresses) — far NARROWER than the BMP: it would make `中文`/many symbols illegal. The probe confirms a DM-JSON model gets the **full-BMP default, NOT DIN** (`é`/`ü`/`€`/`™`/`中文` all legal), so a project-configured DIN charset is genuinely a separate input. A model loaded purely from DM-JSON (the interpreter's path **and** the dynamic-codegen `RuntimeLaws` differential) gets the BMP default, and the interpreter's BMP check matches the kernel for every such model. **That trigger has FIRED:** the interpreter carries the **`LegalCharset` SPI** (IF43 — default BMP, matching the DM-JSON-only kernel; a consumer-supplied set such as DIN 91379 is enforced instead), the `legalCodepoints` half of the customization-surface parity rule. *(Interpreter: a single cross-kind `baselineValueError` in `eval/FormalError.kt`, IF43; locked `adapter.laws.BaselineValueFormalDiffTest`.)*

## §3 supplement — DECLARED field-type CONSTRAINTS are formal errors too (range / length / pattern / digit-count / enum-domain), source + probe-verified 2026-06-25

> Fold: folded → §3.

The format checks above (decimals, invalid chars, blanks, signed-zero) test whether a value is *well-formed for its kind*. But the kernel's `validateFull` **also** auto-validates the value against the field type's **declared constraints** — and these ride the **same `formalePruefung` mechanism**: an out-of-constraint value is a formal error, the field becomes **"unknown"**, and a referencing author rule is **suppressed** (branch-scoped, §3 / IF17). No rule is involved — the kernel emits these purely from the field-type declaration.

The constraints are declared on the field type (kernel `IStringType` / `INumberType`, source-read) and the formal-error codes are the kernel's full runtime set (`FormalErrorCodesProvider.getFormalErrorCodes`, ~55 codes). The constraint → code map (the ones a DM-JSON model can declare and a document can violate):

| field type | DM-JSON declaration | runtime formal-error code |
|---|---|---|
| **NUMBER** | `minValue` / `maxValue` | `zahlZuKlein` / `zahlZuGross` |
| | `minFractionalDigits` (too few / none) | `zahlFalscheAnzahlNachkommastellen` / `zahlOhneDezimalTrenner` |
| | `zeroNotAllowed: true` *(inverted key — NOT `zeroAllowed`)* | `zahlIstNull` |
| | **leading zeros** — forbidden BY DEFAULT; `leadingZerosAllowed: true` opts out | `zahlHatFuehrendeNullen` |
| | **> 15 TOTAL digits** (int + frac, sign/separator excluded) — a GENERIC parse check on EVERY number, not a declaration | `stelligkeitZuLang` |
| | `maxIntegerDigits`, OR `trait:"amount"`'s implicit `15 − scale` cap (only without explicit `maxIntegerDigits`) — the kernel's one `maxDigitsBeforeDecimalPlace` | `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen` |
| | `minLength` / `maxLength` (value-string length) | `zahlZuKurz` / `zahlZuLang` |
| **STRING** | `pattern` (regex) | `stringFalschesMuster` |
| | `noValueValidation: true` — disables the WHOLE string check (pattern/length/newline) | *(skips all — only `maxLength` may accompany it, and it is not enforced)* |
| | `minLength` / `maxLength` | `stringZuKurz` / `stringZuLang` |
| | **newline** — forbidden BY DEFAULT; `lineBreaksPermitted: true` opts out | `stringMitNewline` |
| **ENUMERATION** | the value domain (out-of-domain stored value) | `stringFalschesMuster` |

**Two are kernel DEFAULTS, not declarations** (follow-up probe 2026-06-26): a plain number rejects a leading zero (`"007"` → `zahlHatFuehrendeNullen`; `"0"`/`"0.50"` clean) and a plain string rejects a newline (`"a\nb"` → `stringMitNewline`) unless the field opts in.

**More NumberType integer-digit / length constraints DO serialize (corrected 2026-06-26; the `stelligkeitZuLang`/amount attribution CORRECTED again 2026-07-07 — see the digit-check supplement below):** `maxIntegerDigits` → `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen` (integer-digit count above the cap; sign + decimals excluded); `trait:"amount"` → an implicit integer-digit cap of `15 − scale` **firing the SAME `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen`** (only without explicit `maxIntegerDigits`); NumberType `minLength`/`maxLength` → `zahlZuKurz`/`zahlZuLang` on the value-string length (sign + decimal point counted). All modeled by the interpreter (IF30/IF67). **Still out of scope (probe-confirmed):** a **charset** check (`zeichenNichtImZeichensatz`) is not declarable and does not fire by default (tab / non-ASCII / control chars on a plain string are all clean); a field-level `uniqueValue` is not DM-JSON-deserializable.

**Probe-verified (2026-06-25/26)**: `/Order/BirthDay` (`minValue:1, maxValue:31`) — `"99"` → `zahlZuGross`, `"0"` → `zahlZuKlein`; `/Order/Priority` (enum) — `"BOGUS"` → `stringFalschesMuster`; precedence — string pattern/newline fire **before** length; the **suppression** was confirmed (a rule `[BirthDay] == 99` with `BirthDay = "99"` does **not** fire — only `zahlZuGross`, the field is unknown). So an out-of-constraint value is observable two ways: the **auto-emitted formal error** *and* the **suppression of referencing rules**.

This is the same class as §94/§98 (format-error suppression) — only the trigger differs (a declared constraint / kernel default vs raw well-formedness). The **interpreter now models this dimension** (the per-cell family + the two defaults) — INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF20, differential-locked by `adapter.laws.FieldConstraintFormalDiffTest`.

## §3 supplement — CRLF is normalized to LF at EVAL INGESTION — the format measure, rule reads, and compute reads all see `\n` (source + tri-differential, both strategies, 2026-07-15; feedback F12 → IG70 → IF124)

> Fold: folded → §3.

One normalization, `RuntimeUtils.normalizeLinebreaks` = replace the `"\r\n"` PAIR with `"\n"` (a **lone CR stays**), applied at two source loci with one observable effect — *every evaluation-side read of a valid string value sees the normalized text*:

- **Inside the String format check** (`FormatDefinitionString.validiereFormat`), the clause order is: `noValueValidation` → full-check gate → **line-break permission on the RAW text** (`stringMitNewline`; the gate applies to ENUM fields too — the enum format-definition constructor hard-codes `lineBreakAllowed = false` — so a break-carrying out-of-domain value is the NEWLINE, never `stringFalschesMuster`) → **normalize** → pattern XOR enum-domain (on the normalized text) → min/max length (on the normalized text). So a permitted `"AB\r\nCD"` measures **5** units, not the raw 6: `maxLength 5` is clean, `minLength 6` fires `stringZuKurz`, and a `AB\sCD` pattern (exactly one whitespace char) matches. (A pattern whose own text contains a literal line break is model-REJECTED — "Only values without line breaks are allowed" — so break-y patterns discriminate only via character classes.)
- **At the formal-check stage of every run** (`CheckCommand` → `FormalChecker.checkAndConvertFieldValue`), a checked-and-valid value enters the eval caches as its **`normalizedValue()`** — which **refuted F12's "measure-only" hypothesis**: a RULE read is normalized too (`Length([F]) > 5` over the permitted CRLF does NOT fire while `> 4` DOES — a real normalized 5-read, not a suppression), and a COMPUTATION read is normalized — a bare copy of the CRLF source **stores** `"AB\nCD"` (the stored computed value witnesses the read form). Both strategies agree on every arm. (A **`noValueValidation`** field is OUT of this class — its rule read never surfaces the stored text at all: value operations are model-rejected `MVK_INVALID_RAW_TYPE`, `Length` is authorable only as `Length(f) > <constant>` (`MVK_INVALID_LENGTH_OF_RAW_TYPE`) and is unconditionally non-evaluable at runtime (silent even at `> -1`, filled or empty; presence checks work normally) — the raw-type ELIMINATION mechanism, the §3 raw-type supplement below / IF125.)

*(Interpreter: the twin locus is the `Document` eval-INDEX ingestion — value-bearing placements are CRLF-normalized once at index build, so the format measure (`stringConstraintError`, whose newline gate now also precedes the enum-domain arm), rule operands, and compute operands read `\n`, while the consumer's raw placements stay untouched (`copy()` reproduces them verbatim; the compute delta projection keeps comparing against the RAW seed, matching the kernel's change-vs-input-document equality). Rule-message `$field.value$` is the deliberate sibling channel: it reads the immutable input's raw display bytes and therefore retains `\r\n`. Locked `adapter.laws.CrlfLengthNormalizationDiffTest` — the evaluation matrix — plus `MessageValueDisplayPolicyDiffTest` and multiplatform `CrlfNormalizationTest`/`MessageDisplayPolicyTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF124/IF201.)*

## §3 supplement — raw-type (`noValueValidation`) fields: EVERY value window is model-closed; the one allowed `Length` shape is an ELIMINATED max-length declaration (source + tri-differential, both strategies, 2026-07-15; IG71 → IF125)

> Fold: folded → §3.

A `noValueValidation` STRING field (nvv is an `IStringType`-only property — `FieldUtils.isNoValueValidation` returns false for every other kind, enums included) is a **raw-type**: its value exists in the document but is **never interned for evaluation** — the formal-check stage stores `ValidationCache.PSEUDO_VALUE` for it (`CheckCommand`: *"considered valid, but the value should not be interned"*) instead of the IF124 `normalizedValue()`. The kernel then closes **every value window at the MODEL tier**, so no legal rule or computation ever reads the value:

- a **value operation** anywhere — a comparison `[nvv] == "…"`, a value list, a computation operand (bare copy, concatenation), `FieldValueAsNumber` — is rejected **`MVK_INVALID_RAW_TYPE`** (*"It is not allowed to use a field which defines 'no value validation'"*; the parser's `checkIsValueValidation`, called from every value-position check impl — field-value ops, value lists, semantic index, `Valid(f, type)`, `FieldValuesNotUnique`);
- **`Length`** is authorable **only as the WHOLE rule condition** `Length(f) > <constant>` / mirrored `<constant> < Length(f)` — strict GT/LT only (`AnalyseService.getNameAndLengthOfAllowedValueValidation`); any other shape — `>=`, a surrounding `And`, a computation operation, even the allowed comparison as a computation **precondition** — is **`MVK_INVALID_LENGTH_OF_RAW_TYPE`** (the shape gate is `ParserService.checkNormalRule`, rules only; computation snippets reject `Length(nvv)` outright);
- **`$field.value$` message interpolation** of an nvv field is rejected **`INVALID_NO_VALUE_VALIDATION`** (*"the reference to its value is not allowed"*).

**The one allowed shape is not a rule — it is a disguised MAX-LENGTH declaration.** `ValidationCodeGenerator.handleLengthOfAllowedValueValidationRule` **ELIMINATES the rule** from the generated validation (both strategies — the shared generator, not a template) and lifts its `(field, c)` into the generated meta-model's max length (`addMaxLengthDefinedByRules` → `CodeGenStringTypeImpl.maxLaenge`/`maxLaengePrintable` — a forms/host metadata concern; the runtime `validiereFormat` still short-circuits on nvv, IF29, so nothing fires at runtime either way). Differentially pinned in BOTH strategies: `Length(nvv) > 5` / `> -1` / mirrored `-1 < Length(nvv)` are **silent** on filled and empty values alike, in full AND partial validation — while **presence semantics stay ordinary** (the pseudo-interned cell is content: `FieldFilled` fires, quantifiers and `NumberOfFilledFields` counts over nvv cells behave normally). The facade exposes the same extraction as `RuleCollectionMetaInformation.getNoValidationLength` (tool/editor metadata).

*(Interpreter: the mechanism-exact twin is RULE ELIMINATION, not a per-read np value state — `DmInterpreter.rowOutcomes` returns no rows for the eliminated shape (`eliminatedNvvMetaLengthRule`: whole-condition GT/LT-mirrored `Length` of a `noValueValidation`-constrained field vs a numeric literal), so it never fires and `testRule` reports PASSED; presence/content paths untouched. On kernel-legal inputs every other value read is unreachable (the MVK gates above), so no `Val` change is needed. Locked `adapter.laws.NvvRawTypeDiffTest` (tri-check: the three eliminated arms, presence, quantifiers, the count aggregate, partial validation) + `validate.laws.NvvRawTypeLawsTest` (the accept/reject tier incl. the `>=` precision arm and the computation rejects) + multiplatform `interpreter … NvvEliminatedLengthRuleTest` + the `ModelFuzz.nvvModel` fuzz family (mutation-checked: the gate disabled diverges on 26/200 (model, doc) pairs); INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF125.)*

## §3 supplement — the two DISTINCT number-digit checks: generic `stelligkeitZuLang` (> 15 total) vs the integer-cap `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen` (source + probe-verified 2026-07-07)

> Fold: folded → §3.

Corrects the earlier attribution (which wrongly gave `trait:"amount"` its own `stelligkeitZuLang` code). There are **two** independent digit checks, with different triggers, codes, and precedence:

| check | trigger | code | when it runs |
|---|---|---|---|
| **total-digit sanity** | > 15 **total** digits (integer + fractional, sign + decimal separator stripped) — on **EVERY** number, no declaration needed | `stelligkeitZuLang` | at **parse** (`NumberConversion.sanityCheck` / `FormatDefinitionZahl.validiereFormat`), **third** — right after the plus-sign + invalid-character checks, **before** sign / scale / leading-zero / integer-cap |
| **integer-digit cap** | integer-part digits > the cap, where the cap = explicit `maxIntegerDigits`, else `trait:"amount"`'s `15 − maxFractionalDigits` (the kernel's `TypeConverter` maps both to the one `maxDigitsBeforeDecimalPlace`) | `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen` | later, in the full format check (only reached when total digits ≤ 15) |

**Precedence, probe-verified:** the total-digit check pre-empts leading-zeros, the scale-0 decimal-separator check, and signed-zero (e.g. `01234567890123456` → `stelligkeitZuLang`, not `zahlHatFuehrendeNullen`). The **decisive** case that separates the two: amount `scale=5`, value `12345678901` (11 integer, 0 fractional, 11 total) → **`zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen`** (11 > `15−5`), because total 11 ≤ 15 so the generic check does not fire. Interpreter: INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF67, differential-locked by `adapter.laws.FieldConstraintFormalDiffTest` (+ multiplatform `interpreter.NumberDigitFormalTest`).

**STRING `noValueValidation` DISABLES the whole string format check** (source + probe-verified 2026-06-26). When `StringType.noValueValidation:true`, the kernel's `FormatDefinitionString.validiereFormat` returns `Valid` *immediately* — pattern, length, and the default newline check are all skipped. The key is MVK-constrained: it **forbids** `pattern`/`minLength` (`MVK_STRING_TYPE_PARAMS_INCONSISTENT` — only `maxLength` may accompany it) and **requires** `lineBreaksPermitted:true` (`MVK_STRING_TYPE_NO_VAL_AND_NO_LINE_BREAK`). So on a *kernel-valid* model the single observable runtime effect is that a declared **`maxLength` is NOT enforced** (a longer value is clean). Reachable on shipped A12 Tools StringType fields (Country/City/Person). The interpreter models it — INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF29.

## §3 supplement — the full NUMBER formal-error PRECEDENCE order (source `FormatDefinitionZahl.validiereFormat`, 2026-07-07)

> Fold: folded → §3.

The number format checks fire in ONE fixed sequence (the kernel returns on the first failure), so a value matching several reports the FIRST in this order. Extends the digit-check note above with the whole ladder:

| # | check | code | trigger |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | leading plus | `zahlMitPlus` | a `+` sign |
| 2 | invalid chars | `zahlHatUngueltigeZeichen` | not a number |
| 3 | > 15 total digits | `stelligkeitZuLang` | int+frac digits > 15 (fires even pre-`fullCheck`) |
| 4 | signed zero | `zahlNullEnthaeltVorzeichen` | `-0` on a signed, zero-allowed field |
| 5 | minus on unsigned | `zahlEnthaeltVorzeichen` | a `-` on a `positivesOnly` field |
| 6 | separator in integer field | `zahlHatDezimalTrenner` | a `.` where scale = 0 |
| 7 | missing separator | `zahlOhneDezimalTrenner` | `minFractionalDigits` > 0 but no `.` |
| 8 | wrong # decimals | `zahlFalscheAnzahlNachkommastellen` | too few OR too many fractional digits |
| 9 | integer-digit cap | `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen` | integer digits > `maxIntegerDigits` / amount `15−scale` |
| 10 | zero not allowed | `zahlIstNull` | value == 0, `zeroNotAllowed` |
| 11 | leading zeros | `zahlHatFuehrendeNullen` | `07`/`00`/… — the **LAST** format check |
| 12 | value too short | `zahlZuKurz` | string length < `minLength` |
| 13 | value too long | `zahlZuLang` | string length > `maxLength` |
| 14 | below min | `zahlZuKlein` | value < `minValue` |
| 15 | above max | `zahlZuGross` | value > `maxValue` |

Two traps this order encodes (probe-verified, IF70): **zero-not-allowed (10) fires AFTER the decimal (7-8) and integer-cap (9) checks** — so a `0` on a `zeroNotAllowed`+`minFractionalDigits` field is `zahlOhneDezimalTrenner`, not `zahlIstNull`; and **leading-zeros (11) is the LAST format check** — every earlier check beats it (`07.5` on a scale-0 field is `zahlHatDezimalTrenner`, not `zahlHatFuehrendeNullen`; `0007` over an integer cap is `zahlZuvieleVorkommastellen`). Interpreter: INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF70 (reordered `numberConstraintError` to this sequence, moved `leadingZeros` out of the parse-level `numberFormalError`), differential-locked by `adapter.laws.FieldConstraintFormalDiffTest` (the precedence matrix, each case pinning the kernel winner) + multiplatform `interpreter.NumberDigitFormalTest`.

## §3 supplement — a DATE value is stored and formal-checked in the field's DECLARED format, not ISO (runtime, 2026-06-23)

> Fold: folded → §6.

Also surfaced by the **fuzz net**. A `DateType` field's value is the date **rendered in that field's `format`** — `OrderDate` (`yyyy-MM-dd`) holds `2024-06-15`, but `ApproxDate` (`dd.MM.yyyy`) holds `15.06.2024`. The formal check is **against the declared format**: a value in the field's own format is well-formed; a value in a *different* format is `datumFormatFalsch` (verified both ways — `ApproxDate = "2024-06-15"` → `datumFormatFalsch`; `OrderDate = "15.06.2024"` → `datumFormatFalsch`). So there is **no single canonical wire format** — a consumer must read each field's `format` to parse/validate its dates (the same is already known for `DateRangeType`, IF12). The interpreter canonicalizes every date to ISO internally via the declared format. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.PlainDateDiffTest`.)*

## §3 supplement — a DATE value has an always-on lower bound: `Constants.DATE_FROM = 1583-10-16` (source + differential, 2026-07-09)

> Fold: folded → §6.

A well-formed date VALUE that resolves to a **real calendar date before 1583-10-16** ("the begin of the gregorian calendar") is the formal error **`datumFalsch`** (`FEHLER_DATUM`) — for **every** DATE field, stored or computed, with **no** opt-in required. Source: `FormatDefinitionDatum.validiereFormat` calls `dateInvalid(date) = date.before(getDateFrom(Constants.DATE_FROM))` unconditionally (after the format/calendar parse), where `Constants.DATE_FROM = "16.10.1583"`. A **DATE_RANGE** floors its **start** the same way (`FormatDefinitionDatumBereich extends FormatDefinitionDatum`, `datumBereichDatumFalsch` / `FEHLER_DATUM_BEREICH_DATUM`; end ≥ start covers the end).

This is **distinct from the opt-in "younger than 1900" additional check** (`Constants.DATE_FROM_FOR_ADDITIONAL_CHECK = "01.01.1900"`, gated on the field's `Zusatzpruefung` meta = `IDateType.isYoungerThan1900Check()`, **default false**): the always-on floor is 1583-10-16, so **1899 is valid** while 1000 / 1583-10-15 are not. Boundary pinned exactly by the differential — 1583-10-15 errors, 1583-10-16 is valid (strict `Date.before`). A **DAY_OPTIONAL** date completes an omitted day to `01` **before** the floor check (`PartiallyKnownDatesUtil.replaceUnknownDateParts` runs ahead of `dateInvalid`), so `00.10.1583` (→ 1583-10-**01**) errors while `00.11.1583` (→ 1583-11-01) is valid — kernel-differential-pinned. A computed date below the floor is bucketed **with-errors** (`getComputedFieldInstancesWithErrors`) → reported ERRORED; a stored one is a `validateFull` formal error that **suppresses** a referencing rule. **Both kernel strategies apply it** (the runtime format-definition + the codegen `CodeGenDateTypeImpl` that wraps it — java-static tri-checked). Interpreter treatment + lock: [INTERPRETER-FINDINGS](INTERPRETER-FINDINGS.md) IF88. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.ComputedDateGregorianFloorDiffTest`, both strategies.)*

## §6 supplement — the RUNTIME tolerates any date `format`, but AUTHORING (SME) accepts only 3 canonical ones (source-verified 2026-07-07)

> Fold: folded → §6.

(The runtime-lenient half is canonical in §6 — a value parses by its field's declared format; the SME-openability split below is the authoring/tooling evidence.) Two consumers read a date-ish field's `format`, and they disagree — a **runtime-lenient / authoring-strict** split that the conformance corpus tripped over when its models had to become SME-openable.

- **Kernel RUNTIME** (`IDocumentRtService`, the JVM differential's oracle): `format` is *the pattern to parse stored values with*. Any pattern works — `dd.MM.yyyy` is a first-class German format (`STANDARD_DE_DATE_FORMAT`/`DATUM_FORMAT` in the runtime), so a field declaring `format: "dd.MM.yyyy"` with values like `15.06.2024` validates and evaluates normally. This is why our `order` fixture's `ApproxDate`/`ItemCoverage`/`CoverageWindow` (German format, `-` range separator) have always passed the differential (locked by the date DiffTests — legitimate coverage of the runtime's format flexibility; see the §3-supplement finding above).
- **AUTHORING / SME** (the `simple-model-editor` model deserializer): `format` is *an enum of the canonical A12 storage formats*. Its field-type mapping is a hard `switch` that accepts **only** `yyyy-MM-dd`, `yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm:ss`, `HH:mm:ss` (for Date / DateTime+Time / DateRange alike) and otherwise **`throw new Error("unsupported date format: …")`** — so a model with `dd.MM.yyyy` in `format` *evaluates fine but cannot be opened in the SME*. A localized **display** format is not stored in `format` at all; it belongs in the sibling `notInDCustomFormat` (and a non-`/` range separator in `notInDCustomRangeSeparator`) — the A12 default range separator is `/` (kernel `FieldTypeConstants.A12_DATE_RANGE_SEPARATOR`). The mechanism is verified from the SME source and re-expressed here (license-safe — behavior, not code).

**Consequence for us.** Openability is a stricter bar than runtime-validity: `dmtool model describe` / `rule check` use the lenient runtime read path, so they are *not* a faithful SME proxy for this class (they accept `dd.MM.yyyy`). A model meant to open in the modeling tools must keep `format` in the canonical set. The conformance corpus therefore **canonicalizes date `format`s when it writes a standalone model** (`CaseJson.writeModel` → `CANONICAL_DATE_FORMAT`) and a guard rejects any non-canonical one (`CorpusSchemaGuardTest` via `CaseJson.unsupportedDateFormats`); the shared *differential fixture* keeps its `dd.MM.yyyy` fields (they test the runtime and are never SME-opened). *(Locked: `adapter.laws.CorpusSchemaGuardTest`.)*

## §4 supplement — the "required" property and its two modes (runtime, 2026-06-06)

> Fold: folded → §4.

(The generated-rule shape, the content notion behind "parent filled", the index auto-requirement, and the branch-scoped unknown are canonical in §4 "The generated rule, precisely"; the DM-JSON mode encoding and probe detail stay here as evidence.) The field-editor "required" checkbox is encoded in DM-JSON as `Field.requirednessConfig: {mode: …}` (kernel enum `IRequirednessConfig.Mode`). An unfilled required field raises the `mandatoryField` **formal error**, so the field is **"unknown"** (§3). The two modes — and the gotcha the BA docs flag — verified at runtime:

| mode (DM-JSON) | BA "Only if parent filled" | when required |
|---|---|---|
| `absoluteOrRelativeToNextRepAncestor` | **no** | a non-repeatable field is **always** required; a field under a repeatable ancestor is required once that ancestor is filled |
| `relativeToParent` | **yes** | required **only when the parent group is filled** |

**The gotcha — what "filled" means here.** For `relativeToParent`, the gate is the same §9 `GroupFilled` content law. On the verified **non-repeatable** `/Order/ShippingAddress` parent, a merely-created empty structural instance is not content, while an admitted sibling (`City`) activates the empty Street requirement. A repeatable parent differs: its instantiated row is content even when its fields are empty. Under `absoluteOrRelativeToNextRepAncestor` the non-repeatable Street example is always required. Requiredness is **stronger than `FieldNotFilled`**: it makes the field unknown, so a required-but-empty number is not the §2 empty-as-0. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.RequiredPropertyLawsTest` + `adapter.laws.RequirednessDiffTest`.)*

**Mechanism + four more facts (source-read `AutogeneratedRulesService`/`MandatoryFieldRuleImpl` + differential, 2026-06-23).** The kernel implements `mandatoryField` as an **autogenerated rule** named `vk_vk<field>_req`, error code `mandatoryField`, severity ERROR, **type OMISSION** (filling the field clears it), with the condition decided by `relativeTo = lowestRepeatableGroupPath(field)`:

1. **`absoluteOrRelativeToNextRepAncestor`, no repeatable ancestor** → `FieldNotFilled(field)` — **unconditional**: fires even on an *un-instantiated* document (an empty doc still raises `mandatoryField` at the canonical all-1s pointer; a not-instantiated non-repeatable sub-group still requires its absolute fields).
2. **…, with a repeatable ancestor R** → `GroupFilled(R) And FieldNotFilled(field)` — **per instantiated R row** (no row ⇒ no `mandatoryField`).
3. **`relativeToParent`** → `GroupFilled(parent) And FieldNotFilled(field)`.
4. **Index fields are auto-required** — a group's `indexFieldName` gets an auto-generated mandatory rule with `relativeTo = parent` (i.e. `relativeToParent` semantics), on top of any explicit requiredness. So an instantiated repeatable row with an empty index field fires `mandatoryField` on it.

The **gotcha resolves by the parent's repeatability** (the `GroupFilled` split, §9 below): for a **repeatable** parent a *created-but-empty* row already counts as filled (the row index is content) → the field IS required; for a **non-repeatable** parent a formally admitted filled descendant counts. **What "a filled descendant" means was refined 2026-07-06 and again 2026-07-21:** it includes an INSTANTIATED repeatable descendant ROW, not only a valued field, but it excludes a value rejected by formal conversion. Thus an invalid-only non-repeatable parent does not activate a `relativeToParent` sibling; an admitted sibling or instantiated repeatable descendant does. So this is the SAME admitted-content notion as the §9 `GroupFilled` operator — requiredness and `GroupFilled` share it (they diverge only in that requiredness's *absolute* mode ignores it). And the "unknown" is **branch-scoped, not whole-rule** (§3 refinement): a required-empty operand makes its *comparison* unknown, so `[ReqNum] < 100` is suppressed but `[ReqNum] < 100 Or <true>` still fires. *(Interpreter parity locked: `adapter.laws.RequirednessDiffTest` + `adapter.laws.GroupPresenceFormalInvalidDiffTest`; branch-scoped field unknown by `adapter.laws.SuppressionDiffTest`.)*

## §4/§8 supplement — field-level custom error messages live in a per-locale `errorMessage` array (source-verified, 2026-06-21)

> Fold: not-foldable — a DM-JSON model-structure/serialization fact (where the custom texts live), outside the evaluation scope.

A field carries **two** kinds of custom validation error text, both the "Use Default Error Messages" deselected case (SME 840, 1123) — and both are stored the same way: a per-locale `errorMessage` array `[{locale, text}]`.

| message | DM-JSON location | kernel accessor |
|---|---|---|
| **required-field** (shown when a required field is empty) | `Field.requirednessConfig.errorMessage` | `IRequirednessConfig.getErrorMessage(Locale)` |
| **enum invalid-value** (shown for an out-of-domain value) | `EnumerationType.errorMessage` | (validated by `FieldErrorTextParser`) |

- **"Use default" = absence.** There is **no** explicit `useDefaultErrorMessage` boolean — omitting the `errorMessage` array uses the kernel default; providing it is the custom case. (So the editor checkbox maps to presence/absence, not a stored flag.)
- **Must cover every declared locale** (like enum value labels): a partial map is kernel-rejected. The required-message array also needs the field to *be* required (have a `requirednessConfig`).

Source-read from the kernel (`IField.IRequirednessConfig.getErrorMessage`, `FieldErrorTextParser.hasValidErrorTextInRequirednessConfig`); rulekit's treatment (the `requiredMessages` / `enum.errorMessages` field-spec keys, G23) is in KERNEL-INTEGRATION-NOTES. *(Locked: `cli.field.FieldAddSpecTest` — per-locale enum + required messages round-trip; `modelkit.FieldConfigExtrasTest` reads the enum message back.)*

## §5 supplement — the numeric equality scale gate is asymmetric

> Fold: folded → §5.

The `==`/`!=` decimal-scale gate carries two independent facts for every checked numeric expression: a known signed scale or unknown, and a multiplicative-constant capability. Fractional literals retain authored scale, while integer literals strip trailing zeros (`1000` has scale `−3`); literals are capable. Fields carry declared scale and are not capable. Addition/subtraction take maximum scale and capability AND, multiplication adds scales and takes capability OR, division is unknown/non-capable, and power is unknown/non-capable except that a known scale-0 base raised to a syntactically simple nonnegative constant exponent of known scale ≤ 0 derives scale 0. Explicit rounding supplies its declared scale.

Differing known scales are accepted only when the numerically smaller-scale side is capable; unknown rejects. This explains the earlier misleading sample: scale-2 field `== 0` happens to be legal because the scale-0 literal is the capable smaller side, but scale-0 field `== 0.00` is illegal in either operand order because the smaller side is the non-capable field. The rule is not “literals are exempt.” Ordering (`< <= > >=`) bypasses the scale gate, and `@SuppressWarning(MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES)` remains the explicit equality/assignment escape hatch. The typed builder carries this shared metadata across fields, literals, arithmetic, counts, and rounding; generic/loaded shapes whose model-derived scale is unavailable still defer to the live kernel instead of inventing a result. *(Locked: `validate.laws.NumberLawsTest` + `dsl.operators.NumericScaleGateTest`.)*

**Field/expression comparison (finding 18, corrected 2026-07-20).** Strings compare with `==`/`!=` only (ordering → `MVK_INVALID_TYPE_FOR_COMPARISON`, "only for numbers, amounts and dates"); string-vs-number → `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_ENUM_OR_STRING`. Enum-vs-enum is modeled by `EnumRef.is/isNot(EnumRef)` and compared by stored value, with the kernel retaining model compatibility arbitration. Number equality/inequality follows the derived summary above; ordinary ordering stays scale-free. A scale-2 × scale-2 product derives scale 4, a bare division derives unknown, and an explicit `RoundAccounting(expr, n)` derives `n`, so rounding or suppression remains the corrective when equality or a computed assignment cannot establish a compatible scale.

**Tolerance comparisons (implemented 2026-06-07).** The kernel's `ComparisonOperation` has four tolerance members — `NE1`/`NE2`/`NE5`/`NE10` — rendered as the worded keywords `DiffersWithToleranceRange1`/`2`/`5`/`10` (DE `UngleichMitToleranz{N}`): `[a] DiffersWithToleranceRange5 [b]` is true iff the two numbers differ by more than 5 ("unequal within a tolerance"). Only for numbers/amounts. Confirmed against the kernel source: `FormatterUtil.getComparisonOperator` renders them via the same comparison-operator path as `<`/`==` (so they ride the existing `ComparisonCondition` rendering). Modeled by extending `CompareOp` with the four variants + a public `Tolerance` enum (`RANGE_1/2/5/10`, making any other range unrepresentable); authored via `NumberExpr.differsWithTolerance(Tolerance, …)` / `NumberRef.differsWithTolerance(...)`. *(Locked: `adapter.ArithmeticReadTest.roundTripsToleranceComparison` + BA-corpus `ToleranceCmp`; N7 catalog examples for all four.)* **Read also converts a tolerance op in an EXPRESSION / AGGREGATE comparison** (not just field-vs-field) — the `numberExprComparison`/`exprVsExpr` paths route `NE1`/`NE2`/`NE5`/`NE10` through `differsWithTolerance` (`adapter.AggregateToleranceReadTest`, e.g. `Sum(Items*/Count) DiffersWithToleranceRange1 [Quantity]`).

**Runtime semantics (corrected from the 2026-06-24 probe by the source-backed SPEC-2026-07-19-13 discriminator).** The kernel independently normalizes both numeric operands to scale 19 `HALF_UP`, then fires iff `|R₁₉(a) − R₁₉(b)| > N`; the band is **STRICT**. This ordering matters: with `ε = 49 / 10²¹`, `a = 1 + ε`, `b = −ε`, and range 1, both operands normalize to `1` and `0`, so the rule does not fire; subtracting first and rounding the completed difference would wrongly fire. An **empty** numeric operand reads as **0** and a malformed operand suppresses the rule. Polarity is the same directional rule as numeric `!=`, not equality-like: for a firing gap, only movement of the normalized smaller side upward or the larger side downward can close the band. Thus an empty unsigned `0` against `+2` is OMISSION, but against `−2` is VALUE. The dedicated `Cond.Tolerance` node remains necessary because both its strict band truth and its directional typing differ from a generic `Abs(a-b) > N` composition. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.ToleranceExactnessDiffTest` against Groovy-dynamic + generated Java, multiplatform `ToleranceOpsTest`, the boundary law, and the broader `ToleranceDiffTest`.)*

## §5 supplement — a NUMBER field type is ALWAYS scale-bounded; `maxFractionalDigits` is mandatory (no unbounded decimal) (source + metamodel + probe, 2026-06-28)

> Fold: folded → §5.

An a12 `NumberType` carries a **mandatory** decimal scale — there is **no unbounded-scale decimal**. Three independent oracles agree:

- **Typed API** — `INumberType.getMaxFractionalDigits()` returns a **primitive `int`** (no `Optional`), unlike the genuinely-optional slots on the same interface (`getMinValue`/`getMaxValue`/`getMaxLength`/`getMaxNumberOfDigitsBeforeDecimalPlace` are all `Optional<>`). There is no "unset scale" in the model.
- **Metamodel** — `MM_SMM_FieldTypeChecks` states it in prose (";; maxFractionalDigits is a required Field in case of a number") and flags `NotAllFieldsFilled(.../maxFractionalDigits)` as an integration error for any Number-supertype field.
- **Behaviour (probe)** — a DM-JSON that **omits** the slot is **accepted**, with the scale **silently defaulting to 0** (Jackson fills the primitive `int` → 0) — never unbounded, never rejected.

Consequences: modelkit emitting `maxFractionalDigits` with a `null → 0` default is **correct** (it makes the implicit kernel default explicit), and a bare JSON-Schema `number` (no scale signal) has **no faithful import** — schemakit omits it by default rather than silently coercing a decimal to an integer (SCHEMAKIT-SPEC §4b). *(Locked: `modelkit.NumberScaleKernelLawTest`. This resolves the DEV-API-GAPS "unbounded-scale NUMBER" investigation — a kernel fact, not a modelkit gap.)*

## §5 supplement — power-operator parse laws + the quiet non-execution edges (source-read + 2026-06-17)

> Fold: folded → §5.

The `^` power operator (parser `CheckOperationImpl`) has two separate structural checks. `checkOnlyOnePow` rejects only a power whose **direct left operand** is an ungrouped power; the left-associative grammar makes `a ^ b ^ c` the reachable unbraced rejection, while `{a ^ b} ^ c` and `a ^ {b ^ c}` start fresh regions. `checkNoFractionalDigitsInExponent` requires a known exponent scale ≤ 0 — a scale-0 field or integer literal is accepted, while a decimal-scale field, fractional literal, or unknown-scale expression draws `MVK_INVALID_NUMBER_FOR_EXP`. Arithmetic sub-expressions group with **curly braces `{ }`**, and precedence is `^` → `*`/`/` → `+`/`-`. The neighboring division check counts open divisions only through the plain multiplication/division region: grouping/addition/subtraction/power reset the count, while multiplication/division combine child contributions and division adds one. Operation-valued wrappers remain unclosed evidence because the legacy walk's reset control flow does not compose by that plain rule. *(Locked: `validate.laws.NumberLawsTest` + `dsl.operators.ArithmeticGroupingRenderTest`.)*

**Power runtime value mechanism (source + dual-route differential, corrected 2026-07-20).** Positive power follows OpenJDK 21's X3.274 `BigDecimal.pow(int, MathContext)` numeric-value algorithm, keeping binary-exponentiation intermediates at precision `50 + decimalDigits(exponent) + 1` before the final precision-50 `HALF_UP` round; `0.473471768303411 ^ 7` ends in `…3319`, while exact rational power plus one final round ends in `…3318`. Negative power is kernel-specific reciprocal-first: round `1/base` at precision 50, then apply the positive algorithm to `−exponent`; `3 ^ -3` ends in `…7036`, not the reciprocal-after-power `…7037`. The admitted endpoints `±1000` execute, `±1001` and `0^-1` are runtime-invalid, and `0^0 = 1`; fractional exponents are authoring-rejected before either runtime strategy. On the **rule side** the two runtime-invalid integral regions make a comparison quiet (project to UNKNOWN). On the **computation side** they are the same `INVALID_NUMBER` domain failure as zero division (`CalculationController.handleBerechnetenWert`): a legal `RoundAccounting(0 ^ -1, 2)` / `RoundAccounting(2 ^ 1001, 2)` target has no stored value, carries `berechnungsWertFehler`, and poisons a dependent read — numeric `Abs`/`Min`/`Max`/rounding wrappers preserve it, exactly as for division (IF177 for division, IF203 for power). *(Locked: multiplatform `DecimalHostParityTest`; Groovy-dynamic/generated-Java/interpreter `DecimalPrecisionParityDiffTest` (rule side) and `PowerDomainFailureComputationDiffTest` (computation side).)*

## §7 supplement — pattern-comparison slot rules and the limited kernel regex gate (2026-06-04 experiments; portability corrected 2026-07-19)

> Fold: folded → §7.

`PatternMatched`/`PatternViolated` (DE: `WieMuster`/`NichtWieMuster`) are grammar-level *infix comparison operators* (`vglOp` alternatives like `==`), but their slot rules are far stricter than the grammar:

- **Left side: a bare string field value only.** A non-string field (number, enum, boolean) → **`MVK_INVALID_TYPE_FOR_PATTERN_COMPARISON`** ("only allowed for string values" — note: *enums are not pattern-comparable* even though they compare to strings with `==`). A string *expression* (e.g. concatenation) → **`MVK_INVALID_PARAMETER_FOR_PATTERN_COMPARISON`** ("only allowed with a field value as first parameter"). A constant → the general **`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_CONST`**.
- **Right side: a string constant only.** A field reference → **`MVK_MISSING_CONST_IN_PATTERN_COMP`**; a number constant → `MVK_INVALID_TYPE_FOR_PATTERN_COMPARISON`.
- **The regex gate is Java `Pattern.compile` plus a limited `PatternUtils.isPatternValid` check** (the extmp world uses `CommonUtils.isRegExValid` instead). Lookbehind `(?<=a)b` is Java-valid yet rejected with `MVK_INVALID_PATTERN`, so a Java compile pre-check is necessary but not sufficient. The earlier inference that the additional gate enforces a Java/JavaScript-portable subset was false: live kernel checks accept inline flags `(?i)` and Java escapes `\R`/`\h`, all rejected by JS `RegExp` under Kotlin/JS. Kernel-valid and JS-portable are therefore distinct contracts; IF164 reproduces the admitted Java-pattern semantics on JS.
- **The empty pattern `""` is accepted** for document models (the non-extmp branch; extmp additionally requires non-empty).

*(Locked for the slot rules/lookbehind discriminator: `validate.laws.PatternLawsTest` + N7 `PatternMatched`/`PatternViolated` examples. Live acceptance probes for `(?i)`, `\R`, and `\h`: 2026-07-19. IF154 was the selected-escape increment; IF164 completes the admitted cross-platform dialect.)*

## §7 supplement — string-literal escaping is quote-doubling, not backslashes

> Fold: not-foldable — concrete-syntax/grammar tier; the canonical doc defers grammar pitfalls to the origin project's grammar doc.

The condition lexer's rule is `StringLiteral : '"' ( '""' | ~["] )* '"'` — an embedded quote is **doubled** (`"say ""hi"""`), and **backslash is a plain character** (no escape sequences; regex classes like `\d` are written as-is). Backslash-escaping a quote (`\"`) is a parse error (`MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`). Found while building the pattern operators: rulekit's renderer had C-style escaping and would have emitted wrong regexes for any pattern containing `\` — fixed and locked. *(Locked: `validate.laws.PatternLawsTest` + `RenderConditionTest.quotesStringLiteralsTheKernelWay`.)*

## §8 supplement — a category's `description` is an editor-level note the kernel tolerates (probe + source, 2026-06-21)

> Fold: not-foldable — an editor/tooling model attribute the kernel ignores; no evaluation behavior.

An enumeration category's **optional `description`** (SME 1133 — "an optional description can be set") is **not** modeled by the kernel core: both `IEnumerationType.getCategoryValues()` and the a12internal `IEnumerationDataTable` expose categories as `name → values` only. A `description` key on a category node is **tolerated** by `checkConsistency` (probe: injected into a category, `model check` → `valid: true`, no diagnostic) — it's an editor/tool attribute the kernel ignores. Per the owner rule (*what's editable is part of the model, unless workspace-related*), it belongs **in the model** (the category node), not workspace settings — so rulekit writes it there (`EnumCategory.description`), preserved by the generic-tree round-trip and harmless to the kernel. *(Locked: `cli.field.FieldAddSpecTest.enumCategoryCarriesAnOptionalDescription`.)*

## §8 supplement — `FieldValueIncludedInValueList` rides the 3VL: an empty operand is UNKNOWN (runtime, 2026-06-23)

> Fold: folded → §2.

On an empty field the membership check is UNKNOWN, not "absent from the list" — NEITHER form fires (the `Not` form does not fire just because the field is empty); canonical in §2's override table (value-list membership row). *(Locked: `adapter.laws.ValueListDiffTest`.)*

The **authoring-side** constraints are already in the MVK ledger, not repeated here: members are type-matched to the field — number field → number literals, string/enum → string literals; mixing them is `MVK_INVALID_TYPES_FOR_COMPARISON` (ledger: *erased*), and a boolean/confirm field has no value-list (`MVK_ONLY_STRING_ENUM_NUMBER_ALLOWED`, ledger: *erased*). For `GroupFilled`/`GroupNotFilled`: a repeatable group reference needs the `*` aggregate form (`MVK_NO_WILDCARD`, ledger: *kernel-delegated*) and the error field must be referenced — a `GroupFilled(G)` reference covers `G`'s members *indirectly* (`MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_REFERENCED`, ledger: *pre-checked*).

## §8 supplement — enum-category codes and the positional mapping

> Fold: folded → §8.

In DM-JSON, a category's `values` list pairs **positionally** with the enumeration's `values` (entry *i* categorizes enum value *i*); the comparison domain is the category's distinct value set. Codes: comparing to one of the *enum's own* values instead of a *category* value → **`MVK_INVALID_STRING_CONSTANT_FOR_ENUM_COMPARISON`**; an unknown category name → **`MVK_CATEGORY_UNKOWN`** (the kernel's spelling); `->` on a non-enumeration field → **`MVK_NO_ENUMERATION`**. Curiosity: `[Enum -> Category] == [Enum]` (category vs. plain enum field) is *accepted*. *(Locked: N7 `EnumCategory` examples + `dsl.operators.EnumConditionTest`.)*

**Runtime (2026-06-10, RT-semantics drain):** the many-to-one mapping fires for *every* value sharing the category value (`STANDARD,EXPRESS,OVERNIGHT → NORMAL,FAST,FAST`: both `EXPRESS` and `OVERNIGHT` satisfy `== "FAST"`, `STANDARD` does not), and an empty enum rides the not-evaluated track — the category hop doesn't change that. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.EnumCategoryLawsTest`.)*

*(Interpreter, probe-reconfirmed 2026-06-24: `Expr.EnumCategory(path, category)`, parsed in the `[...]` atom via the `->` arrow (same symbol in every language). `EvalContext.enumCategory` maps the stored value to its category value by POSITION in the enum's value list (`FieldDef.enumValues`/`enumCategories`, loaded from `EnumerationType.{values,categories}`); the comparison is string-based. An **empty** enum → `Val.Unknown` (the empty-string compare rule → no fire); a value **not in the enum** → `Val.Unknown` (the kernel emits `stringFalschesMuster` and suppresses — the interpreter's out-of-enum→Unknown reproduces the same no-fire). Polarity is always VALUE when it fires (the enum is filled). Locked: `adapter.laws.EnumCategoryDiffTest` — EXPRESS/OVERNIGHT→FAST, STANDARD→NORMAL, empty/out-of-enum no-fire, the `!=` direction.)*

## §8 supplement — predefined-type validity (`ValidType`/`InvalidType`) is a runtime CLIENT SPI, not kernel-core behavior (probe + source, 2026-06-24)

> Fold: not-foldable — client-SPI registration/wiring mechanics (the operator form itself is canonical in §6/the field-types table).

`Valid(field, "EMail")` / `Invalid(field, "IBAN")` validate the field's value against a **predefined custom field type**. The type name is a free string-literal operand and is **NOT checked at `checkConsistency`** (the model accepts any string). The reason is structural: the predefined types are a **runtime client SPI** — `IPredefinedType` ("*intended to be implemented by clients … Predefined types are project specific … The runtime must include the code for the validation*"), with `ValidationStrategy ∈ {NONE, NOT_SUPPORTED, PROVIDED_VALIDATION_CODE, LENGTH_AND_PATTERN_CHECK}`. The codegen emits a call into client-provided runtime methods; the kernel CORE ships **no** "EMail"/"IBAN" validators.

**The differential needs a registered type — and CAN have one (probe-confirmed).** With NO registered `IPredefinedType` the bare kernel is a **degenerate no-op** — `Valid` AND `Invalid` **both fire on ANY filled value** (`alice@example.com` and `not-an-email` indistinguishable). But the kernel exposes a registration SPI, `CustomFieldTypeService.getInstanceV2().registerV2(factory)` (a `ServiceLoader`-free static registry of `ICustomFieldTypeFactory` → `ICustomFieldValidator`; the runtime path the oracle uses consults it). **Registering a factory for `"EMail"` makes the oracle do a REAL check** (probed: `Valid` fires only on a valid e-mail, `Invalid` only on an invalid one, empty → neither — VALUE polarity). The kernel core itself ships only the German set (`kernel-rt/kernel-core-customfieldtype-impl`: IBAN/BIC/Steuernummer — EUPL, reference-only, never vendored).

*(Interpreter, SUPPORTED 2026-06-24: the interpreter mirrors the client SPI with a **consumer-supplied registry** — `PredefinedType(minLength,maxLength,pattern)` (the kernel's `LENGTH_AND_PATTERN_CHECK`) keyed by name, injected into `DmInterpreter`, default the built-in `PredefinedTypeRegistry.DEFAULT` (`EMail`; no tax/gov). `Valid(field,"Name")`/`Invalid(...)` dispatch by arity off the date-construct `Valid`/`Invalid`; an EMPTY field is "not specified" → `UNKNOWN`/no-fire; an **unregistered** type name → the rule is reported **unsupported** (fail-fast), never a silent no-op. Differential-locked by `adapter.laws.PredefinedTypeValidityDiffTest`, which registers the SAME validator in BOTH engines via the SPI so the test verifies the operator semantics (firing/polarity/empty), not the regex. Checksum types (IBAN mod-97 — `PROVIDED_VALIDATION_CODE`) are a future function-valued-registry extension.)*

## CustomCondition — a runtime CLIENT SPI with TWO API levels; the interpreter must support it (probe + source, 2026-06-24)

> Fold: folded → §14.

(The runtime semantics — callback-owned dependency checks, four exact inputs, structural empty eligibility, VALUE polarity, and the bare-form rejection — are canonical in §14; the SPI levels, registration, and oracle wiring below stay as the evidence and treatment record.) `CustomCondition <Name>` delegates the whole fire/don't-fire decision to **client/host code**, resolved by **name** at runtime (like the predefined types — a client SPI, not kernel-core behavior). The kernel's own metamodels use it heavily (MM_Rule/MM_Field/MM_Computation/MM_Typedef…), and the owner confirms real project models use it heavily — so the interpreter MUST support it (decided 2026-06-24).

**Two API levels** (this is the key fork):
- **Core / classic** — `com.mgmtp.a12.kernel.core.rt.a12internal.validation.ICustomCondition` (formerly `IApplicationCondition`): `boolean check(IData data, Set<IIdentifier> relevant, Set<IIdentifier> formallyIncorrect, IIdentifier errorField)`. `IData` = `getValue(IIdentifier)`/`getValueForDisplay`/`iterator`/`identifierToString`; `IIdentifier` = `getName()` (path, ≥2 parts) + `getIndices()` (long[] reps) + `getUsb()`. A constructible `IdentifierImpl(name, long[])` exists in `kernel-core-utils`. The kernel's own metamodel conditions (`CheckEnumOrCategoryValue`, `CheckStringPattern`, `CheckHintList`) implement THIS level.
- **Modern / md** — `com.mgmtp.a12.kernel.md.rt.api.ICustomCondition`: `check(DocumentV2, Set<DocumentMultiPointer> relevant, Set<DocumentPointer> formallyIncorrect, PartiallyKnownDocumentMultiPointer errorEntity)`. `CustomConditionWrapper` (md-runtime-service) adapts md→core and **requires the runtime data be a `ValidationData`** ("a custom condition provided through the md-API must not be used by validation calls which bypass the md-API"). **The owner's projects target THIS (md/DocumentV2) level (decided 2026-06-24).**

**Registration:** the static `CustomConditionService.getInstanceV2().registerV2(ICustomConditionFactory)` (core-level; deprecated, `forRemoval` A12K-3589, but present in 30.8.1) OR the modern `DocumentProcessingConfig.builder(locale).customConditionFactory(...)` passed to `validateFull(DocumentV2, DocumentProcessingConfig)`. The facade `DocumentRtCustomExtensionService.registerCustomConditions(mdFactory)` wraps an md factory in `CustomConditionFactoryWrapper` and registers it into `CustomConditionService`. A factory maps name→condition (`createCustomConditionV2(name): Optional<ICustomCondition>`); first-registered name wins; `cleanRegistry()` for tests.

**The processing context is a complete immutable input record (source-audited 2026-07-22).** Kernel 30.8.1's `DocumentProcessingConfig` carries the effective error-message locale, optional `currentDateForTest`, custom-condition factory, custom-field-type factory, and deprecated `additionalInformation`. The date slot selects deterministic current time/date instead of the host sample; model time-zone rules still interpret temporal values. Deprecation does not erase observability: the low-level config-transfer path still propagates `additionalInformation` for downstream consumers. The interpreter maps locale, clock, and both factories explicitly, alongside its project-charset and model-time-zone inputs; it deliberately excludes legacy additional information and requires affected consumers to close that configuration over their custom implementation. Existing locale, clock, custom-condition, custom-field-type, charset, and time-zone differentials lock the supported inputs; the exclusion is a documented input boundary, not a claim that the kernel slot is absent.

**Runtime semantics (source + dual-strategy differential correction, 2026-07-20):** once ordinary boolean evaluation reaches the leaf, `CustomCondition X` calls `X.check(data, relevant, formallyIncorrect, errorField)` directly. Full validation passes `relevant = null`; partial passes the exact external relevant set. `formallyIncorrect` is the separate exact field-address set produced by that call's formal pass, including required-empty and repeated malformed pointers and excluding non-relevant cells that partial validation did not formally check. `errorField` is the current repeated rule error pointer. The callback—not the evaluator—owns hidden-dependency relevance/formal checks and its Boolean decides the leaf; `true` contributes **VALUE**. A custom leaf is structurally empty-eligible, `Or` propagates any eligible child, and `And` requires both, so `CustomCondition X Or FieldFilled(error)` can run on a blank row while the customary guarded `FieldFilled(error) And CustomCondition X` remains silent. A **bare complete rule** still fails **`MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_REFERENCED`**; another branch may supply the required error-field reference without guarding the custom leaf. The earlier constant-condition probe generalized its guarded-And empty result and an older core-interface sentence into automatic gating; generated Java/Groovy/TypeScript `ApplicationCondition.st`, `MainValidatorController.applikationsBedingung`, `ValidationCache.getAllFehlerhafteFelderExtern`, `CustomConditionWrapper`, and `CanConditionFireEmptyVisitor` establish the corrected mechanism.

**Oracle-harness path (for the differential) — CONFIRMED 2026-06-24, extended 2026-07-20.** Registering a *core* `ICustomCondition` via the static `CustomConditionService` and validating through the deprecated `validateFull(IDocument, Locale)` path invoked **constant** conditions reliably but **not data-reading ones** in the test harness (a throwing condition never threw, yet the rule fired). The reliable path — wired into `RuntimeLaws` as the full and partial custom-condition overloads — is the modern `DocumentV2` validation with `DocumentProcessingConfig.customConditionFactory`; generated-static Java uses the same per-call factory through its retained runtime service. The original ACME probe established guarded-And value/no-value behavior; `CustomConditionInvocationDiffTest` now inspects the supplied formal/relevance/error inputs and empty-eligible `Or` shape across Groovy-dynamic, generated Java, and the interpreter. The md `ICustomCondition` may implement the modern `check(DocumentV2,…)` or the deprecated `check(IDocument,…)` (the default V2 method converts and delegates). `kernel-core-runtime-api` resolves to **32.3.4** in the 30.8.1 distribution; `kernel-core-utils` 30.8.1. md (`DocumentProcessingConfig`/`ICustomConditionFactory`/md `ICustomCondition`/`DocumentV2`) is already on the adapter classpath via `kernel-md-runtime-api`.

**JVM bridge for EXISTING kernel conditions — `KernelCustomConditions.fromCore` (`:adapter`, 2026-06-24).** A JVM consumer adapts a project's existing kernel **core** `ICustomCondition` (or a `name→condition` map) onto the interpreter's pure `CustomCondition` SPI, so the kernel-free interpreter runs the SAME condition object the kernel runs — replacing the kernel's generated code. The bridge supplies its **own** `IData`/`IIdentifier` over the interpreter's `CustomConditionData`/`FieldPointer` (`FieldPointer.indices: List<Int>` ⇄ `IIdentifier.getIndices(): long[]`), constructs **no** kernel `…Impl` (the kernel API documents the caller provides these — with content `equals`/`hashCode` — and the runtime re-wraps them; honors the N8/D15 "never construct kernel Impl" guardrail), and refuses mutation (`IData.addValue` throws — a condition must not mutate the data). This made `:interpreter` an `:adapter` **MAIN** dep and added `kernel-core-runtime-api:32.3.4` (`implementation`). The md `DocumentV2`-shaped conditions are reachable via a thin future `DocumentV2`→`IData` view on top of this core bridge — not built, since core `IData` is the unified cross-platform contract. Locked: `CustomConditionDiffTest` (the real-core-condition-through-the-bridge differential) + `KernelCustomConditionsTest` (bridge data-shaping).

**The JS/TS kernel runtime mirrors the `IData`-based contract EXACTLY — so `IData`-based is the UNIFIED cross-platform contract (re-checked 2026-06-24 at the owner's prompt).** The kernel ships a TypeScript runtime (`kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime-ts` + `kernel-core-runtime-api-ts`) whose JS codegen path DOES support custom conditions, via TS twins of the **core** API: `ICustomCondition.check(data: IData, relevant: Set<IIdentifier>|undefined, formallyIncorrect: Set<IIdentifier>, errorField: IIdentifier): boolean`, `IData.getValue(IIdentifier)`/`iterator`/`addValue`, `IIdentifier.getName()/getIndices()/getUsb()`, and a `CustomConditionService` singleton (`register`/`getCustomCondition`/`cleanRegistry`) + `ICustomConditionFactory.createCustomCondition(name)` — **identical in shape to the Java core API**. There is **no `DocumentV2` in TypeScript** — `DocumentV2` is a JVM-only modern facade (`CustomConditionWrapper` adapts md→core). So the contract that exists on **both** platforms (and that the kernel's own conditions + JS codegen use) is the **`IData`-based** one — the natural, only fully cross-platform choice for a JVM+JS interpreter SPI.

**Interpreter design (kernel-free multiplatform SPI; decided 2026-06-24 — JVM AND JS/TS, prefer modern APIs):** the core defines a **multiplatform pure SPI** in `commonMain` mirroring the **`IData`-based** `ICustomCondition` contract (`CustomCondition.check` over pure `IData`/`IIdentifier`-shaped interfaces: `getValue(path,indices)`, the relevant + formally-incorrect sets, the error entity), so it compiles to JVM **and** JS and existing **Java core** AND **TS** impls plug in (their shapes already match).
