A project's JVM **md `DocumentV2`** impl is adapted via a thin JVM-only `DocumentV2`→`IData`-view adapter if needed. **"Prefer modern APIs"** applies to the **oracle/runtime path** (`validateFull(DocumentV2, DocumentProcessingConfig.customConditionFactory(...))`, not the deprecated `Locale`/static-service path), NOT to the SPI *contract* (which must be the cross-platform `IData`-based one). `CustomCondition X` parses to `Cond.Custom(name)`; unregistered name → reported unsupported (fail-fast). Implementation: INTERPRETER-ARCHITECTURE.md §7 (custom conditions).

## Custom-extensibility surface — the kernel registers exactly TWO custom things (conditions + field types); there is NO custom computation/function (source-verified 2026-06-26)

> Fold: folded → §14.

The umbrella over the two SPI findings above (§8 predefined-type validity, and CustomCondition): the kernel's **project-facing extension API** — `kernel-md-facade/.../DocumentRtCustomExtensionService` — exposes exactly **two** custom-registration hooks, `registerCustomConditions(ICustomConditionFactory)` and `registerCustomFieldTypes(ICustomFieldTypeFactory)` (each with a `…V2`). **There is no `registerCustomComputations`/`registerCustomFunction`/`registerCalculation` — a custom *computation* is not a kernel concept.** Corroborated four independent ways:

- **German-source sweep** (`.java` + `.g4`): the only `Applikations*` construct is `ApplikationsBedingung` (= CustomCondition). No `ApplikationsBerechnung`/`-Funktion`.
- **SPI interfaces:** the only `I(Custom|Application)*` types are condition / field-type / field-validator / **migration** (`ICustomMigrationCode` — a *third* custom hook, but it is data-migration between model versions, not validation or computation). None for functions/computations.
- **Computation vocabulary is closed:** `CalcOp` is an enum `{ADD, MULTIPLY, SUBTRACT, DIVIDE, POWER}`; a computation operation is built solely from it + the standard value-expression catalog (rounding, aggregates, date math, …). `ICalculationService`/`IBaseCalculationService` are the *internal* evaluator (`checkCalculationRule`, no register/custom methods), not a project SPI.
- **The one custom hook that could reach a computation is barred:** `CustomCondition` is **forbidden inside computation rules** (KERNEL-SEMANTICS §14).

So the kernel's custom escape hatches are for **conditions** and **field types** only. (Challenged + re-verified 2026-06-26 across kernel source, BA/semantics docs, the grammar, and this registration API.) rulekit/CLI treatment: a computation's operation is the closed `Calculation` sealed set; the interpreter's treatment (an unknown function fails loud) is INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF22.

## §8 supplement — a CUSTOM-typed field is validated by FULL DELEGATION to a registered validator; an unregistered one THROWS (source-verified 2026-06-26)

> Fold: folded → the field-types table.

How the kernel validates a field declared with a **`CustomFieldType{name, minLength?, maxLength?}`** at `validateFull` (the sibling of the §8 `Valid`/`Invalid` *operator* — this is the *declared-field* form). Source: `FormatDefinitionVordefiniert.validiereFormat` (+ its TS twin `FormatDefinitionVordefiniert.ts`, byte-identical):

- **Full delegation, NOT generic enforcement.** Unlike a plain `StringType` (whose `FormatDefinitionString` checks `minLength`/`maxLength` itself → `stringZuKurz`/`stringZuLang`), a `CustomFieldType` field delegates the **entire** check — length included — to the registered `ICustomFieldValidator.validate(value, param, …)`. The kernel performs **no** length comparison of its own.
- **The declared bounds are only HINTS.** The kernel passes effective `minLength`/`maxLength` values to the validator via `ICustomFieldTypeValidationParam`; when the DM declaration omits them, both dynamic-Groovy and generated Java normalize them to `1` and `999`. The raw model metadata remains absent, and the kernel still performs no generic length comparison—the validator owns the decision.
- **The validator context is observable.** `validate(value, param, isDisplayValue)` receives the validation call's locale through `param`, the effective declared/defaulted bounds, and a separate display-value flag. The public `IDocumentRtService` stored-document validation path passes `isDisplayValue = false` in both dynamic-Groovy and generated Java; `true` belongs to a lower-level presentation-validation path, not to ordinary document validation. The sibling explicit `Valid`/`Invalid(field, type)` condition invokes the validator without a declaring field's bounds, with the runtime's fixed condition locale and stored-value mode.
- **Relevance short-circuits invocation, and one formal result is reused.** A non-relevant custom cell is not passed to the validator. For a relevant cell the formal pass obtains the validator result once and reuses that formal state for message emission and downstream unknown/suppression behavior; a stateful validator or remote bridge must not be observed twice for one cell in one pass.
- **No registered validator → it THROWS.** `getCustomFieldValidator` resolves the validator from the factory / global `CustomFieldTypeService`; missing → `ValidatorException("Custom field validator '<name>' missing.")`, which `validateFull` surfaces as `FaultException("Unable to invoke validation")`. So a *filled* custom-typed field on a bare model fails the **whole** validation (an empty/not-relevant cell short-circuits before reaching it). There is no "fire a formal error" and no "skip".
- **The error code is the validator's OWN key**, `ICustomFieldTypeCheckError.getErrorKey()` (project-specific, e.g. `"invalidCreditCardNumber"`) — **not** a fixed kernel code like the `StringType` length codes.
- **The validator's own message is processed by the ordinary error handler.** Every literal `$<fieldName>$` token in the returned message is replaced by that concrete field instance's localized identifier; ordinary text is preserved.
- **The bounds DO reach the model + runtime** (`md.model.api.fieldtypes.ICustomFieldType.getMinLength/getMaxLength`; codegen carries them into the validation param) — they are real model properties, just never kernel-enforced.

This is the kernel truth behind the interpreter's treatment (auto-validate each filled custom cell against the consumer-registered predefined type; unregistered → report unsupported + degrade leniently, since the native runtime cannot host the validator) — INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF26. JS/TS identical, so a Kotlin/JS consumer mirrors it.

## §12 supplement — a starred VALUE-LIST quantifier's complete extent needs wildcard coverage (source + differential, 2026-07-22, SPEC-2026-07-22-09)

> Fold: folded → §12.

In partial validation a starred value-list quantifier entry carries a **complete-extent** fact SEPARATE from its per-cell relevance: the extent is established only when the relevant set **wildcards** the starred level (`ALL_SEMANTIC`, or a relevant ancestor whose descent covers it) — enumerating every currently-instantiated row CONCRETELY does not. An extent-unknown entry is gated **side-specifically**, exactly the IF42 all-rows-aggregate rule applied to the value-list operators: `No` is suppressed to UNKNOWN on **either** side (`RuntimeController.keinFeldWertInWerteListeIntern` and `fuegeFeldWerteZuWerteListe` reject an extent-unknown starred fields/values entry before scanning), `NotAll` on the **values** side, while `AtLeastOne` (either side) and `NotAll` on the fields side scan the relevant cells and ignore extent (`mindestensEinFeldWertInWerteListeIntern` / fields-side `nichtAlleFeldWerteInWerteListeIntern`). Full validation is unaffected.

**Interpreter:** the fix reuses the existing `starRelevant` wildcard-extent seam (IF42) — `ValueListQuantEval` sets a `VlqOperand.extentUnknown` bit from `starRelevant(starPath)` (null in full validation → never set → the full path is byte-identical) and routes it through the same side-specific arms (since IF218 the fields-side gate for `No` sits at the operand's position in the ordered scan); per-cell relevance and formal-cell classification are unchanged. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.PartialValidationValueListDiffTest` — dynamic-Groovy vs static-Java vs interpreter, the three extent-sensitive cases plus fields-side-`NotAll`/`AtLeastOne`/non-repeatable controls — + multiplatform `interpreter … PartialValidationValueListExtentTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF42.)*

## §4/§12 supplement — an eligible empty INDEX field's declared S-value: FULL validation stages it, PARTIAL silently suppresses the cell (source + dual-strategy differential, 2026-07-23, SPEC-2026-07-23-14)

> Fold: folded → §4 + §12.

An ordinary closed **Enumeration may declare one stored token as its S-value** (DM-JSON `sValue` on `EnumerationType`; `IEnumerationType.getsValue`) — a **model-owned INDEX default**, not a document value. One index-cell instance is **eligible** when the index field is *not explicitly mandatory* (the author's requiredness — the auto index requiredness doesn't count), its repeatable group has **exactly one physically instantiated row under the current parent** (per-parent scoping: sibling parents are independent), and that row's index cell is **clean-empty** — physically absent or present-empty, never an admitted explicit value (`IndexFieldDefaultValueUtils.getIndexFieldIdsEligibleForDefaultValueHandling`). What happens next splits by CALL KIND, not by the relevant set's extent (`ValidationCache.addDefaultValuesForIndexFieldsIfNecessary`, invoked from `CheckCommand.formalePruefung`'s ingestion tail):

- **FULL validation stages the S-value into its transient evaluation cache** — before the generated index mandatory/uniqueness checks and before authored rules, **without mutating the document** (`setIndexFieldToDefaultValue` → `addFeldToDatenCache`, which also feeds the index cache and flips group fill). Observables (dual-kernel-route + JVM/Node locked): no `mandatoryField`/`uniqueIndex` for the defaulted cell; a dependent authored rule reading the index **sees the staged token** (`[ServiceLevel] == "STD"` fires on the otherwise-bare row); a bare eligible row satisfies its generated checks entirely.
- **PARTIAL validation does NOT stage it** — even with the whole document wildcard-relevant. The same eligible cell is **silently marked unavailable for that call** (`markFieldAsInvalidForValidation`: the formal-invalid set + `erroneousGroups` propagation — **no message**): neither `mandatoryField` nor `uniqueIndex` is emitted, dependent authored conditions read UNKNOWN and are Kleene-suppressed, and the **containing group retains error state** (a `NoGroupFilled` over the group cannot prove absence). This prevents partial from reporting errors full validation would not.
- **Ineligible shapes get no default in either call kind**: multiple sibling rows (each empty index draws its ordinary `mandatoryField` — and NO injected `uniqueIndex` pair, the duplicate/default interaction), an admitted explicit value (evaluates normally), an explicitly mandatory index (`mandatoryField` stands), a non-Enumeration index (the S-value is not even representable), and a **formally invalid explicit value stays unavailable and never exposes the S-value** to authored evaluation. With **zero** instantiated rows nothing is staged and **no phantom row** appears (`NoGroupFilled(G*)` still fires on the genuinely empty group).

**Interpreter reconciliation (SPEC-2026-07-23-14):** the model surfaces gained the representation (modelkit `EnumSpec.sValue` + `EnumFieldBuilder.sValue(...)`, the DM-JSON key round-trips through the CLI field-spec; the interpreter model carries `FieldDef.enumSValue` → `ModelDef.indexDefaults`); a FULL call stages the eligible cells through the existing call-local computed-overlay document (never mutating the caller's document), and a PARTIAL call feeds the same eligible cells to the formal-status cache as silently **unavailable** (a message-less generated failure that suppresses the generated checks, turns dependent reads UNKNOWN, and joins the group-presence error state) — INTERPRETER-FINDINGS. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.IndexDefaultStagingDiffTest` — absent/present-empty staging, dependent-rule visibility, admitted-explicit/invalid-explicit/multi-row/mandatory controls, explicit-duplicate control, per-parent scoping, no-phantom-row, and the four partial-suppression arms — dynamic-Groovy · static-Java · interpreter, full AND `validatePart`; `interpreter.IndexDefaultStagingMultiplatformTest` — the pure engine on JVM + Node, including document-not-mutated; authoring: `modelkit.EnumSValueTest`.)*

**Two-corner supplement (IG104 probe, 2026-07-24) — the staging cache is EVAL-only, and an out-of-domain S-value is a MODEL error, both dual-strategy-confirmed:**

- **A `$field.value$` message interpolation over a STAGED index cell renders as if the cell were EMPTY** (→ the field's format default, `""` for an enum, `0` for a number) — even though the same staged token DECIDED the firing condition. Mechanism (source): the staging writes only into the transient `ValidationCache.datenCache` (which `getWertXML` / condition reads consult), while `MainValidatorController.getReplacementForParam` reads the **original caller data** — `validationCache.getDaten().getValueForDisplay(id)` — which never sees that cache; an empty display then falls to `IFormatDefinition.getDefaultValue`. So the split is between the eval cache (staged) and the caller document (`getDaten`, original). *(Locked dual-route + interpreter: `adapter.laws.StagedIndexDefaultInterpolationDiffTest`, `interpreter.StagedIndexDefaultInterpolationMultiplatformTest` (JVM+Node); interpreter reconciliation IF221.)*
- **An enum `sValue` OUTSIDE its declared value domain is a MODEL-consistency error, `MVK_S_VALUE_INVALID`** ("The s-value '…' is not contained in the list of XML-values for the enumeration"), reported by `checkConsistency` — and **BOTH kernel strategies REFUSE to generate validation code** (dynamic-Groovy throws `DocumentValidationException`; static-Java's `generateValidationCode` returns null). There is no runtime "staged-formal-check" path: an out-of-domain S-value model is simply not kernel-consistent and never runs. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.OutOfDomainEnumSValueRejectionTest` (kernel MVK stance + the interpreter loader's matching loud refusal), `interpreter.load.EnumSValueLoadTest` (JVM+Node); interpreter reconciliation IF221.)*

## §12 supplement — Partial validation (`validatePart`) — rule-gating + 3VL relevance (source + probe-confirmed 2026-06-24)

> Fold: folded → §12.

`validatePart(DocumentV2, Set<DocumentMultiPointer> relevantEntities, DocumentProcessingConfig)` validates only a *part* of the document. The contract (kernel javadoc on `IDocumentRtService.validatePart`, probe-confirmed) is two mechanisms:

- **Rule-level filter skip precedes relevance and 3VL.** Every code-generation target's rule-definition template (`validation/java`, `validation/groovy`, and `validation/java_script_vk` `rulesDir/CodeGenRuleDefinition.st`) emits a method-entry return under `t.filterConditions` when the validation cache is in partial-with-3VL mode. Generated Java and Groovy confirm the emitted branch, and the BA appendix lists rules with filter conditions among the checks partial validation may skip. Therefore a rule containing any `Having` filter does not run in partial validation even when its error field and all field instances are wildcard-relevant; the same rule evaluates normally under full validation. This is the shared mechanism behind the seed-33 fuzz mismatch, not a value-specific correlation edge. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.PartialValidationFilterSkipDiffTest`, with equality and ordered-correlation controls live under full validation and absent under partial; the exact randomized reproducer remains in `PartialValidationFuzzDiffTest`.)*

- **Rule-gating by the error field.** A validation rule is **executed only if its error-field instance is in the relevant set** (this includes the auto-generated formal/mandatory/unique field checks, whose error field is the field itself). So a rule whose error field is not relevant simply does not run — no message.
- **Suppression via 3VL on non-relevant references.** Of the rules that *do* run, "validation errors will be suppressed if the rule references any repetition of a field/group that is not marked as relevant — **except for some cases where the kernel is sure that no possible value of that field/group repetition could have prevented the error**." That exception is exactly **Kleene three-valued logic**: a non-relevant reference is **UNKNOWN**, and `true Or Unknown = true` (still fires — no value could prevent it) while `true And Unknown = Unknown` (suppressed). Probe over a two-field rule confirmed: `And` with only the error field relevant → suppressed; the same as `Or` → fires; only the *other* field relevant (error field not) → not run.

Relevant pointers: `DocumentMultiPointer.of(fullName, repetitions)` with **`ALL_SEMANTIC` (index `0`)** as the per-level wildcard ("all repetitions"); a relevant **group** makes all its **descendants** relevant. The kernel **encourages** wildcarding every path level — a *subset* of repetitions has subtle suppression around wildcards/aggregates (`/RG*/f1` references not-filled reps too) and `NumberOfFilled…`/semantic-index/parallel-iteration.

**GLOBAL fields are auto-added to the relevant set — but by the MD-LAYER, not the core validator (source-verified 2026-06-26).** A field flagged **`global`** (DM-JSON `"global": true` in the Field body; `IField.isGlobal`) is **always relevant in partial validation**, regardless of the caller's set. The mechanism is precise: the **core** validator (`ValidationCommand`) does *not* touch global fields; the **md-runtime-service** layer — exactly the `IDocumentRtService.validatePart(DocumentV2, …, config)` boundary the oracle calls — injects them first (`LowKernelApiUtils.validatePartlyWith3ValueLogic` → `addGlobalFieldsToRelevantEntities`, which adds `metaModel.getValue("GlobalFields")` = each global field's pointer **wildcarded at all reps**, `MetaModelImpl` building them with all-zero `ALL_SEMANTIC` indices). `IField.isGlobal`'s own javadoc states the contract: *"Global fields should always be passed to a partial validation in addition to the usual relevant entities. The caller of the validation is responsible for passing the global fields."* So at the kernel's *public* runtime boundary they ARE auto-added; a rule whose error field is global therefore RUNS even when the caller's relevant set omits it. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.PartialValidationGlobalFieldDiffTest` — global vs. non-global control vs. the kernel; the loader mapping by `cli.interp.InterpreterRealModelDiffTest.loaderReadsTheGlobalFieldFlagIntoModelDef`.)*

**Interpreter treatment:** `PreparedModel` records `hasFilterConditions` once per rule and `DmInterpreter.rowOutcomes` returns before iteration, correlation planning, or condition evaluation when that flag meets a partial-validation pass. This is an O(1) hot-path branch and makes partial filtered rules cheaper, while full validation retains the existing indexed correlation path unchanged. For every non-filtered rule, the remaining behavior maps directly onto the existing 3VL engine — `DmInterpreter.validatePart` gates each emitted message by its error field's relevance and resolves non-relevant referenced instances to `Val.Unknown` (the same `invalid`-operand mechanism formal errors already use), so Kleene logic yields the kernel's exact suppression. **Starred-aggregate relevance is PER-OPERATOR (probe-confirmed 2026-06-26).** Under a subset relevant set, an aggregate over a starred reference `G*/f` is relevant per its operator:
- **all-rows aggregates** (`Sum`, `MaxValue`, `MinValue`, `NumberOfDifferentValues`, `SumOfProducts`) are evaluated **only when the relevant set WILDCARDS the starred level** — `ALL_SEMANTIC` at the repeatable group, or a relevant ANCESTOR group (descent makes all rows relevant). Enumerating concrete rows — **even ALL of them** — leaves the aggregate UNKNOWN (no firing): the `*` reference's extent is "fully known" only via a wildcard (the BA's "encourages wildcarding every path level"). Controlled probe (2 rows, no fillable tail): `Sum(Lines* / Price) > 1000` fires under a `Lines` wildcard but NOT under `{Lines[1], Lines[2]}` listed concretely.
- **`FirstFilled`** is **order-aware**: it returns the first FILLED cell's value and is UNKNOWN only when a non-relevant cell precedes that first filled cell. So it fires when the first filled row is relevant (wildcard, both-rows, or that-row-only) and is UNKNOWN when that row is non-relevant (probe: row-1-filled fires under row-1-only, suppresses under row-2-only).

This is exactly the runtime `isWertAngegeben` 3VL (a non-relevant cell read is UNKNOWN; an all-rows fold with any UNKNOWN → UNKNOWN), with the wildcard-vs-concrete distinction governing whether the star's cells are check-relevant. *(Interpreter: `DmInterpreter.starRelevantOf` + the per-operator `evalFunc` gate, IF42; locked `adapter.laws.PartialValidationAggregateDiffTest` + the randomized `perf.PartialValidationFuzzDiffTest`.)*

The oracle path is the modern `validatePart(DocumentV2, …, config)` (`RuntimeLaws.firedCodesPart`/`messagesPart`). Global fields are auto-relevant (IF37, above); a `CustomCondition` receives the relevant set (null for full, the subset for partial — IF38, source `MainValidatorController` → `getAllPruefungsrelevanteEntitiesExtern`; the set is the caller's *external* pointers **as passed** — wildcards preserved, groups NOT expanded — plus the auto-added globals); and uniqueness needs the duplicate **partner** relevant too (IF39 — the kernel's duplicate caches are built from the relevant fields only, `befuelleCacheMitRelevantenFeldern`). *(Locked: `adapter/…/laws/PartialValidationDiffTest` + `interpreter/commonTest/PartialValidationTest`; the rule-level `Having` skip by `PartialValidationFilterSkipDiffTest`; subset-repetition aggregates by `PartialValidationSubsetRepDiffTest` (IF35, non-directional); global fields by `PartialValidationGlobalFieldDiffTest` (IF37); custom-condition relevant-set propagation by `PartialValidationCustomConditionDiffTest` (IF38); subset uniqueness by `PartialValidationUniqueIndexDiffTest` + `PartialValidationRepetitionNotUniqueDiffTest` (IF39); per-operator starred-aggregate relevance by `PartialValidationAggregateDiffTest` (IF42, above); and a randomized `PartialValidationFuzzDiffTest` sweeps (document × relevant-subset) combinations across the whole partial surface. The interpreter matches the pinned kernel across that exercised surface.)*

## §9 supplement — `validateFull` also emits STRUCTURAL over-repetition errors — `zuGrosseZeile` / `zuGrosseKontextnummer` (model-fuzz finding, 2026-06-24)

> Fold: folded → §9.

A document instance with **more rows than the group's declared `repeatability`** (or a repetition index beyond it) is flagged by the kernel's `validateFull` with **`zuGrosseZeile`** ("row too large" — the repeatable group) and **`zuGrosseKontextnummer`** ("context number too large" — each over-limit field instance), as VALUE errors; these also **suppress** the ordinary checks (uniqueIndex/mandatory/rules) on the over-limit rows. This is a **structural/consistency** check on the document's *shape*, not a rule-evaluation result — the kernel runtime emits it alongside rule firings. The model-fuzz harness (E) surfaced it by generating documents whose row counts exceeded a low declared repeatability.

The check is per **entity instance** (`FormalChecker.checkIndicesLessOrEqualThanMax`): walk the instance's repetition indices against the per-level declared maxima; the first level over its max emits — the instance's **own** (innermost) over-limit level → `zuGrosseZeile`, an **intermediate** (ancestor) over-limit level → `zuGrosseKontextnummer` — then stops. So a repeatable **group** row whose own index is over → `zuGrosseZeile` (the group entity, its own trailing index omitted in the pointer); a **field** inside an over-limit ancestor → `zuGrosseKontextnummer` (full field pointer). (A third sibling, `FEHLER_LAUFENDE_NR_ZU_GROSS`, rides the USB/multi-form form counter — out of a single-document model's scope.) Interpreter treatment: now **modeled** (emission + suppression), INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF27.

**It is an ADDRESS-FORMAL gate that runs BEFORE scalar conversion — not a later annotation over an already scalar-checked cell (source + dual-strategy differential, SPEC-2026-07-23-13, 2026-07-23).** `CheckCommand.formalePruefung` invokes `FormalChecker.checkIndices` *before* value conversion and inserts a cell/group into the `ValidationCache` **only when the address gate succeeds**, so an over-limit coordinate never reaches scalar/custom checking and is absent from the evaluation cache. The observable consequences (each dual-kernel-route + JVM/Node locked):

- The first over-limit coordinate supplies `zuGrosseZeile`/`zuGrosseKontextnummer` **in place of** any scalar/custom cause — an over-limit descendant that is *valid*, *malformed*, *absent*, or *registered-custom* all expose over-repetition, never a format/custom result. A registered `CustomFieldType` callback the kernel would otherwise invoke on that cell is **not invoked** (proven with an instrumented validator on both kernel strategies + the interpreter). An **absent** over-limit descendant reads **unavailable**, not clean empty — a per-row rule referencing it is suppressed, not fired on empty→0.
- The ancestry walk is complete: an in-cap **inner** coordinate beneath an over-limit **outer** ancestor is still over-repetition (terminal-axis-only checking is wrong); an in-cap sibling stays ordinary.
- Because the over-limit cell/row is never in the cache, it is **outside every star aggregate / quantifier / count domain**: `Sum`, `NumberOfFilledFields`, and `NumberOfFilledGroups(G*)` range over the in-cap (declared-range) rows only — **clean and definite, never poisoned** (with rows `10.00`, `20.00` in cap and an over-limit `99.00`, `Sum` is `30.00` and the count is `2`). The immutable physical over-limit row nonetheless stays **instantiated**: it emits `zuGrosseZeile` and remains `GroupFilled`-visible structural content for its non-repeatable ancestors. (This is distinct from the index-field mandatory/uniqueness rules `AutogeneratedRulesService` runs in the later preliminary-rule phase.)
- **The same exclusion holds in the index-keyed PARALLEL ITERATION** (the §9 outer join, validation and compute — dual-kernel-route, 2026-07-24): an over-limit row is not in the `IndexFieldCache`, so its index value is **not a join key** and a reference to it reads **UNMATCHED**, not its value. Observable in COMPUTE: a calc `Total*/Amount = [../Demand/Amount] + [../Supply/Amount]` whose `Total[Extra]` key matches only an OVER-LIMIT `Demand[Extra]` row reads that side **empty-as-0** (`Total[Extra]` = `0`), never the over-limit cell's poison — the reader computes cleanly. The exclusion covers the joined groups, the common-parent rows, and a repeatable **frame** row above a joined group (`/Plan/X(frame)/A ⋈ B`), all the same way. In VALIDATION a per-row cross-group compare over the over-limit key does not fire (its cell reads unavailable either way, so the interpreter already agreed there). *(Interpreter reconciliation IF220: the compute divergence was the interpreter's `parallelContexts` join reading the rows unfiltered, so the over-limit row joined and its cell poisoned the reader; fixed by the same [`overLimitRows`] exclusion the star domain uses, across `indexMap` / `parentRowsOf` / the frame-row expansion — no second walker.)*

Interpreter reconciliation (SPEC-2026-07-23-13): the per-cell gate order + ancestry walk already matched (the phase separators landed as agreement locks), so the sole **observed divergence** was that over-limit rows leaked into the interpreter's star-aggregate row domain — fixed by excluding them from that domain via the one document-owned `declaredReps` decision, while per-row rule suppression and structural emission keep reading them (INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF27); the parallel-iteration join was the same class, excluded at the same root (IF220). *(Locked: `adapter.laws.OverRepetitionFormalGateDiffTest` — the phase separators + the aggregate-exclusion siblings, dynamic-Groovy · static-Java · interpreter; `interpreter.OverRepetitionFormalGateMultiplatformTest` — the pure engine on JVM + Node; the parallel-iteration domain by `adapter.laws.OverLimitParallelIterationDiffTest` — plain + framed, validation + compute — and multiplatform `interpreter.OverLimitParallelIterationTest`.)*

## `mandatoryGroup` (`FEHLER_PFLICHTKONTEXT`) is a DEAD code in 30.8.1 — defined but never emitted; a group cannot be declared mandatory (source-verified 2026-06-26)

> Fold: not-foldable — a dead-code inventory fact (defined, never emitted) with its re-open trigger; nothing to fold.

The runtime formal-error code **`mandatoryGroup`** (`IFormaleFehlerConstants.FEHLER_PFLICHTKONTEXT`, "auto generated rule: mandatory group") is **referenced nowhere** in the kernel `30.8.1` source except its own constant definition (Java + the TS twin) — **no code, codegen template, or generated validator emits it**. And **no DM-JSON property declares a *group* mandatory**: the md-serializer and the `a12-kernel-mm` metamodels carry mandatory/`requirednessConfig` only on **fields** (→ the `mandatoryField` rules `AutogeneratedRulesService` generates, KERNEL-FINDINGS §4), not on groups. So `mandatoryGroup` **cannot fire on any loadable DM model** — an unemitted code over an undeclarable property (a probe accordingly returned empty). The interpreter correctly never emits it; this is **parity by construction**, not a gap. *Re-open trigger:* a kernel upgrade that wires `FEHLER_PFLICHTKONTEXT` to an emission site **and** a group-mandatory declaration (re-check on the next [`KERNEL-UPGRADE`](KERNEL-UPGRADE.md)).

## `multiSelect` value uniqueness (`FEHLER_MULTI_SELECT` = `uniqueValue`) is DEAD code in 30.8.1 — generated by NEITHER strategy (source-verified 2026-06-27)

> Fold: not-foldable — a dead-code inventory fact (generated by neither strategy) with its re-open trigger; nothing to fold.

A `usageType == "multi-select"` group (KERNEL-FINDINGS "group templates", the `MultiSelectGroupRule` consistency shape) is described as carrying an auto value-uniqueness check — "no value selected twice". In kernel `30.8.1` **that check is generated by no evaluation path**, so it never fires on any loadable model:

- **md-RUNTIME** (`AutogeneratedRulesService`, the `IDocumentRtService` autogen provider): generates only `MANDATORY_FIELD` / `UNIQUE_FIELD` (index) / `SOLITARY_FIELD` rules — there is **no multi-select method** at all. (Confirmed by differential: a 3-row multi-select group with duplicate values fires `mandatoryField` on an empty row but **nothing** for the duplicate — `adapter.laws.MultiSelectUniquenessDiffTest`.)
- **Groovy CODEGEN** (`CodeGenRuleDefinition`): has a `case UNIQUE_VALUE → "FEHLER_MULTI_SELECT"`, but that case is **provably unreachable**. `RuleInfo.getAutogenType()` returns `((IAutogeneratedRule) rule).getGenerationType()`, and the **only** `IAutogeneratedRule` implementations (`MandatoryFieldRuleImpl` / `UniqueFieldRuleImpl` / `SolitaryFieldRuleImpl`) return `MANDATORY_FIELD` / `UNIQUE_FIELD` / `SOLITARY_FIELD` — **none returns `UNIQUE_VALUE`**, and no code constructs such a rule. So the `case UNIQUE_VALUE` switch arm is dead.

**The COMPLETE `usageType: "multi-select"` handling map (full read/write trace, 2026-06-27).** Tracing every site that writes or reads the flag across the whole kernel shows it is a **model + editor + consistency marker ONLY** — no evaluator touches it:

| layer | site | what it does with `multi-select` |
|---|---|---|
| write | `GroupSerializer` | serializes `usageType` to DM-JSON |
| write | editor / `DocumentModelExpand`+`CollapseService` | sets / propagates / unsets across include expansion |
| read (consistency) | `MultiSelectGroupRule` | **the only active handler** — validates the group SHAPE at `checkConsistency` (repeatable · one enum/string field · required · not-index) → `DOCUMENT_MODEL_MULTI_SELECT_*` problems |
| read (model→runtime) | `ValidationModelConverter:194-209` | stores `usageType` in the validation-model `Group`, but only the `A12_ATTACHMENT` branch *acts* on it — multi-select is stored then **never consumed** |
| read (attachment-only) | `DecorationField/GroupModelJoiner`, `ExpandService:689` | all gated on `A12_ATTACHMENT`/`cockpit_attachment` — never multi-select |
| runtime eval (`kernel-rt`) | — | reads `usageType` **nowhere** (only the dead `FEHLER_MULTI_SELECT` constant) |
| codegen (`kernel-tool`) | — | reads `usageType` **nowhere** (only the dead `UNIQUE_VALUE_ERROR_ID`); **no `.stg` template emits it** |
| generated code | — | no `.stg`/`.groovy` references `multi-select`/`uniqueValue` |

So `usageType: "multi-select"` drives only the UI checkbox-list widget + the `checkConsistency` shape check; it reaches the validation (runtime) model via `ValidationModelConverter` but `kernel-rt` never reads it back (dead storage). For ANY document-validation evaluator (runtime or codegen-equivalent) it is a no-op beyond the shape check.

`uniqueValue` is therefore **vestigial** — the runtime-and-codegen sibling of the dead `mandatoryGroup` above. Both kernel strategies produce nothing, so the interpreter (matching BOTH per the CLAUDE.md #1 rule) correctly does **not** fire it — **parity by construction**, NOT a gap, and NOT a runtime-vs-codegen divergence (they agree: dead in both). **Corroborated from the model side (2026-06-27 sweep):** all **6** real multi-select groups in the 2025.06-ext5 workspaces are the minimal shape (`repeatability` + `usageType: "multi-select"` + one field — **no** `indexFieldName` / `unique` / extra property that could trigger a uniqueness rule), and the **`a12-kernel-mm` MM_Group metamodel** defines `multi-select` as merely a `usageType` **enum value** (beside `attachment`/`complexType`) with **no uniqueness constraint attached** (zero `uniqueValue`/`FEHLER_MULTI_SELECT` anywhere in the metamodels). Source + runtime + codegen + real models + metamodel all agree. The kernel's *consistency/editor* layer still validates the multi-select group **shape** (`MultiSelectGroupRule`) and the UI widget still prevents duplicate selection — but neither produces a `validateFull`/runtime `uniqueValue` message. *Re-open trigger:* a kernel upgrade that adds a multi-select method to `AutogeneratedRulesService` **or** an `IAutogeneratedRule` returning `UNIQUE_VALUE` (re-check on the next [`KERNEL-UPGRADE`](KERNEL-UPGRADE.md)). *(Locked: `adapter.laws.MultiSelectUniquenessDiffTest`.)*

## `IRule.getCustomRefErrorEntitiesIfFilled/IfNotFilled` (modeler-defined error-entity attribution) is UNWIRED in 30.8.1 (source-verified 2026-06-26)

> Fold: not-foldable — an unwired kernel API slot (feature not supported in 30.8.1) with its re-open trigger; nothing to fold.

A rule can, *in the metamodel*, carry **custom-ref error entities** (`MM_Common_v2` defines a `CustomRefErrorEntities` slot with `CustomRefErrorEntityIfFilled`/`IfNotFilled`) — modeler-defined relative paths that refine **which field(s) a fired rule blames**, chosen by the referenced field's filled-state (the "nichtangabe-fehlerfelder"). But the feature is **not supported in kernel 30.8.1**: the md-model rule mapping (`kernel-md-model/.../mapping/Rule.java`) returns `List.of()` for both accessors, commented *"update implementation when customRefErrorEntities get supported in A12"* (A12K-2281 / A12K-2361); the `IRule` default and every autogenerated rule impl (Mandatory / UniqueField / SolitaryField) also return empty; and `RuleParser` short-circuits the candidate-relpath expansion whenever both are empty — i.e. **always**. So a loaded rule **never** carries custom-ref error entities, and the kernel **always** uses its default automatic error-entity attribution. The interpreter attributing a fired rule to its declared error entity is therefore **parity by construction** (the default path the kernel always takes); the modeler-defined refinement is moot until the kernel wires it. *Re-open trigger:* a kernel upgrade that lands A12K-2281/2361 (re-check on the next [`KERNEL-UPGRADE`](KERNEL-UPGRADE.md)).

## §8 supplement — the ==/!= string-enum compatibility matrix (display-texts, probe-confirmed 2026-06-17)

> Fold: folded → §8.

For `==`/`!=` between direct string/enum fields, the kernel first classifies each Enumeration by its **effective display remapping** (`IEnumerationType.hasDisplayValues`), not by whether label nodes were authored. No labels and identity-only labels (display equals stored token for every value) are effectively textless; either may compare with a plain String or another effectively textless Enumeration. A genuinely display-bearing Enumeration is incompatible with those profiles (`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_ENUM_TO_STRING`). Two display-bearing Enumerations need not have identical declarations: disjoint and partially overlapping maps are accepted, but in every shared locale their overlap must be a partial bijection — a shared stored token cannot map to different displays and a shared display cannot denote different stored tokens (`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_ENUM_TO_ENUM`). An identity pair still participates in that consistency test once another member makes both profiles display-bearing. Literal-domain checking and category projection remain separate. (Source `IEnumerationType.hasDisplayValues`, `FieldUtils.isEnumWithDisplayValues`, `CheckVergleichsBedingungImpl.checkEnumVsStringType`, and `CheckEntityListenUtils.checkDisplayValues`; a category comparison is exempt.) *(Locked: `validate.laws.EnumComparabilityLawsTest`.)*

## §8 supplement — three enum-ish kinds: "extensible enum" is an IStringType quality, NOT a field type (source-verified 2026-06-17)

> Fold: not-foldable — a type-system/model-structure classification (what is a field type vs a client-SPI quality), outside the evaluation scope.

Three distinct concepts, easy to conflate:

- **Closed `EnumerationType`** — a fixed value set; a normal DM-JSON field type (the md `EnumerationType` DTO: values/categories/errorMessage/alphabeticalSorting/sValue — *no* extensible flag).
- **Extensible (non-closed) enumeration** — **not a field type at all**: an **`IStringType` with `isExtensibleEnum() == true`** (parser `FieldUtils.isExtensibleEnum` = `type instanceof IStringType && isExtensibleEnum()`; `isLikeString` = string ∪ enum ∪ ext-enum). `isExtensibleEnum()` is a **client-SPI/runtime quality** — `IStringType` is "intended to be implemented by clients", the kernel's md `StringType` **defaults it `false`**, and there is **no DM-JSON property** for it (its open/dynamic values are served at runtime, e.g. via `DynamicEnumerationService`). So a DM-JSON field is **just a `StringType` — fully buildable and modelkit-authorable**; the "extensible" quality itself is unauthorable via DM-JSON (always `false` in a plain model) but doesn't need to be: `ValidType`/`InvalidType` accept the field **because it is-a string** (`isLikeString`), which the N7 `Valid(CustomerName, "EMail")` validExample already exercises. **NOT a modelkit gap.**
- **Dynamic / table-backed enumeration** — the genuinely **un-buildable** one (the `ValueNotConsistent` finding below): values from an external table, not DM-JSON-serializable.

(The model/editor metamodel `MM_Common` carries an "ExtensibleEnumerationType" config — maxLength/displayValues — and form-model/UI notions like `fieldConfigurationEntry`/`externalEnumeration` exist, but those are **tooling/FM layers outside rulekit's DM-JSON scope**, not runtime DM field types.) *(Corrects an earlier draft that wrongly called extensible enums "un-buildable" and cited `IEnumerationType.isExtensible` — it conflated the extensible `IStringType` quality with the dynamic/table-backed enum.)*

## §9 supplement — ordinary-iteration negative admission is level- and OPERAND-sensitive, and the host conversion is the separator (probed 2026-07-25; a12-kernel-lean SPEC-2026-07-24-02)

> Fold: folded → §9.

**`MVK_NEG_CONDITION_IN_ITERATION` is not a fixed list of "negative" operators — admission is decided per ordinary iteration level, and for a direct Number field against an immediate literal it turns on the literal's HOST-CONVERTED integer form.** The kernel's `IterationNotValidVisitor` enumerates the relevant levels and applies connective rules (`And` needs one child that both references and is admitted at that level; `Or` needs every child to), then dispatches a per-operator/per-operand leaf test. `FeldOperationUtil` supplies the literal conversion: `Double.parseDouble` → `Math.round` → `(int)`. Measured against the real kernel (`rule check`, the accept/reject oracle) on a repeatable `Items` group:

| condition (rule group `/Order/Items`) | admission | why |
|---|---|---|
| `[Count] == 0`, `>= 0`, `<= 0` — and `0 == [Count]`, `0 >= …`, `0 <= …` | **REJECTED** | converted literal is zero; symmetric in operand order |
| `[Count] > 0`, `< 0`, `!= 0` | admitted | strict comparison / ordinary `!=` |
| `[Count] == 5`, `>= 5`, `<= 5`, `5 == [Count]` | admitted | converted-nonzero literal |
| `[UnitWeight] == 0.40`, `== 0.49`, `0.40 == [UnitWeight]` | **REJECTED** | `Math.round` → 0 |
| `[UnitWeight] == 0.50`, `0.50 == …`, `== 0.60`, `== 1.00` | admitted | `Math.round(0.5)` → **1** |
| `[UnitWeight] == -0.50`, `== -0.40` | **REJECTED** | `Math.round(-0.5)` → **0** — half-up toward +∞ |
| `Abs([Count]) == 0` · `MinValue(Count, UnitWeight) == 0` · `MaxValue(…) == 0` · `Length(Sku) == 0` | **REJECTED** | direct field *operations* keep the zero-sensitive partition |
| `Length(Sku) > 0`, `== 3` | admitted | strict / nonzero, same partition |
| **`[Count] + 0 == 0`** | **admitted** | top-level arithmetic is an `ICompositeOperation` — no direct-operand branch |

**The ±0.5 pair is the sharpest control**: `0.50` admitted and `-0.50` rejected pins `Math.round`'s half-up-toward-+∞ semantics, ruling out HALF_EVEN (0 for both) and away-from-zero (1 for both). **The composite separator is the second**: wrapping a field in arithmetic flips static admission while leaving runtime arithmetic identical — so "flatten arithmetic to its field references" is a wrong model of this classifier.

**Scope of what is locked here (deliberately partial).** `RuleGroupIterationLawsTest` covers the direct-Number partition, both operand orders, the conversion boundary including the negative-half asymmetry, direct `Abs`/`MinValue`/`MaxValue`/`Length`, and the composite separator. **Not yet locked** from SPEC-2026-07-24-02's full matrix: `RangeAsNumber`/`FieldValueAsNumber` scope+runtime controls, temporal component extraction, the ordered two-operand `DifferenceIn{Months,Years,Days,Hours,Minutes,Seconds}` family, entity-list/aggregate partitions (`FirstFilledValue`, distinct/value count, `NumberOfValueInFields`, `SumOfProducts`), mixed-level any-literal rejection, and the filter-reference exclusion. rulekit **delegates** the visitor to the kernel (no reimplementation), so these are boundary locks, not a ported classifier.

**Correction (2026-07-25): the `(int)` narrowing is NOT unrepresentable — it is authorable on any scale-0 field, and is now locked.** This entry previously claimed the `2^32` wrap and long saturation "need a literal the scale gate (`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES`) or the grammar rejects first". That was inference, and it is wrong: the dec-places gate compares the literal's *fractional* digits against the field's scale, so on a **scale-0** field a bare integer literal matches trivially and reaches the partition, where it is judged on its **narrowed** value.

| condition (`Count` is scale-0) | admission | why |
|---|---|---|
| `[Count] == 4294967296` (2^32) | **REJECTED** | `(int)` narrows to **0** — a literal of 4,294,967,296 classified as zero |
| `[Count] == 4294967297` (2^32+1) | admitted | narrows to 1 |
| `[Count] == 9223372036854775808` (2^63) | admitted | `Math.round` saturates at `Long.MAX_VALUE`, narrowing to −1 |

**The ±1 pair straddling 2^32 is the sharpest control of the three separators**, and better than the ±0.5 pair: two literals differing by one, both astronomically far from zero, land on opposite sides of the whole partition. A widening or saturating conversion admits both, so the pair pins 32-bit truncation specifically *(locked: `RuleGroupIterationLawsTest.theThirtyTwoBitNarrowingIsObservable`)*.

## §9 supplement — the host conversion's PARSE step lifts a below-half decimal onto one half, and the witness IS authorable via the suppressible dec-places gate (measured 2026-07-25; answers a12-kernel-lean SPEC-2026-07-25-02)

> Fold: folded → §9.

**The conversion `Double.parseDouble` → `Math.round` → `(int)` has TWO rounding steps, and the first one is the surprising one.** `Math.round` does not lift a double strictly below one half (that was Java ≤ 6's `floor(x + 0.5)`, fixed by JDK-6430675 in Java 7 — on our JDK 21 pin `0.49999999999999994` converts to **0**). But **`Double.parseDouble` can lift a below-half *decimal* onto `0.5` before `Math.round` ever sees it**: binary64 round-to-nearest-**even** maps every decimal in `[½ − 2⁻⁵⁵, ½)` onto exactly `0.5`, because that interval's values are ties between `0.5` (even significand) and `0.5 − 2⁻⁵⁴` (odd). Measured through the exact chain:

| decimal literal | `parseDouble` | `→ int` |
|---|---|---|
| `0.49999999999999994` = ½ − 2⁻⁵⁴ | `0.49999999999999994` | 0 |
| `0.49999999999999998` (**17 fractional digits — the shortest lifting decimal**) | **`0.5`** | **1** |
| ½ − 2⁻⁵⁵ exactly (55 fractional digits) | **`0.5`** | **1** |
| one ulp below the interval | `0.4999999999999999` | 0 |
| −(½ − 2⁻⁵⁵) | `-0.5` | **0** |

**The lift creates a separator only on the positive side**, because `+0.5` is where `Math.round`'s boundary is inclusive; on the negative side a lifted `-0.5` still converts to 0, same as any smaller magnitude — so the ±0.5 asymmetry survives the parse step rather than being softened by it.

**Reaching it needs 17 fractional digits, which ordinarily exceeds what a field can be compared against — but that gate is SUPPRESSIBLE, so the witness is authorable.** Three links, all locked: (1) the shortest lifting decimal needs **17** fractional digits (at ≤16 the interval holds no decimal strictly below ½ — the ceiling lands on `0.5` itself); (2) a Number field's `maxFractionalDigits` **caps at 14** — the kernel rejects 15 at model check with a plain, MVK-less message ("Only values smaller than or equal to 14 are allowed") *(`aNumberFieldScaleCapsAtFourteen`)*; (3) the comparison gate allows the literal **at most** the field's scale, rejecting 17-against-14 as `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` *(`aComparisonLiteralMayCarryAtMostTheFieldsScale`)* — **but `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` is the kernel's ONE suppressible warning** (`@SuppressWarning(...)`, the CLI's `allowDifferingDecimals`; GRAMMAR-LEDGER `preamble` row, `DirectiveLawsTest`), so link (3) is defeatable by a first-class directive.

With the gate suppressed against a scale-14 field, two 17-digit literals differing only in their final digit straddle the entire partition — both strictly below one half:

| condition (scale-14 field, gate suppressed) | admission | why |
|---|---|---|
| `[W] == 0.49999999999999998` | admitted | inside `[½ − 2⁻⁵⁵, ½)` → parses onto `0.5` → converts to **1** |
| `[W] == 0.49999999999999990` | **REJECTED** | just below the interval → converts to **0** |

**So the separator is a real language property, not a host-only detail** *(locked: `RuleGroupIterationLawsTest.theParseStepLiftIsAuthorableWithTheGateSuppressed`; `.theZeroSensitivePartitionStillHoldsAtTheMaximumScale` is the control that the partition runs at scale 14 at all)*. A reimplementer that models the classifier with exact decimals diverges on a legal model.

**Correction of this entry's own first version (same day).** It originally claimed the witness was "unauthorable on every kernel-legal model, by exactly three decimal places", and was filed `> Fold: not-foldable` on that basis. The three links were each measured, but the proof was never tested for a *defeater* — and the defeater was a documented directive already exposed in our own CLI and schema. Measuring every link of a chain does not establish the chain; it has to be attacked.

**Two traps this closed, both from reading a message instead of measuring.** The `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES` text says the two digit counts "are different", which reads as an equality requirement — it is **at most**: a *shorter* literal is admitted and reaches the partition normally (`[W] == 0.5` on a scale-14 field is accepted; `== 0.4` is rejected as converted-zero). And `FeldOperationUtil.getZahlKonstanteWert` returns **−1 as its "not a number constant" sentinel**, which collides with the value a saturating 2^63 literal converts to; for *this* classifier the collision is unobservable (both are nonzero ⇒ admitted), and whether any other caller distinguishes them is untested — **hypothesis, not a finding**.

## §11 supplement — the parallel-iteration computation route replaces the read-time index gate with post-loop marking, whose blast radius depends on WHICH index group is malformed (differential, 2026-07-25; answers a12-kernel-lean EXP-2026-07-25-03)

> Fold: pending — the route split needs a deliberate edit to the §11 computation prose.

**A computation reached through a parallel-iteration operand has no read-time semantic-index gate. Instead the iteration-values lookup returns empty (so the loop body never runs) and `CalculationCache.markFieldsUsingInvalidIndexGroupsAsIncorrectlyCalculated` marks affected instances afterwards — and it marks by TRUNCATED coordinates, so an off-path malformed key clears clean sibling rows while an on-path one does not.** `CodeGenCalculatedField.getParallelIterationGroupsCode()` passes **both** the index group on the computed field's own path and the off-path groups the operands iterate; marking then keys on each existing field instance's indices **truncated to `numSharedLevels` = `commonAncestorLevel(computedField, indexGroupParent) + 1`**. So the blast radius is the shared prefix, not the malformed row:

| malformed index key lives in | shared prefix reaches | sibling parent row's computed target |
|---|---|---|
| the **off-path** iterated index group (`/Order/Refs`) | only `/Order` — above the sibling level | **CLEARED** — a clean sibling does *not* survive |
| the computed field's **own on-path** index group (`/Order/Lines/Slots`) | `/Order/Lines` — below the sibling level | **survives** with its value |

Observed on a two-sibling-row document: with all keys distinct both targets compute (100 / 200); duplicating a key in the off-path group clears **both**, including the sibling whose own slot key still matches a clean row; duplicating a key inside one line's own `Slots` clears only that line's instances and leaves the sibling at 200. **Both kernel strategies agree on all three, so this is kernel truth, not a strategy split.** The kernel's own comment concedes the off-path case marks "more field instances as invalid than strictly necessary … we don't think we need to get too clever."

**Authoring preconditions (and why no corpus reaches this route):** the iterated group's index field must have the **same name** as the index field on the computed field's path, else the model is rejected with `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_INVALID_INDEX` ("iteration is specified, but the index field on the error field path has another name; therefore wildcard has to be specified or another error field must be defined"). The operand must also be a bracketed **non-starred** reference (`[../../Refs/Amount]`) — a starred `Refs*` is a *wildcard*, not an iteration, and takes the ordinary route; `Sum(...)` over a single such reference is rejected `MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALIDN`.

**The two loci are NOT separately reachable — one model must carry both (a12-kernel-lean's marker derivation, 2026-07-25; their reading, our fixture is the evidence it is right).** The bean's level is `-1` unless **two independent facts hold**: **(A)** a `PARALLEL_ITERATION` marker exists in the computation's own operand trees, and **(B)** the computed field has an index-field-bearing group on its own ancestry (`getIndexFieldLevel`, `-1` if none). Neither implies the other. The marker itself is stamped in two passes (`ParserService`), and **pass two — the only producer of ON-PATH markers — runs only if pass one stamped an OFF-PATH one.** So an on-path index group can never be parallel-iterated unless an off-path iterated group exists in the same computation: both loci are present together or neither is. **Consequence for any experiment here: use ONE document containing both and move the malformed key**, never two structurally different documents, which would compare shapes rather than loci. That is why the three cases below are comparable at all.

**Two caveats, both scoped deliberately.** *(1)* The stamping call carries the kernel authors' own TODO — *"is this safe/applicable for operations or condition snippets at all?"* — so the mechanism above is a settled reading of **how** it behaves, not evidence the behavior is intended. *(2)* Four further checks reject parallel iteration outright — with `RepetitionNotUnique`, with a filtered iterated entity, with a filter condition that itself parallel-iterates, and with a semantic index inside a filter (`MVK_PARALLEL_ITERATION_NOT_ALLOWED` / `…_FOR_FILTERED_ENTITY_NOT_ALLOWED` / `MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_NOT_ALLOWED`) — but all four sit in `checkNormalRule(IRule, …)`, so they are verified for the **rule** path only. Whether an equivalent gate exists on the computation path is **unverified**; do not assume it from these.

*(Locked: `adapter.laws.ParallelIterationIndexMarkingDiffTest` — all three cases assert dynamic-Groovy and static-Java. Our interpreter initially diverged on the on-path case, clearing the sibling the kernel keeps — one coordinate-blind root with a second reproduced symptom (the same key under two parents read as a duplicate); fixed as [IF222](INTERPRETER-FINDINGS.md), and all four cases now assert all three engines.)*

## §5 supplement — the value-list quantifiers have TWO emission routes, and the fields-presence prepass belongs to only one (source, 2026-07-25; a12-kernel-lean SPEC-2026-07-25-01)

> Fold: folded → §5.

The canonical clause now states the route-independent contract and demotes the prepass; this entry keeps the mechanism, the route predicate, and the equivalence argument.

**`No`/`AtLeastOne`/`NotAll` over a FIELD-list values side are emitted by one of two routes, and the `NotAll` fields-presence prepass exists only on the ordinary one — so it is an optimization detail, never the observable contract.** `EntityListAndValueConditionFieldList.getBedingungNameCode()` picks the runtime method by suffix — `"FL"` (ordinary) or `"OL"` (optimized) — and `shouldBeOptimized()` returns true only when **all** hold: the condition is one of the three quantifiers (`AtLeastOneDateRangeOverlaps` is excluded outright); the **fields** side has an operand with `getNumberOfIterations(...) >= OPTIMIZATION_BOUND_FOR_ITERATION` (**10**); the **values** side has no filter condition and no iteration; and the values side has an operand with `getLengthAsList(...) >= OPTIMIZATION_BOUND_FOR_VALUE_LIST_LENGTH` (**20**). *(The inline comment above that last check says "at least 50 repetitions" and is stale against its own constants.)*

The routes differ exactly as follows — `nichtAlleFeldWerteInWerteListeFL` opens with `if (noEntityFilled(index, entityList)) return FALSE_OR_UNKNOWN;` (**the prepass**), *then* builds the values set, then delegates to the shared `…OLIntern`; `nichtAlleFeldWerteInWerteListeOL` has **no** prepass and goes straight to `…OLIntern`, because its values set, `foundNonFilledField`, and `foundNonRelevantFieldInValueList` are computed **ahead of the whole condition** by `InitEntityListAndValueListConditionParam.st`. **They are nonetheless observationally equivalent:** the membership test counts only cells marked as given, so with no present fields cell the un-prepassed route collapses to the same non-firing result. The prepass is a short-circuit, not a semantic gate.

**The numeric-kind residue is closed — the two routes cannot disagree (settled by reading, the open question in SPEC-2026-07-25-01).** They do source `isNumber` from *different sides*: FL calls `isZahlFeld(entityList.get(0).getUniqueName())` — the first **fields** operand — while the OL init template emits `controller.isZahlFeld(<valueList>.get(0).getUniqueName())` — the first **values** operand (so it is a hoisted runtime probe, not a codegen constant). A mixed-kind condition would separate them, but authoring forbids one: `CheckFeldListeUndWerteListenBedingungImpl` takes `expectedBaseType` from the first *fields* operand and calls `matchBaseType(values, …, expectedBaseType, …)`, on top of `checkSameBaseType(fields, …)` — so **every** operand on both sides shares one base type (String / Enum / Number-Money, with predefined types and extensible enums counted as String). Base-type agreement is strictly finer than the number-vs-not question `isZahlFeld` asks, so the two probes always agree.

**Coverage status — the OL route is now EXERCISED and agrees (2026-07-25, closing the coverage gap rather than documenting it).** No corpus fixture reaches either threshold, so `adapter.laws.ValueListOptimizedRouteDiffTest` authors the shape: **the fields side must be NON-STARRED** (a star is a WILDCARD, contributing to `getLengthAsList` and *not* to `getNumberOfIterations`), which makes the rule an ordinary iterating rule and therefore also requires a positive per-row guard against `MVK_NEG_CONDITION_IN_ITERATION` — that interaction is why the shape is near-impossible to reach by accident. It **proves route engagement from the emitted source** (`…WerteListeOL(` at/above the thresholds, `…WerteListeFL(` below, for both quantifier names) and then confirms the two routes agree on all three engines across the prepass case (an instantiated row with an empty code), a no-row document, a firing row, a matching row, and a wholly-empty values side. **No divergence** — the equivalence argument above holds observationally, not just by construction. *(`ValueListQuantifierOrderDiffTest` still cannot separate prepass from no-prepass — both accounts predict its outcomes — and its header says so.)*

## §8 supplement — table-backed (dynamic) enums are NOT DM-JSON-serializable ⇒ `ValueNotConsistent` is un-buildable (2026-06-08)

> Fold: not-foldable — a serialization/infrastructure limit (what a self-contained DM-JSON can express) plus a codegen capability gate (which strategy may lower it), both outside the evaluation scope.

`ValueNotConsistent(enumField <toOp> col1, col2, …)` (DE `WertNichtKonsistent`) checks a row's dependent columns against a **table-backed** enumeration. The checker (`CheckFieldAndValueListConditionImpl`) requires its first operand to be an enum field **with a table reference** (`IEnumerationType.getTableName()`/`getDependentColumns()` present), and verifies the dependent-param arity against the column count (value checks are skipped when the runtime data table is absent).

**But that field type cannot be expressed in a document model's DM-JSON.** The public a12internal `EnumerationType` DTO declares exactly **6 properties** — `values`, `annotations`, `categories`, `sValue`, `errorMessage`, `alphabeticalSorting` — and the deserializer is **strict**: injecting `tableName`/`columnName`/`dependentColumns` raises `UnrecognizedPropertyException` (*"not marked as ignorable"*, probed 2026-06-08). The table backing is materialized at **runtime** from a separate enumeration/table catalog, not from the field's type JSON. **Which `IEnumerationType` matters here (the same-name trap the golden rule warns about):** the accessors exist only on the tool-internal **ADO** `…core.tool.a12internal.api.ado.IEnumerationType` (`getTableName()`/`getDependentColumns()`), and they return empty Optionals when the model came from a plain document model; the **md model API** (`…md.model.api`, what `dm.core`/`ModelIndex` import) has **no `tableName` notion at all** — zero occurrences in `kernel-md-model-api`. Outside the md tier, `tableName` appears in `kernel-core-extmpspecifics` (the extmp/legacy-XML front-end) and the CSV table catalog (`enumTable`/`CSVTableChecker`) — so the construct is reachable through *that* front-end, not through a document model.

**Consequence:** no self-contained document model can mark a field table-backed, so the checker's precondition can never be met, so rulekit's "author DM-JSON → `checkConsistency`" oracle **cannot author or verify** a `ValueNotConsistent` rule. It is **un-buildable within single-document-model scope** — same root cause as multi-form/Vordruck (needs infrastructure outside the document model), and it makes the would-be "dynamic-enum modelkit authoring" moot (there is nothing in DM-JSON to emit). Stays a tolerant `ReadResult.Opaque` if ever encountered. *(Demonstrated by probe; not locked by a test — there is no expressible fixture to lock.)*

**Even where a table enum IS reachable, only static-Java can lower it — so the normative oracle structurally cannot observe `ValueNotConsistent` (source, 2026-07-25).** Three facts compose: **(1)** the grammar's mixed value-list production has exactly one condition name — `fieldAndValueListConditionName: 'WertNichtKonsistent'` — and its sole creator (`FieldAndValueListConditionCreator`) *always* populates the mixed list (`new EntityListAndValueListConditionImpl(name, ids, null, null, mixedList)`), so **every** parsed `ValueNotConsistent` becomes the `EntityListAndValueConditionMixedList` codegen bean (the string/field-list beans belong to the sibling `feldListeUndWerteListenBedingung` family instead); **(2)** codegen resolves a template by `getClass().getSimpleName()` within the dialect's own dir (`STParameterClassBase`), and `conditionsDir/EntityListAndValueConditionMixedList.st` **exists only under `validation/java`** — as does `operationsDir/ConsistentValueOp.st` (the dynamic-enum value op, emitting `controller.getUiXmlValuesForTableEnum(…)` via `CodeGenDynamicEnumerationBuilder`); **(3)** this is not an accidental gap but a **declared, diagnosed restriction** — `NoMetaModelChecks.checkEnumTables()` rejects any table-enum field for `GROOVY` and `JAVA_SCRIPT_VK` with `MVK_TABLE_ENUMS_NOT_ALLOWED_FOR_PROGRAMMING_LANGUAGE`. **Consequence — and the correction that matters (a12-kernel-lean, 2026-07-25; verified here): the region has NO witness on ANY dialect, so "static-Java is the only witness" was too generous.** The ADO accessor `IEnumerationType.getTableName()` is abstract and its **sole implementor** — `kernel-md-model`'s `EnumerationTypeImpl` — returns `Optional.empty()` unconditionally (as does `getColumnName()`), and the md public API has no table concept at all. So `getTableName().isPresent()` is never true: the Groovy/JS rejection `MVK_TABLE_ENUMS_NOT_ALLOWED_FOR_PROGRAMMING_LANGUAGE` can never fire, the java-only `ConsistentValueOp`/`MixedList` templates are never instantiated, and `RuntimeController.wertNichtKonsistentML` is unreachable. The static-Java lowering *capability* is real but **dead**; there is no witness to promote, so no doctrine exception is needed — the region is simply unreachable at 30.8.1 (a12-kernel-lean reports the same at 31.1.0, and voided its EXP-2026-07-25-02 on that basis rather than conceding a region). The front-end-reach scoping above stands unchanged; only the witness claim needed narrowing. *(Source-read; the template asymmetry was the candidate and the check code + error code is the independent confirmation — the template-layer routing rule, DOC-DISCIPLINE §1. Not probe-locked: unreachable from any authorable fixture.)*

## Header `modelVersion` compatibility — same-major and not-newer than the kernel's model-format (probed 2026-06-12)

> Fold: not-foldable — a model-header compatibility policy (structure/versioning), outside the evaluation scope.

The kernel polices a document's `header.modelVersion` during `checkConsistency` (not at parse): a mismatch surfaces as a **`KERNEL` ERROR** — `"Version mismatch: Document model version is <X> and application version is <28.4.0>"` — so the model is reported `valid:false` (it loads enough to validate; this is a consistency diagnostic, not a load crash). The "application version" is the **model-format version the running kernel implements** (`30.8.1` implements `28.4.0`). Probed band (against `order-ruled`, app-format `28.4.0`):

| `header.modelVersion` | verdict | rule |
|---|---|---|
| `28.4.0` | valid | exact |
| `28.0.0` | **valid** | same major, **≤** app-format → backward-compatible |
| `28.4.1`, `28.5.0`, `28.9.0` | invalid | same major but **newer** than app-format |
| `27.0.0`, `29.0.0`, `30.8.1` | invalid | different major |

So the kernel is **tolerant of an older same-major model and fail-fast on a newer/different-major one**, with a clear message — exactly the posture we want, enforced upstream. rulekit only *classifies* this verdict (it does not re-derive the rule): the un-coded kernel ERROR is enriched to **`RK_MODEL_VERSION_INCOMPATIBLE`** (+ a fix), and an accepted-but-off-reference model gets a tolerant INFO **`RK_MODEL_VERSION_SKEW`**. The reference is `core.ModelFormat.VERSION` (`28.4.0`), bumped in lockstep with the kernel pin (KERNEL-UPGRADE). *(Locked: `cli.ModelVersionPolicyTest` — the band's tolerant/exact/fail-fast cases + the message pattern via `validate.ModelVersion.isVersionMismatch`; the no-implicit-bump round-trip is in the same test.)*

## §1/§3 supplement — value-vs-omission typing is DIRECTIONAL and sign-aware (source + runtime, 2026-06-23)

> Fold: folded → §12.

A fired comparison is an OMISSION error (`TRUE_AF`) only when filling an operand **in the breaking direction** could clear it (`BedingungsOperatorHelper.vergleiche`): `GT`/`GE` → `left.kannKleinerWerden || right.kannGroesserWerden`; `LT`/`LE` → the mirror; `EQ` → any operand can move; `NE` → directional on which side is larger (`getFehlerFuerNE`). The per-operand fillability of an **empty number** is asymmetric: `kannGroesserWerden = true` (always fillable larger) but **`kannKleinerWerden = hatVorzeichen`** — fillable smaller only if the field is **signed** (`RuntimeController.feldWertAlsZahl`, TS twin `BigDecimalUtils.makeDecimalFromNonEmptyString`).

- **The sign trigger is `NumberType.positivesOnly`, NOT `minValue`** (the trap): a field with `minValue:1` is still *signed* (probed — `[BirthDay] >= 0` empty types OMISSION); only `positivesOnly:true` makes it unsigned (`[StockOnHand:positivesOnly] >= 0` empty types **VALUE**). `minValue` is a range constraint, independent of `hatVorzeichen`.
- Consequence: `[Unsigned] >= 0` (empty) is a **VALUE** error — an unsigned 0 can't shrink, so no fill breaks `>= 0`; the same over a *signed* field is **OMISSION**. Both `<= X` are OMISSION (filling larger breaks them).

*(Locked: `adapter.laws.DirectionalPolarityDiffTest` (unsigned `StockOnHand` vs signed `Quantity`); the signed cases also in `adapter.laws.PolarityDiffTest`/`AggPolarityDiffTest`; the **`NE` directional arm** (`getFehlerFuerNE` — previously documented here but never `!=`-directionally DiffTest-exercised) is **corpus-locked** since 2026-07-17 by the portable `empty-polarity` cases (signedness + literal-direction + filled-state pairs, tri-engine replay incl. the JS oracle). The interpreter's directional treatment: INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF9.)*

**Function-operand fillability — `CurrentRepetition` and `FirstFilledValue` (probe + full-message fuzz, 2026-06-24).** The directional fillability above extends to value FUNCTIONS, not just fields/aggregates — and the naive "any empty/short star tail ⇒ fillable" over-reports OMISSION for two:

- **`CurrentRepetition(group)`** is the structural **row index**; no field-fill changes it, so a fired comparison against it is **always VALUE** (probed: `CurrentRepetition(Lines) > 2` on row 3 → VALUE). The generic fallback wrongly reads its group argument as an "unfilled field" and types it OMISSION.
- **`FirstFilledValue(g*/f)`** is **OMISSION iff NO row is filled** (the empty selection folds to the kind's fillable not-given value — the 2026-07-08 refinement below) **or an instantiated EMPTY row PRECEDES the first filled one** — filling that (earlier / any) row could become the new first value and clear the error — else **VALUE** (the first value is fixed; *later* empties are irrelevant). Probed: `(empty, 60)` → OMISSION; `(60, empty)` and `(60)` → VALUE.

The naive star-omission notion (`starOmission`: any empty cell / short tail) is too broad for `FirstFilledValue` — only an empty/filter marker reached before the terminal value matters. Both were mis-typed OMISSION until the **harness-D full-message fuzz** caught them (117/200 seeds — invisible to the codes-only gate). *(Locked: `adapter.laws.FullMessageFuzzDiffTest` + `CurrentRepetitionDiffTest`/`FirstFilledValueDiffTest`/`FirstFilledValueFilterEncounterDiffTest`, now asserting polarity, not just firing; interpreter `firstFilledScan` + `funcFill`.)*

**A no-row first star DOES taint a later operand's value — the source-level "omitted tail" clause does not manifest at runtime (differential vs. dynamic-Groovy, 2026-07-22).** In a multi-operand `FirstFilledValue(star, …, fallback)`, a first `star` that visits **no concrete cell** — an uninstantiated group (flat `Parcels*/Weight` with zero rows), or an instantiated parent with an empty child repetition (nested `Parcels*/Segments*/Load` where a parcel row has zero segments) — reads as a **not-given prefix**: `RuntimeController.combineFeldListe` reaches the star's repetitions and `addValue`s a not-given cell, so `FirstValueCombiner.notGivenFieldBeforeFirstGiven` is set and a fired later-fallback comparison types **OMISSION**, not VALUE. Reading `FirstValueCombiner.getValue` alone predicts the opposite — its `(value == null) && omittedTail` clause would let an omitted tail *not* taint a selected value — but that clause only decides the **all-exhausted identity**; whenever the star is actually reached, the not-given cell taints first, independent of `omittedTail`. The interpreter's `firstFilledScan` marks the no-row star fillable (`if (!visitedRow) fillable = true`), matching the kernel. This **refutes** a12-kernel-lean **SPEC-2026-07-22-06** (which requested the interpreter type such a firing VALUE); an empirical sweep found no document shape where dynamic-Groovy yields VALUE for a fallback selected after a no-row first star, and applying the requested change makes the interpreter diverge. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.FirstFilledValueOmittedTailDiffTest` — flat + nested no-row star, instantiated-empty prefix, and a star-selects-value VALUE control, all matching the kernel.)*

**`FirstFilledValue` over an EMPTY selection returns its operand kind's OWN not-given value — number → the fillable 0, else NOT-GIVEN (source + differential 2026-07-08).** The kernel's `FirstValueCombiner` is a per-kind family — `FirstValueCombinerNumber`/`Date`/`DateRange`/`String` — and on an empty selection (no filled cell) each returns its own `notGivenValue()`: `NICHT_ANGEGEBENE_ZAHL` for NUMBER (a *value*, the fillable `0` — the empty-as-0 its Sum/Min/Max siblings give), but `NOT_GIVEN_DATE`/`NOT_GIVEN_DATE_RANGE`/`NICHT_ANGEGEBENER_STRING` for the others (NO value → not-given). Observable: a DATE/STRING `FirstFilledValue(empty)` in a comparison does **not** fire (`vergleiche…` on a not-given side), and a computation into a DATE/STRING target is **CLEARED**; only a NUMBER empty `FirstFilledValue` yields the fillable 0. So the kind-following FirstFilledValue's empty identity is NOT uniformly a number (the sibling of the §6 min/max `DateCombiner` NULL-date identity — a numeric identity where a date/string is expected is an interpreter-only type mixture no legal kernel input produces). *(Interpreter: `firstFilledValue`'s empty identity is `Val.Num(0)` iff the operand kind is NUMBER, else `Val.Unknown`; before the fix an unconditional `Num(0)` crashed `ValCompare` against the real value. Locked `adapter.laws.FirstFilledValueKindDiffTest`, INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF83.)*

**Fill propagates through ARITHMETIC — an empty operand keeps the result fillable (source `VkBigDecimal`, differential 2026-07-07; aggregate correction 2026-07-21).** The directional fill (`kannGroesserWerden`/`kannKleinerWerden`) is a property of the *value*, and every arithmetic operation carries it forward, so `[F] != [A] + [B]` with `B` an empty (fillable) field types the fired comparison **OMISSION** (the RHS can still grow) — not VALUE. Each op propagates differently (`VkBigDecimal.add`/`subtract`/`multiply`/`divide`/`power`): `+` combines both directions; `−` **flips the subtrahend** (`a−b` grows if `a` grows or `b` shrinks); `×`/`÷` are **SIGN-DEPENDENT** (`produktKannGroesser/KleinerWerden` — a factor pushes the product up by growing when the other factor is positive / shrinking when it is negative; `÷` is `a × (1/b)`); `^` follows `powCanChange` (base magnitude vs 0/1/−1 and exponent parity). This rides the SAME runtime `VkBigDecimal` in both strategies (codegen emits calls to it) ⟹ codegen-parity. **The VALUE FUNCTIONS carry the flags too**, each via its own `VkBigDecimal` op: `setScale` (the `Round*` family) **PRESERVES** them exactly; `abs` **TRANSFORMS** them with the value sign (`g'=g∨s`, `s'=(pos∧s)∨(neg∧g)` — `|x|` grows off either direction, shrinks only toward 0); `min`/`max` (operand-list `Min`/`Max`) **COMBINE** directionally (min shrinks via either operand, grows via the smaller; max mirrors); `bereichAlsZahl` (`RangeAsNumber`) is **GROW-ONLY** (`kannKleiner=false`, value ≥ 0). So `RoundAccounting(<fillable arith>, n)` / `AbsValue(…)` / `Min(…)` / `RangeAsNumber(…)` are likewise fillable. **NUMBER aggregate fill has two stages** (`NumberCombiner`): before any present value, `Sum`/`MaxValue`/`MinValue` expose the zero identity with both directions; afterward, `Sum` grows and shrinks exactly when a missing contributing declaration is signed, `MaxValue` is grow-only, and `MinValue` shrink-only. The earlier unsigned-all-empty VALUE claim conflated those stages and is superseded. *(Locked: `AggDirectionalPolarityDiffTest` + `NumericAggregatePolarityDiffTest`; interpreter `funcFill`, INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF9/IF200.)* *(Interpreter treatment IF69 for arithmetic + IF71 for the value functions: `ArithFill` + `Interpreter.fill(Expr.Arith)`/`funcFill`; differential-locked `adapter.laws.ArithmeticFillPolarityDiffTest`/`FunctionFillPolarityDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter.ArithmeticFillTest`/`FunctionFillTest`.)*

**Arithmetic fill is executable, not merely “sign-dependent/parity” (source-completed + dual-route discriminator 2026-07-20).** With `(g,s)` = can-grow/can-shrink, addition is `(a.g∨b.g, a.s∨b.s)` and subtraction is `(a.g∨b.s, a.s∨b.g)`. Multiplication grow is `(a.g∧b.g) ∨ (a.s∧b.s) ∨ (b>0∧a.g) ∨ (b<0∧a.s) ∨ (a>0∧b.g) ∨ (a<0∧b.s)`; shrink is `(a.g∧b.s) ∨ (a.s∧b.g) ∨ (b>0∧a.s) ∨ (b<0∧a.g) ∨ (a>0∧b.s) ∨ (a<0∧b.g)`. The joint terms survive when both current signs are zero. Division first rejects current zero, then multiplies by reciprocal fill `(b.s∨(b<0∧b.g), b.g∨(b>0∧b.s))`. Power is deliberately conservative: negative exponents recurse through reciprocal-first staging; fixed exponent, fixed base, and both-variable branches split around exponent zero, base `−1/0/1`, and parity; only the both-variable `base>1`/neither-shrink case is grow-only, otherwise it may return both directions. This is policy rather than exact reachable-set analysis: with the same `0^0` value, `0 ^ empty-unsigned > 0` fires OMISSION while `0 ^ empty-signed > 0` fires VALUE in both kernel strategies. Quiet-invalid power domains never reach comparison typing as an ordinary fixed value, and fractional exponents are rejected during authoring. *(Locked: `ArithmeticFillPolarityDiffTest` zero-base tri-check + multiplatform `ArithmeticFillBranchTest` full branch matrix.)*

## §5 supplement — tolerance boundary + comparison rescaling (runtime, 2026-06-10, RT-semantics drain)

> Fold: folded → §5.

- **`DiffersWithToleranceRange{N}` fires strictly beyond the tolerance**: satisfied iff `|R₁₉(a) − R₁₉(b)| > N` after independently normalizing both operands — a difference of *exactly* N is still within tolerance and does **not** fire, in either direction. The boundary and normalization order are the traps. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.ToleranceComparisonLawsTest` + `ToleranceExactnessDiffTest`.)*
- **Source-read precision policy** (`VkBigDecimal`): a NUMBER **comparison** rescales both operands to scale 19 `HALF_UP` before comparing — and this is **`BedingungsOperatorHelper.vergleiche` (lines 164-167), not `VkBigDecimal.compareTo`** (which is plain): so the rescale applies to **all** comparison ops `<`/`>`/`<=`/`>=`/`==`/`!=`, but **NOT to `min`/`max` selection**, which the kernel does at full precision via `VkBigDecimal.compareTo` (`SummeCombiner`/`MinWertCombiner` etc.). Separately, **every arithmetic operation `+`/`−`/`×`/`÷`/`^` rounds to `MathContext(50)` `HALF_UP`** (`INTERNAL_PRECISION`). `Sum` therefore starts at zero and left-folds the resolved encounter stream, rounding after every present addition; model declaration order for group expansion and row order for repetitions are semantic, so sorting, reassociation, exact accumulation followed by one rounding, and parallel reduction are invalid. Missing contributors perform no arithmetic step but retain their own declaration for polarity. The kernel team's note was "no legal model surfaces the scale-19 rounding (field scales cap far below 19)" — but arithmetic expressions can exceed it, so the rounding is observable. **Interpreter status: reproduced in full.** The comparison scale-19 rounding rides `Dec.scale19` (comparison ops only — `min`/`max` stay full-precision), every `Dec` arithmetic operation takes the same context on JVM and Node, and aggregate group expansion preserves declaration order. `DecimalPrecisionParityDiffTest` tri-checks observable addition, subtraction, division, positive-power, and reciprocal-first negative-power discriminators against Groovy-dynamic and generated Java; `DecimalHostParityTest` and `NumericAggregateSemanticsTest` lock the exact primitive staged/order discriminators on JVM and Node. *(Also locked: `PrecisionDiffTest` — scale-19 comparison; `ArithmeticDiffTest` — multiplication; `DivPowDiffTest` — operator-domain behavior.)*
- **The rounding operators are directional, not truncation** (`BigDecimalUtils`, second drain): after the shared scale-19 `HALF_UP` pre-round, `RoundDown` is **FLOOR** (toward −∞: `RoundDown(−1.4) = −2`), `RoundUp` is **CEILING** (`RoundUp(−1.4) = −1`), and `RoundAccounting` is **HALF_UP** (away from zero on the .5 boundary, both signs). The negative-value behavior is the modeller trap — "down" does not mean "toward zero".
- **Exactly one ordered post-order arithmetic rewrite precedes evaluation.** After visiting its children, each original multiplication pulls only immediate division-root operands. Ordinary factors remain first in authored order; extracted numerators and denominators follow in division encounter order; multi-factor products are left folds; newly created products are not revisited. `a*{b/c}` and `{b/c}*a` both become `{a*b}/c`, but `x*{{a/b}/c}` becomes `{x*{a/b}}/c` and stops.
