**…and it makes an AGGREGATE / COUNT over a starred index field NON-EVALUABLE too (differential + source, 2026-07-08).** An index field's not-check-relevant cell — mandatory-empty OR duplicated — suppresses an aggregate/count over `Group* / indexField` exactly as a malformed cell does: the shared loop `RuntimeController.combineFeldListe` (and the count `anzahlAngegebenerFelder`) returns `NICHT_PRUEF_REL_ZAHL` on the first such cell; the fill quantifiers count it in neither bucket. Both strategies share these runtime methods ⟹ codegen-parity. An index field can be numeric (a `Sum` over one is authorable). *(IF86; locked: `adapter.laws.IndexAggregateDiffTest` + `interpreter … IndexAggregateTest`.)*

## §4 supplement — `FieldValuesNotUnique`: a duplicate among the operands' values, plain-list OR starred (probe, 2026-06-24)

> Fold: folded → §9.

Canonical in §9 "Other repetition rules"; the evidence skeleton: two operand shapes with identical semantics — a plain field list (distinctness across named siblings) and a starred operand (distinctness across a repetition's rows; the no-`--group` alternative to `RepetitionNotUnique` on a non-index field — on an index field the auto `uniqueIndex` fires first and suppresses it). Fires iff two present values are EQUAL (typed comparison — scale-0 `5` equals scale-2 `5.00`), VALUE-typed; empties are skipped (two empties are not a duplicate); a formally-invalid operand suppresses; the error field must be referenced (`MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_REFERENCED`). *(Locked: `adapter.laws.FieldValuesNotUniqueDiffTest` — plain + starred — + a fuzz rule `FU`.)*

## §9 supplement — `RepetitionNotUnique`: per-row cross-row composite-key uniqueness (probe, 2026-06-24)

> Fold: folded → §9.

The author-written **cross-ROW** uniqueness rule (vs `FieldValuesNotUnique`'s value-list, and the auto `uniqueIndex`). The rule sits at the **PARENT** group and iterates the keys' repeatable group; the error field is **inside** the repeated group, so it emits **one message per offending row**. Probed semantics:

- **Fires per row whose (composite) key DUPLICATES another row's** within the parent-row scope — `Count [5,5]` flags both rows (2 messages); `[5,7,5]` flags rows 1 & 3, not 2; `[5,7]` and a single row → none. A **composite** key is the tuple: `(Count,Weight) [(5,1),(5,2)]` distinct → none, `[(5,1),(5,1)]` → both flagged.
- **An ALL-empty key row is skipped** (`empty,empty` → none; `5,empty` → the empty row drops, the lone `5` is not a duplicate). But **among participants** (≥1 key field filled) **empties match empties**: a composite `(5,empty),(5,empty)` → the two rows ARE duplicates → flagged.
- **Polarity is directional**: an all-filled duplicate is **VALUE** (change a value); a duplicate whose key has an **empty (fillable) component** is **OMISSION** (`(5,empty),(5,empty)` → OMISSION — filling the empty `Weight` could make them distinct).
- **Keys are plain fields on one path** — no groups (`MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED`), no asterisks (`MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED`), all on a single path (`MVK_NOT_IN_SAME_PATH`); each must be repeatable relative to the rule group, so the rule must be authored at the PARENT (error field inside the repeated group), not inside it (`MVK_REPEATABLE_GROUP_MISSING`).
- **Key admission is by VALUE-VALIDATION, not field KIND; every non-Number key compares by exact STORED TEXT** (source + differential, 2026-07-22, SPEC-2026-07-22-10). `CheckWiederholungBedingungImpl` applies field/category/wildcard/semantic-index/value-validation gates but **no field-kind gate** (`CheckEntityUtils.checkIsValueValidation` rejects only the no-value-validation profile), so a direct **Enumeration, Date, Time, DateTime, Boolean, or Confirm** key is admitted alongside Number/String; a String configured without value validation is rejected (`MVK_INVALID_RAW_TYPE`) as are groups (`MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED`)/wildcards (`MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED`)/semantic indices (`MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_NOT_ALLOWED`), and a category is not a referenceable operand. At runtime `RuntimeController.getWerteOrNullIfEinFeldNichtPruefungsRelevant` special-cases **Number as `VkBigDecimal`** (scale-19 value equality) and passes **every other kind through exact `feldWertIntern` STORED-text equality** — its nearby Number/String-only Javadoc is STALE. So a direct **Enumeration** key compares stored **tokens** (not the display label or category projection: two different tokens sharing one category do not duplicate), and a **Date/Time/DateTime** key compares stored text, never a parsed instant (fixed-width formats are 1:1, so there is no same-instant/different-text firing witness — only Number keys exhibit value-vs-spelling). The peer `RepetitionNotUniqueEvaluator` is already mechanism-aligned (Number → `rnuNumericKey`, every other declared kind → `KeyAtom.Exact`). *(Locked own-domain: authoring `validate.laws.RepetitionNotUniqueKeyKindLawsTest`; differential `adapter.laws.RepetitionNotUniqueKeyKindDiffTest` (groovy-dynamic vs static-Java vs interpreter); multiplatform `interpreter … RepetitionNotUniqueKeyKindTest`.)*
- **It is a per-row 3VL PREDICATE; a guard conjunct gates firing, NOT the cache** (probe + source, 2026-07-07). `RepetitionNotUnique(key)` composes like any predicate: `<guard> And RepetitionNotUnique(key)` fires a duplicate row only when the guard also holds there, and the combined polarity follows the ordinary `And` (OMISSION if either operand is OMISSION; a formally-invalid guard operand → UNKNOWN → suppresses). Crucially the **duplicate cache is built over the full scope INDEPENDENT of the guard** — so a duplicate whose guard is FALSE does not fire, yet it still makes its partner a duplicate. Source, BOTH strategies: runtime `RuntimeController.wiederholungNichtEindeutig` builds the cache once (`RepetitionNotUniqueErrorCache.isInitialised`) over ALL rows in `ermittleWiederholungenMitDuplikaten`, then per-row returns `TRUE_WF`/`TRUE_AF`/`FALSE_OR_UNKNOWN`; codegen `EntityListCondition.st` emits a call to that same runtime method with a per-rule cache (so codegen carries no separate RNU semantics — runtime-parity ⟹ codegen-parity). *(Locked own-domain: `adapter.laws.RepetitionNotUniqueGuardedDiffTest`, incl. a groovy-dynamic vs static-JAVA tri-check.)*
- **A FORMALLY-INVALID key cell is EXCLUDED from the duplicate cache — per-KEY-CELL, not row-wide** (source + differential, 2026-07-07). The cache is built from **check-relevant** cells only: `ermittleWiederholungenMitDuplikaten` calls `getWerteOrNullIfEinFeldNichtPruefungsRelevant`, which returns null — the whole row is `continue`-skipped — the moment ANY key cell's `isWertAngegeben` is **UNKNOWN** (`= !isPruefungsRelevant =` the cell is marked `fehlerhaft`): a bad FORMAT, a duplicate-INDEX (`uniqueIndex`) cell, an over-repetition cell, **and a required-EMPTY cell**. **The required-empty case is a CORRECTION (2026-07-07, composite-key differential): a required-empty key cell IS UNKNOWN, not a valid participant.** The kernel's mandatory check is a **PRELIMINARY autogen rule** (`CodeGenRuleDefinition.isPreliminary()` = *"exactly the auto generated rules are preliminary"* → `MainValidatorController.preliminaryError(…, PFLICHT_FELD_FEHLER, …)` → `ValidationCache.markFieldAsInvalidForValidation`) that runs BEFORE the RNU rule builds its cache, so a required-empty cell is already `fehlerhaft` ⟹ `isWertAngegeben == UNKNOWN` ⟹ the row is dropped. Only an **OPTIONAL-empty** cell (a non-required field left empty) is `isWertAngegeben == FALSE` → a valid empty participant contributing a null key component. So the distinguishing property is **requiredness, not emptiness** (differentially: `(Sku,Count)` with `Sku` required+empty on both rows and `Count` duplicated fires **nothing**; the same shape with an OPTIONAL empty component fires OMISSION). Consequences: `RepetitionNotUnique(indexField)` over a duplicated index does **not** fire (each index cell is `uniqueIndex`-invalid → excluded → no two rows are "duplicates" in the check-relevant cache; only the auto `uniqueIndex` fires), and a malformed / required-empty key cell is likewise never a duplicate. The exclusion consults only the KEY fields (`entityList`), so a formal error on a NON-key field of the same row does NOT drop it — `RepetitionNotUnique(Count)` still fires on rows whose INDEX field is a duplicate. This is the exact sibling of the field-local `uniqueIndex` suppression above (§4 supplement, IF15). *(Locked own-domain: `adapter.laws.RepetitionNotUniqueFormalKeyDiffTest` — dup-index key, malformed key, composite required-empty key, fully-duplicated control, mixed rows, the clean-non-key control, + a groovy-dynamic vs static-JAVA tri-check; the OPTIONAL-empty participant by `adapter.laws.RepetitionNotUniqueDiffTest.compositeEmptyComponentIsOmission`.)*
- **Scope (the BA "basic version", no `@From`):** the **reference group** is the **first repeatable group BELOW the rule group** on the key path, and uniqueness is checked per that group's **PARENT row** — NOT per the key's immediate parent. So a key two repeatable levels deep (`/Project/Milestones/Tasks/Effort`, rule at `/Project`) is checked across **ALL** tasks of **all** milestones (scope = the `/Project` row), not per milestone (kernel-probed on the nested project fixture). For a single-level key (`/Order/Items/Count`) the two coincide. The **`@From _Group_`** selector (the `FromScope` path-op) **overrides the reference group**, scoping uniqueness per the named group's PARENT — so `@From Milestones` (reference Milestones) checks across all milestones (global), while `@From Milestones/Tasks` (reference Tasks) narrows to per-milestone (both kernel-probed on the project fixture). `@From` resolves relative to the rule group: a relative name/path, the `RuleGroup` keyword (the rule group itself — requires it be repeatable, else `MVK …must be repeatable`), `..` segments, or an absolute `/…` path. Naming the rule group by its own name (`@From Project`) is `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY` — use `RuleGroup`. The keyword is localized (`@From` EN, `@Von` DE), unlike the `In`/`IN` separators.

*(Interpreter: `PredicateOp.REPETITION_NOT_UNIQUE` is an ordinary 3VL leaf in the normal boolean walk (IF149), not a top-level recognizer. `RepetitionNotUniqueEvaluator` builds the call-scoped duplicate cache once over the full scope, and `evalPred`/`predType` consume its per-row result wherever the leaf is composed; branch guards therefore affect combination/firing but never membership in the duplicate relation. The cache drops a row when any key cell is formally invalid, including `MANDATORY_FIELD`. Locked: `adapter.laws.RepetitionNotUniqueCompositionDiffTest` for nested/And/Or composition and `testRule`, plus `RepetitionNotUniqueDiffTest`, `RepetitionNotUniqueNestedDiffTest`, `RepetitionNotUniqueGuardedDiffTest`, and `RepetitionNotUniqueFormalKeyDiffTest` for scope, polarity, optional-empty participation, and invalid-key exclusion.)*

## §9 supplement — `SemanticIndex` (`[field For "value"]`): keyed row lookup, absent → empty value (probe, 2026-06-24)

> Fold: folded → §10.

`[Field For "value"]` is the value of `Field` in the repetition whose **index field** equals `"value"` (the kernel's "semantic index" — by the index field, not the positional row number). Probed:

- A matched, **filled** cell yields its value — `[Items/Count For "SKU-1"]` is the `Count` of the Items row with `Sku == "SKU-1"`; `== 5` / `> 0` fire as VALUE.
- **No matching row OR a matched-but-empty cell both read as the kind's EMPTY value** (a number → 0) — `[Items/Count For "SKU-1"] == 0` **fires (OMISSION)** when there is no `SKU-1` row or its `Count` is empty (the absent value is fillable); `> 0` / `== 5` do not fire. So a missing key is NOT "unknown" — it is the empty value, exactly like a missing field reference (empty-as-0 for a number, with the field's directional polarity).

*(Interpreter: `Expr.SemanticIndex(path, indexValue)` (parsed in the `[...]` atom via a `For "…"` clause); `EvalContext.indexedCell` scans the target's repetition rows in scope for the sibling index match, returning the resolved cell or null (→ the kind's empty value). The group's index field is resolved from the threaded `indexFields`; the polarity rides the standard `fill` (fillable iff the cell is absent/empty). Locked: `adapter.laws.SemanticIndexDiffTest` — matched value, other key, and the empty/missing OMISSION cases.)*

## §9 supplement — `SemanticIndex` PRESENCE: absent row ≡ empty cell (FALSE), malformed cell / invalid key → UNKNOWN (source + differential, 2026-07-07)

> Fold: folded → §10.

An indexed ref is also a legal **presence operand** — `FieldFilled(Count For "SKU-1")`, `FieldNotFilled(…)`, or an operand of a fill quantifier (`AllFieldsFilled(Count For "K", Other)`) — and `FieldValueAsString(field For key)` reads through it. The kernel compiles `FieldFilled`/`FieldNotFilled` as one-operand `AllFieldsFilled`/`NoFieldFilled`; the indexed ref resolves to AT MOST ONE cell (`ValidationCache.resolveSemanticIndex` → `IndexFieldCache.lookup`, first match wins) whose presence is the ordinary `isWertAngegeben` 3-state:

- a matched, check-relevant **filled** cell → TRUE;
- a matched row with an **empty** cell, an **ABSENT row** (no row carries the key), or an **empty/no-match KEY field** (the field-keyed `[X For KeyField]` form) → FALSE — an absent row is judged like an empty cell, **not skipped**, so `FieldNotFilled([X For "k"])` FIRES when no row carries the key;
- a **malformed** matched cell, or a formally **invalid key field** → UNKNOWN (not check-relevant — neither the positive nor the negative presence fires).

`FieldValueAsString` over an indexed cell reads the matched cell's stored value; absent/empty reads as `""` (which the `vergleicheSTRING` empty rule then suppresses in a comparison). *(Interpreter: `semanticIndexPresence` behind the shared `presenceTri`; locked by `adapter.laws.SemanticIndexPresenceDiffTest` — IF59.)*

## §9 supplement — `Having`: a per-row filter on a starred aggregate (probe, 2026-06-24)

> Fold: folded → §9.

`Sum(Items* / Count Having <cond>)` (and the other aggregates) consume **only the rows for which the complete `<cond>` is known TRUE** — the filter is evaluated per repetition row before the aggregate. FALSE or UNKNOWN drops a candidate before the selected cell is consumed. Probed: rows (UnitWeight, Count) `(1,3),(2,10),(1,4)` with `Having [Items/UnitWeight] == 1` → the aggregate sees only the W==1 rows → `Sum == 7` (the unfiltered Sum is 17); `> 5` fires, `> 7` does not; `Having [...] == 9` (no matching rows) → `Sum == 0` fires. Polarity is the aggregate's own (Sum over a growable repetition → OMISSION). The filter is a full three-valued condition over the row's fields: ordinary composition still applies, so a complete `TRUE Or UNKNOWN` is TRUE and keeps the candidate.

Correlation does not exclude the current row implicitly: a same-group filter includes the current outer coordinate unless it carries an explicit `CurrentRepetition(G) != CurrentRepetition($G)`. That comparison is by the coordinate of the **named repetition level**, not physical-row identity; in an `A*/B*` candidate set, comparing `B` excludes every candidate with the outer row's `B` leaf index across different `A` rows. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.ValueListQuantifierHavingDiffTest` + `CorrelationLawsTest` + `SelfCorrelatedOverlapQuantifierDiffTest` + `MultiStarOuterCorrelationDiffTest`.)*

*(Interpreter: `Expr.Star` gained an optional `having: Cond?` (parsed from a `Having` clause on the starred operand in `readArgs`); `Interpreter.starVals` evaluates the filter in each per-row context (`EvalContext.rowContexts` → the Document's scoped `RowContext`s) and keeps the matching rows' filled values. Locked: `adapter.laws.HavingDiffTest`.)*

## §9/§11 supplement — the computation-phase filtered iterator PREFETCHES ONE KEPT SUCCESSOR before the current target is consumed (source + differential, both strategies, 2026-07-23; SPEC-2026-07-23-10/-11)

> Fold: folded → §9 + §11.

Filter and target reads of a filtered field list are **interleaved by one kept-candidate lookahead**, not phased. `EbenenIterator.InnerIterator` initializes by searching forward for the first kept candidate (evaluating each complete candidate's `FilterPredicate.isRelevant`, skipping FALSE ones), and each `next()` advances to the NEXT kept candidate *before* returning the current one; `RuntimeController.combineFeldListe`'s consumer loop applies the value provider only to the returned identifier. During computation, `CalculationCache.convertInvalidFieldsForCalculation` makes any read of a formally-invalid cell throw, so the read ORDER decides which cell aborts the computation:

- a filter poison met while searching for a kept candidate's successor **precedes the pending current target** — a filled current `FirstFilledValue` target still CLEARS when the immediate-successor filter, or a filter reached through FALSE candidates, is invalid;
- once one successor is kept, the current target is consumed next — `FirstFilledValue`'s prefix stop (`FirstValueCombiner.canChange`) then leaves every filter **beyond that successor** unread, so a filled first target still WINS over an invalid third filter;
- an earlier terminal authored operand still hides a later filtered operand completely (the later slot's iterator is never constructed);
- the consume-every-row value aggregates (`Sum`/`MinValue`/`MaxValue`/`NumberOfDifferentValues`) ride the **same** iterator but drain it, so for them the lookahead changes only *which* poison aborts first — and that cause is **not observable**: the kernel's generated catch discards `CalculationFieldException.getInternalId()` and marks the target "fehlerhaft berechnet" silently (the interpreter's `PoisonedReadException` is equally cause-blind at its `ComputationEngine` catch). Eager whole-selection filtering and the kernel's lookahead are therefore outcome-EQUIVALENT for the draining aggregates; the read-order boundary is outcome-observable exactly through the prefix consumer `FirstFilledValue`. Validation is untouched — its filter reads are 3VL row-drops (never aborts), so the kept-row stream is order-insensitive there.

*(Interpreter: one shared lazy `keptRowsWithSuccessorLookahead` sequence in `ExprEval` now serves both consumers — `firstFilledScan` keeps the prefix stop, `starOperandVals` keeps the full fold; locked `adapter.laws.FirstFilledValueKeptSuccessorDiffTest` (red→green on the successor-filter poison) + `adapter.laws.AggregateKeptSuccessorDiffTest` (the aggregate agreement locks) + multiplatform `FirstFilledValueKeptSuccessorTest`/`AggregateKeptSuccessorTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF214.)*

## §9 supplement — the star-binding anchor: the `*`-marked level ALWAYS iterates fully; only levels STRICTLY ABOVE the star bind to the enclosing row (differential, both strategies, 2026-07-09)

> Fold: folded → §9.

A starred operand read from an ITERATING row (a per-row rule, or a per-row computation's operand/precondition) enumerates its rows by binding the enclosing iteration's indices only for the repeatable levels **strictly above the starred group** — the `*`-marked level itself (and anything below) always iterates its **full** range. So a bare **same-group star** (`MaxValue(../Pricing* / Helper) == [Helper]` in a per-row rule ON Pricing — the BA "predecessors" shape without the filter) spans **ALL rows**, not the current one: only the max row(s) fire. An **ancestor star** read from a deeper row (`MaxValue(../../Pricing* / Helper)` per Tier row) spans all Pricing rows, not the row's own ancestor. A star **nested below** the iterating row (`Sum(Items* / Amount)` per Order row) still binds the Order row — the standard cross-level case, unchanged. This is coherent with the `$` design (KERNEL-SEMANTICS §9): `$` inside a `Having` is the ONLY outer-row correlation — a bare star never correlates. Differentially pinned in BOTH modes and BOTH strategies (groovy-dynamic == java-static): validation per-row fire sets AND per-row computation preconditions agree. Compute-side corollary (the IG44 R2 atom): the all-rows span makes a per-row dependent's aggregate READ a poisoned sibling cell — one poisoned cell aborts every row's dependent ("fehlerhaft berechnet"), where an own-row collapse would hide it. *(Interpreter: `Document.RowContext.starRows` caps the shared prefix at the starred group's parent — the same anchor `rowContexts` always used; locked `adapter.laws.ComputeStarAggregatePoisonDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter … SameGroupStarBindingTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF93.)*

**…and a CROSS-SUBTREE star binds only along the SHARED named ancestry — never positionally (differential, both strategies, 2026-07-10).** A starred group in a **different subtree** read from an iterating row — `NumberOfFilledGroups(/Root/Settings/Regions*)` in a per-`Claims`-row rule (the kernel team's own "an error text per supported language" metamodel shape: `NumberOfFilledGroups(ErrorMessages*) < NumberOfFilledGroups(/MVK/ModelProperties/SupportedLanguages*)` per `Rules` row) — binds the enclosing row's indices only for the ancestor levels the two named chains **share** (here: the root); from the subtree fork down, the starred chain iterates fully. So the cross-subtree count/presence is **identical from every iterating row**: the rule fires per row on ALL rows (kernel-pinned via `NumberOfFilledGroups`, `AtLeastOneGroupFilled`, and the reversed-polarity `NoGroupFilled`, groovy-dynamic == java-static). An enclosing row's index is never applied **positionally** to an unrelated chain. (A stored condition may carry embedded newlines/indentation inside an operator's parens — the MVK metamodel does — lexed as hidden-channel whitespace.) *(Interpreter: `Document.RowContext.groupScopePrefix` — the group-star row scope (`rowContexts`/`correlationBuckets`) now applies the same `commonPrefixLen` guard `starRows` (IF93) always had; locked `adapter.laws.CrossSubtreeGroupStarDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter … CrossSubtreeGroupStarBindingTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF97.)*

**…and a bare GROUP reference is an iteration anchor of its own — ancestor-or-SELF, per row, judging the row's cells; the ERROR ENTITY is not an anchor (differential + kernel-captured corpus cases, both strategies, 2026-07-10).** A group-scope quantifier argument may reference a repeatable group WITHOUT a star only when the group is INDEX-keyed (`indexFieldName`; otherwise `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` — "missing specification of an asterisk") and the rule's error field sits INSIDE it: the rule then iterates PER ROW of that group and the quantifier judges the CURRENT row's cells — `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled(OrderInformation)` (a real-workspace idiom) fires exactly on the rows that are individually PARTIALLY filled, at each row's own pointer; every-row-complete, all-empty-row, and no-rows instances are silent (groovy-dynamic == java-static). The DECLARED repetition range plays no part. The error entity itself does NOT drive iteration — a rule whose error field sits deeper-repeatable than its condition refs keeps the refs' scope (pinned by the kernel-captured conformance cases). *(Interpreter: `scopeFromRefs` treats a GROUP ref ancestor-or-self; the per-row group fillScope was already row-correct once the scope was; locked `adapter.laws.CollectivelyFilledGroupScopeDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter … GroupRefRuleScopeTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF106.)*

**…and a rule's iteration is REFERENCE-driven — a rule with NO repeatable non-starred ref evaluates ONCE, anchored at its error entity, regardless of its declaring container's content (differential, both strategies, 2026-07-10).** A real-workspace `*_Rules` container ROOT declares rules whose references and error entities all live in OTHER roots (e.g. "the membership end is covered by no contract" — a filtered `NumberOfFilledFields(…) == 0` over `/Contracts/Contract*`, erroring at `/Membership/LinkEnd`): the kernel fires it exactly once at the error entity even though the declaring container has NO instance content (groovy-dynamic == java-static). The declaring group matters for resolving relative refs, never for iteration or content-gating. *(Interpreter: `iterationContexts` re-anchors a no-repeatable-ref rule at its error entity's parent when the declaring scope yields no contexts — the ordinary-rule sibling of IF100's implicit-validation anchor; locked `adapter.laws.CrossRootRuleScopeDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter … CrossRootRuleScopeTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF103.)*

**…and compatible NESTED references select the DEEPEST path — one evaluation per ACTUAL inner row, with per-parent outer resolution; zero rows evaluate zero times (differential, both strategies, 2026-07-24; probed for the lean client's SPEC-2026-07-24-01).** A condition reading an outer (`Lines/Weight`) and an inner (`Lines/Items/Qty`) repeatable field iterates the INNER rows — once per actual `Items` row across all `Lines` rows, with complete parent coordinates: `[../Weight]` resolves against the current row's OWN line (a row exceeding another parent's limit but not its own stays silent — the aliasing discriminator). An outer row with NO inner rows contributes nothing; an existing row with content evaluates exactly once and can fire on its empty target cell (behind the mandatory positive guard); nothing is ever synthesized at zero cardinality. The static complement pins the "only actual rows" law from the authoring side: an iterating rule whose condition could hold on a NOT-FILLED repetition is rejected at model consistency — `MVK_NEG_CONDITION_IN_ITERATION`, naming the offending LEVEL ("Iteration for '/Order/Lines' is specified, but the rule can fire for not filled repetitions of this repetition level" — flagged at the OUTER level for a rule on nested `Items`, confirming the per-level iteration markers); the guard law is `RuleGroupIterationLawsTest`. *(Interpreter: `IterationScopes.deepestRepeatableAnchor`/`iterationContexts` already the sole reference-driven owner — verification-first probe, no divergence; locked `adapter.laws.ReferenceDrivenIterationScopeDiffTest` (tri-check) + multiplatform `interpreter … ReferenceDrivenIterationScopeTest` (JVM+Node).)*

**…and a MULTI-star operand binds at the FIRST `*`-marked level; `NumberOfFilledFields` honors a `Having` filter (differential, both strategies, 2026-07-09).** For `A* / B* / F` read from an iterating row, only the levels strictly above **A** (the first star) bind — a per-`B`-row read spans ALL of `A`'s rows (kernel-pinned for a filtered count AND an unfiltered `Sum`). And the count aggregate `NumberOfFilledFields(star Having <cond>)` consumes only filter-kept rows, exactly like the value aggregates (`anzahlAngegebenerFelder` rides the same filtered `EntityIterator`): the filter drops rows FIRST — a malformed cell in a dropped row does not suppress the count — and a KEPT row's malformed cell makes the count non-evaluable. A `$`-correlated filter (`[X] == [$X]`) resolves against the computing/iterating row, giving the "count my duplicates across the whole list" idiom real corpus models use. *(Interpreter: `Expr.Star.bindDepth` threaded through the star family; the count gained the filtered loop; locked `adapter.laws.MultiStarFilteredCountDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter … MultiStarFilteredCountTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF94.)*

**…and the same first-star boundary governs declared-tail polarity at every reopened level (tri-differential, both strategies, 2026-07-21).** For `A*/B*/C*/F`, the aggregate remains fillable/OMISSION-typed when any in-scope `A` capacity is missing, any actual `A` lacks a declared `B`, any actual `A/B` lacks a declared `C`, or an instantiated leaf cell is empty. An unbounded starred repetition always retains that open tail. A flattened `leafRowCount < C.repeatability` test is therefore wrong: it loses missing outer and middle branches. Repeatable levels strictly above the first `*` remain bound and their unused declared tail does not taint the aggregate. *(Interpreter: a cached per-scope hierarchical child-count proof, O(actual rows × star depth) with no Cartesian enumeration; locked by `MultiStarDeclaredTailPolarityDiffTest` + `StarOnlyIterationScopeDiffTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF194.)*

## §9 supplement — a STAR-ONLY condition scopes at the star's BINDING levels; with no repeatable reference level the rule fires ONCE, root-content-gated, at the pinned first-row anchor (source + differential, both strategies, 2026-07-18)

> Fold: folded → §9.
> (2026-07-24, with the reference-driven rewrite: the headline scope law — star binding levels iterate, the starred level collapses — and the once-evaluation anchor are canonical; the 16-arm evidence, root-content gate, and phantom-anchor pinning stay here.)

The star-only completion of the reference-driven iteration law above: when a rule's condition carries **no per-row (non-starred) repeatable reference at all**, its iteration scope comes from the starred refs' **BINDING levels** — the unindexed repeatable levels **strictly above the first `*`-marked group** carry the per-row ITERATION marker exactly as a non-starred ref's ancestors do, while the starred level and everything below are a WILDCARD collapse and never iterate. Source mechanism (codegen): `RuleIterationLimitCreator.getIterationsEndeFuerKontexte` pins every error-entity context level to 1 unless `AnalyseService.isIteration` finds a reference carrying ITERATION at that level (refs inside a `Having` filter excluded unless `$`-outer); the rule's **declared group appears nowhere**. Kernel-pinned consequences (groovy-dynamic == java-static, 16 arms):

- A rule at repeatable `Items` whose whole condition is `Sum(/Order/Items*/Count) > -1` fires **ONCE**, anchored `/Order[1]/Items[1]/Count` — never per row (the shape the interpreter over-fired 3-vs-1). A multi-star (`Items*/Lines*`) collapses **both** starred levels; a deep star (`Items/Lines*`) iterates the unstarred `Items` above it — the same fire set from ANY declaring group (placement-irrelevant).
- With **no repeatable reference level anywhere** (star-only with non-repeatable binding levels, or star-free with only non-repeatable refs — a rule at repeatable `Items` referencing only a root field fired 3 duplicate interpreter messages vs the kernel's 1), the rule evaluates exactly **ONCE**: every unreferenced repeatable level of the error path pins to row **1**, **phantom anchors included** — the kernel fires at an UNINSTANTIATED `Fees[1]`/`Lines[1]`.
- The once-evaluation is **content-gated at the root instance**, not the collapse subtree: it fires with content only OUTSIDE the starred subtree and stays silent on a fully-empty document. The fired polarity is the aggregate family's own (`Sum(star) > c` OMISSION even with all cells filled; the count aggregates VALUE) — unchanged by this law.

*(Interpreter: `IterationScopes.iterationContexts` derives tier-2 scope from `starRefs`' binding levels (`Expr.Star.bindDepth`) and the tier-3 once-context from the error path's root group — whose `evaluated` flag IS the root content gate — replacing the declaring-group fallback and subsuming the IF103 error-parent re-anchor; locked `adapter.laws.StarOnlyIterationScopeDiffTest` (tri-check, 16 arms) + multiplatform `interpreter … StarOnlyIterationScopeTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF127.)*

## §6 supplement — DATE_TIME rides the DATE format definition: the date extractors + date diffs accept a datetime operand (source + differential, both strategies, 2026-07-09)

> Fold: folded → §6.

The kernel has **no datetime-specific format definition** — a DATE_TIME field is a `FormatDefinitionDatum` whose format string carries the time (`yyyy-MM-dd'T'HH:mm:ss`), so `feldWertAlsDatum` parses the full value into a `VkDate`. `DayFromDate`/`MonthFromDate`/`YearFromDate`/`QuarterFromDate` accept DATE_TIME and read its date fields (an EMPTY datetime → the fillable 0). `DifferenceInDays` also accepts DATE_TIME, but retains the operand clock and counts model-zone legacy-calendar day steps, so times can change the result. `DifferenceInMonths`/`DifferenceInYears` use calendar date fields only for legal DATE operands; they reject DATE_TIME, as do `AddMonths`/`AddYears`. *(IF95's original date-part claim is superseded for `DifferenceInDays` by IF186; locked by `DateFunctionsOverDateTimeDiffTest` + `CalendarStepDifferenceInDaysDiffTest`.)*

## §8 supplement — the value-list membership family (2026-06-04 experiments)

> Fold: folded → §8 + §12.

(The shape-B quantifier runtime semantics and the `Having` escalation family are canonical in §8 "The value-list quantifiers" and §12; the authoring slot rules and per-arm evidence stay here.) Five operators in two grammar shapes. **Shape A** `FieldValueIncludedInValueList(field, v1, …)` / `FieldValueNotIncludedInValueList(…)` (DE: `FeldWertInWerteListe`/`FeldWertNichtInWerteListe`); **shape B** `AtLeastOne|No|NotAllFieldValue(s)IncludedInValueList(f1, f2 In v1, …)` (DE: `MindestensEin…`/`Kein…`/`NichtAlle…`; `In` is a language-independent grammar literal like the symbols). Slot rules:

- **Only string, enum, and number fields participate** — a boolean/confirm field draws **`MVK_ONLY_STRING_ENUM_NUMBER_ALLOWED`** ("only allowed to use string fields, enumerations or numbers (including amounts)").
- **Numeric values are integer literals only** — the grammar's value-list `number` is `'-'? digits`; a decimal (`10.50`) is a *parse error* (`MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`), even against a scale-2 field. Negative integers are fine.
- **Value types must match the field kind** — string field vs numbers or number field vs strings → **`MVK_INVALID_TYPES_FOR_COMPARISON`** (the plural-`TYPES` sibling of the comparison code).
- **Enum admission is UNION, not intersection (SPEC-2026-07-22-05, source `CheckFeldListeUndWerteListenBedingungImpl.checkEnumValuesForAllFields` + `CheckWerteListenUtils.checkEnumValues`)** — shape A: an out-of-domain value → **`MVK_INVALID_STRING_CONSTANT_FOR_ENUMERATION`**. Shape B (literal value list): each literal must be in the **union** of the selected members' projected domains — a *direct* enum reference contributes its distinct stored values, a *category* access `[field -> Cat]` contributes that category's values — and need **not** be in every listed member; an absent literal → **`MVK_INVALID_STRING_CONSTANT_FOR_ENUMERATION_OR_CATEGORY`**. A shape-B member may itself be a category projection, and a direct reference and a `[field -> Cat]` reference on the **same** field are **distinct** members (complete `EntityRef` equality includes the category name).
- **No exact reference may repeat** — a duplicate direct or category reference → **`MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM1`** (`checkParamsReferencedOnlyOnce`); wildcard/starred refs are skipped (as `checkDuplicatesInDirectRefs` skips them). Applies to string and enum field lists alike, and — for the entity-valued form — across the **combined** field+value lists.
- **Shape B field lists must be type-homogeneous** — mixing a string and a number (or enum) field → **`MVK_VARYING_TYPES_NOT_ALLOWED`** ("All fields must be the same type"). A single-field list is legal. **Entity-valued shape B** (the value side is another field list): both sides are nonempty and share one base type (String/Enumeration/Number, else `MVK_VARYING_TYPES_NOT_ALLOWED` / `MVK_ONLY_STRING_ENUM_NUMBER_ALLOWED`); enum-domain **containment is explicitly NOT required** (the source's `Anmerkung`), so two disjoint enum declarations are legal.
- The field slot is a **bare path** (a bracketed `[Field]` is a parse error); a category projection renders bare too, `field -> Cat`. The value list may also contain **field references** (`[OtherField]`) and may mix them with literals; shape B's right side accepts an entity list or strings (no numbers, per grammar).

*(Locked: `validate.laws.ValueListLawsTest` + N7 examples of all five operators. **Field-valued (entity) lists are now implemented** — the value-list quantifiers take `FieldListRef` operands (incl. `*`-flattened scopes), locked by `adapter.EntityListValueListReadTest`. **Shape A with a field entry** — `FieldValueIncludedInValueList(Choice, [Allowed])`, where a value-list entry is a field rather than a constant — is also read, `adapter.ValueListFieldEntryReadTest`.)*

**Runtime semantics of the shape-B QUANTIFIERS (source + differential 2026-07-07; pass structures + witness-specific filter polarity source-confirmed + tri-locked 2026-07-24, SPEC-2026-07-23-16; supersedes the 2026-06-24 probe note — that note missed the per-cell star expansion and wrongly assumed a shared blanket malformed-suppression "untestable" because lists were string-only: NUMBER field lists ARE expressible, e.g. `(Items* then Count In Quantity, StockOnHand)`, so malformed cells are reachable).** The kernel (`mindestensEinFeldWertInWerteListe` / `keinFeldWertInWerteListe` / `nichtAlleFeldWerteInWerteListe`, shared by both strategies) expands BOTH sides per cell — a STARRED entry contributes one cell per instantiated row — and classifies each cell by `isWertAngegeben`: TRUE (filled + check-relevant), FALSE (empty), UNKNOWN (present, not check-relevant — malformed). **Each operator then owns its OWN pass structure over the two operand lists — a side is never one flattened UNKNOWN/filter summary; encounter order and the deciding operand matter:**

- **`AtLeastOne`** (`fuegePruefungsrelevanteFeldWerteZuWerteListe` → `mindestensEinFeldWertInWerteListeIntern`) consumes the COMPLETE values side first — only TRUE cells are collected (empty/UNKNOWN member cells skipped outright; an **empty value SET → no fire**), and a values-side filter marks the fire only when that filtered operand **contributed a PRESENT member** (the flag rides the present add). It then scans fields in authored encounter order to the first TRUE cell ∈ the set: the fire is `TRUE_AF` (OMISSION) iff the **DECIDING (matching) operand** carries a filter or a filtered values operand selected a present member, else `TRUE_WF` (VALUE) — an earlier filtered NONwitness operand, a filter that kept only empty/UNKNOWN cells, or one that dropped everything never escalates.
- **`No`** (`fuegeFeldWerteZuWerteListe` → `keinFeldWertInWerteListeIntern`) materializes the COMPLETE values side before fields, poison-sensitively: an **UNKNOWN member POISONS** it (`FALSE_OR_UNKNOWN`, no fire — values are never lazy, a fields-side match cannot rescue), while an empty member, an omitted declared tail, or a **REACHED values-side filter — whatever it kept, even nothing** — contributes sticky omission potential (`einWertDerWerteListeNichtAngegeben`). It then scans fields in authored cell order: a **MATCH terminates non-firing before every later cell** (a later UNKNOWN/filter/tail is never reached), while a reached UNKNOWN before the match suppresses. If the scan exhausts it fires — including when nothing is filled at all (the sole `canFireEmpty`) — `TRUE_AF` iff any reached empty fields-cell / omitted tail / fields-side filter occurred (`isAuslassungsFehler`) or the values side carries omission potential, else `TRUE_WF`.
- **`NotAll`** (`noEntityFilled` → `fuegeFeldWerteZuWerteListe` → `nichtAlleFeldWerteInWerteListeIntern`) runs a fields-PRESENCE prepass first: with **no TRUE fields-cell it does not fire WITHOUT consuming the values side** — an UNKNOWN values member cannot poison it. Otherwise it consumes the values side exactly like `No` (UNKNOWN poisons; empty/tail/filter → omission potential), then RESTARTS the fields scan and fires at the first TRUE cell ∉ the set; an UNKNOWN **fields-cell is skipped** (no poison on that side — the `No`-vs-`NotAll` asymmetry). The fire is `TRUE_AF` iff the **outside-WITNESS operand** carries a filter or the values side carries omission potential — it has NO fields-side empty/tail account.
- An **EMPTY values MEMBER** contributes nothing to the set — never the number `0` — and sets the `einWertDerWerteListeNichtAngegeben` flag: a fired `No`/`NotAll` is then **`TRUE_AF` (OMISSION)**.
- **Filter polarity is witness-/reached-specific, never one side-wide flag** (supersedes the earlier "a `Having`-filtered entry escalates a fired result unconditionally" generalization, which was over-drawn from single-operand evidence — with ONE operand the filtered operand always IS the decider/reached, so every prior lock stays green). The kernel-observable separators: an earlier filtered nonmatch followed by an unfiltered `AtLeastOne` match (or `NotAll` outside witness) fires **VALUE**, and an `AtLeastOne` values-side filter that selected nothing present leaves the fire **VALUE** — while `No` keeps a reached values-side filter's omission even for a filtered absence. *(Both strategies agree; the pre-fix interpreter's global flag typed all of these OMISSION — the S2/S4 reds.)*
- The decided-vs-suppressed distinction inside the fields scan (a match BEFORE a later UNKNOWN decides; UNKNOWN first suppresses) is **not kernel-message-observable** — `ValidierungsErgebnis` merges FALSE and UNKNOWN (`FALSE_OR_UNKNOWN`) and the DSL has no negation, so the fire set is order-insensitive; both kernel routes agree on no-fire in either order. The mechanism (the early `return` in `keinFeldWertInWerteListeIntern`) is source-anchored and pinned at the interpreter's evaluator seam (JVM+Node).
- **A category projection `[field -> Cat]` is a per-cell operand, on either side (SPEC-2026-07-22-05, tri-checked both strategies):** its cell presence is the underlying enum field's — an **EMPTY** enum via a category hop is an EMPTY cell (so a fired `No` is **OMISSION**, not the VALUE a "present unknown" would give; it does NOT poison), an **out-of-domain/malformed** enum is UNKNOWN, and a **filled** enum contributes its (many-to-one) category value. This is the case that surfaces only once category projections are authorable in the quantifier.

**The `Having` escalation holds for the FILL quantifiers as well — a fired quantifier over a `Having`-filtered field star is UNCONDITIONALLY `TRUE_AF` → OMISSION (differential, both strategies, 2026-07-10).** Pinned in BOTH directions: `NoFieldFilled(G* / F Having <cond>)` fired with NOTHING kept by the filter (and at zero rows) is OMISSION, and `AtLeastOneFieldFilled(G* / F Having <cond>)` fired over a fully-KEPT, FILLED row is OMISSION too — the escalation does not depend on what the filter dropped or whether filling could change the outcome. (The unfiltered starred quantifiers keep their two-range fillability polarity — the escalation is the *filtered* iterator's.) This was the dominant real-workspace validateFull divergence class (the `polarity-flip OMISSION→VALUE` histogram bucket — 73 one-sided sigs, mostly implicit computation-validations whose preconditions carry filtered "currently active" quantifiers). *(Interpreter: a fired single-star `Having` quantifier returns OMISSION before the kept-rows fillability; locked `adapter.laws.HavingQuantifierPolarityDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter … HavingQuantifierPolarityTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF101.)*

**A `Having`-filtered quantifier tallies each KEPT row's OWN cell — a dropped row's invalid cell never counts, and per-row self-correlation judges the kept row, not the iterating one (differential, both strategies, 2026-07-10).** The real-workspace per-row self-correlated period-overlap idiom (`FieldFilled(Start) And AtLeastOneFieldFilled(../G* /Price Having (CurrentRepetition(RuleGroup) != CurrentRepetition($RuleGroup) And <overlap disjunction>))`): evaluating row 2 — whose OWN `Price` is formally invalid — the kernel's filter DROPS row 2 (the self-exclusion) before its cell is ever read, keeps the valid row 1, and fires (groovy-dynamic == java-static). The kept cell's 3-state (`isWertAngegeben`) is judged per KEPT row. *(Interpreter: the filtered tally + the `$`-join buckets judge invalidity at the KEPT row's reps — the old check resolved at the ENCLOSING rule row's reps, so a per-row rule with an invalid own cell fired nowhere; locked `adapter.laws.SelfCorrelatedOverlapQuantifierDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter … HavingTallyRowCorrectInvalidityTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF105.)*

**…and it holds for COMPARISONS consuming an encountered filtered star too — the escalation is the COMBINERS' (differential, both strategies, 2026-07-10; positional refinement 2026-07-20).** A fired comparison whose operand rides a reached `Having`-filtered star — `[F] != RoundDown([Share]/100 * FirstFilledValue(star Having <active-window>), 0)`, the real-workspace per-row implicit computation-validation shape — is `TRUE_AF` → OMISSION **unconditionally**: when the filter drops every row (the FFV empty-selection, IF80's polarity through the filter) AND when it KEEPS a filled row whose value merely disagrees (groovy-dynamic == java-static). The mechanism is combiner-level: every combiner's `getValue(containsFilter)` marks the encountered filtered result "not specified", so the fired consumer escalates regardless of direction or keep-set. `FirstFilledValue` adds a positional boundary: `combineFeldListe` presents each slot's filter marker immediately before that slot and stops once `FirstValueCombiner` becomes non-changeable; therefore a filter on a later unvisited slot cannot escalate polarity. *(Interpreter: ordinary value consumers retain the shared `hasHavingStar` escalation, while `FirstFilledValue` uses its ordered value/polarity scan; locked by `adapter.laws.HavingStarComparisonPolarityDiffTest`, `adapter.laws.FirstFilledValueFilterEncounterDiffTest`, and multiplatform `interpreter … HavingStarComparisonPolarityTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF104/IF184.)*
- **Index-field errors are field-LOCAL:** a starred INDEX field's empty cell is mandatory-empty and EVERY duplicate-valued cell carries `uniqueIndex` (§4) — those cells are UNKNOWN (skipped by `AtLeastOne`, poisoning `No`) — while a CLEAN SIBLING field in the same rows reads all its cells normally (the row is not collectively compromised).
- **A NUMBER field-list's value list is field-valued only** — a numeric literal is authoring-rejected both unquoted (`MVK_INVALID_ENTITY`) and quoted (`MVK_INVALID_TYPES_FOR_COMPARISON`); string lists take string literals. Numeric membership compares scale-19-insensitively (`VkBigDecimal.equals` rescales to `DEFAULT_SCALE`).
- **A `Having` filter is ACCEPTED + EVALUATED on EITHER side** (probed authoring + runtime 2026-07-08; the grammar is `entityListe : entitySpec*`, each `entitySpec` carrying an optional `Mit`=`Having`). It **DROPS a row BEFORE the per-cell classification** — a dropped row contributes NOTHING (not to the membership test, not to the empty/UNKNOWN flags), so only kept rows are classified by `isWertAngegeben`. Keep-law = the overlap-predicate one (§6, IF72): keep iff the filter fires TRUE per row (a **malformed** filter operand → runtime-UNKNOWN → not-TRUE → DROP). Its POLARITY effect is the witness-/reached-specific per-operator rule above (SPEC-2026-07-23-16) — never an unconditional side-wide escalation. `No` still fires vacuously when the filter drops ALL rows (empty kept set) — and that reached filter makes the fire OMISSION.

*(Interpreter: the dedicated `Cond.ValueListQuant` node, evaluated by the per-OPERAND encounter-ordered `vlqOperands` expansion + the single-pass `vlqRes` — ONE evaluator whose dispatch owns the three pass structures, truth + witness-specific polarity in one scan (IF218). The starred operand is filtered via `havingInterp`, only kept rows classified; an `Expr.EnumCategory` cell classifies three-way via the underlying field's `filledTri` — an empty enum → EMPTY, not a present Unknown (SPEC-2026-07-22-05). Locked by `adapter.laws.ValueListQuantifierDiffTest` (plain literal form), `EntityValueListDiffTest` (values star), `ValueListQuantifierStarDiffTest` (starred fields, malformed asymmetry, empty members, index-local errors, polarity — IF60), `ValueListQuantifierHavingDiffTest` (the filter keep-law, tri-checked — IF74/IG48), `ValueListQuantifierOrderDiffTest` (the six SPEC-2026-07-23-16 separator families: encounter order + witness-specific polarity, tri-checked incl. java-static polarity signatures), `EnumValueListUnionDiffTest` (union + category firing/polarity, tri-checked) + multiplatform `interpreter … EnumCategoryValueListEvalTest` / `ValueListQuantifierOrderEvalTest` (JVM+Node polarity twins + the decided-vs-suppressed internal-verdict pins); authoring/read locked by `dsl.operators.EnumValueListUnionTest`, `validate.laws.EnumValueListUnionLawsTest`, `adapter.EnumCategoryValueListReadTest`.)*

## field-level numeric functions + mixed-scope field-fill (corpus tail, 2026-06-07)

> Fold: folded → §1.

- **Field-level rounding/abs** — `RoundDownValue(f[, n])`/`RoundUpValue(f[, n])`/`RoundAccountingValue(f[, n])` and `AbsValue(f)`: the kernel's `FieldIdOperationWithParameters` numeric ops, **distinct from the operand-list `RoundDown(expr[, n])`**. Both rounding forms accept **0..14 inclusive** and omission means zero; the value forms honor a present places argument rather than being integer-only. They take one bare field before that optional parameter. The kernel parse representation stores only an integer places value defaulted to zero, so persisted explicit `, 0` reads back canonically as omission; a positive places argument is retained. Modeled as `FieldRounding`; locked by `adapter.FieldRoundingReadTest`, `adapter.FieldRoundingPlacesReadTest`, and `adapter.laws.SingleArgRoundDiffTest`.
- **A field-fill quantifier accepts a *mixed* group+field scope** — `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled(Section, Other/Note)` (a group operand alongside a field): the kernel expands each group to every descendant field recursively. Read makes that explicit (a field-list quantifier over the flattened fields); nested descendants are locked by `adapter.RecursiveGroupOperandExpansionReadTest` and the direct-child control by `adapter.MixedScopeFieldFillReadTest`.

## §5 supplement — numeric coercions (family 4, completed 2026-07-23)

> Fold: folded → §5.

The number twins of the string coercions (`FieldIdOperationName`): `FieldValueAsNumber(field)` (DE `FeldWertAlsZahl`) and `RangeAsNumber(field, start, end)` (DE `BereichAlsZahl`, an `IFieldIdOperationWithParameters` carrying a **0-based** start — rendered 1-based, like `RangeAsString`). Both return a number, so they compose into comparisons/arithmetic. Two distinct convertibility rules:
- **`RangeAsNumber` tolerates any string field** — a non-numeric substring (or an empty field) yields **0**, not an error (so `RangeAsNumber(ShipmentRef, 1, 2) == 0` is valid against a plain string field).
- **`FieldValueAsNumber` requires the complete SELECTED domain to *always* convert**, else `MVK_NO_CONVERT_IN_NUMBER`. A String source must be value-validating, have the exact pattern `[0-9]+`, and declare finite `maxLength <= 15`; its result scale is 0. An ordinary Enumeration selects either its stored values or one named category's values. Every token in that selected domain must obey the decimal grammar and the 15-digit budget, and the result scale is the maximum fractional length in that domain. The grammar admits an optional leading `-`, at most one `.`, a leading point, and host decimal digits; it rejects a trailing point, leading `+`, exponent, whitespace, and comma. **The digit predicate is the host UTF-16 `char`-unit `Character.isDigit(char)` class (SPEC-2026-07-23-07): `CheckEntityUtils` delegates each selected token to Commons Lang `NumberUtils.isParsable`, which iterates `String.charAt` — so non-ASCII BMP decimal digits (Arabic-Indic, fullwidth) are admitted and converted by their decimal value, but a supplementary-plane decimal code point is rejected because its two surrogate code units are tested separately. This is narrower than `Character.isDigit(int)` or a Unicode-scalar predicate. In a kernel-valid model a supplementary char never reaches this check: the model's supported-characters set is BMP-only (surrogates are not declarable — MVK), so such a token is rejected earlier as an unsupported sign; the char-unit digit-class rejection is therefore directly observable only in the interpreter (which applies no model charset gate on this path) and holds here as a source-level kernel property.** Category selection happens before admission and runtime conversion, so nonnumeric stored values may legally map to numeric category tokens. *(Source-verified in `CheckFeldOpImpl`/`CheckEntityUtils`; admission locked by `FieldValueAsNumberDomainLawsTest`, the supplementary char-unit rejection by the interpreter's JVM/Node `FieldValueAsNumberDomainTest`.)*

The operation-form `RoundDown`/`RoundUp`/`RoundAccounting` and `Abs` admit a direct `FieldValueAsNumber(...)` child over either stored or named-category selection. Their shared static gate rejects an **immediate** literal or bracketed literal and requires a number-like child result; it does not recursively forbid constants elsewhere in a legal child subtree. Rounding replaces the derived conversion scale with its authored places (omission means 0) and preserves directional fillability. `Abs` retains the conversion scale and transforms fillability by the current sign, so symmetric missing zero becomes grow-only in magnitude. In computation, clean missing stays numeric zero through both wrapper families and a formally unavailable source remains poison. *(Source-verified in `CheckRundenOperOpImpl`/`CheckFeldOperOpImpl`/`CheckOpUtils`; dynamic Groovy + generated Java + interpreter locked by `FieldValueAsNumberWrapperDiffTest`, JVM/Node by `FieldValueAsNumberWrapperTest`.)*

The same operation-form wrappers admit `RangeAsNumber(...)` directly. The range is already a nonliteral scale-0 Number operation: rounding replaces that scale with the authored places (omission means 0) while preserving the range's fillability, and `Abs` retains scale 0 and is value-identical because range conversion never produces a negative value. A missing source remains grow-only zero through either wrapper; a filled numeric selection or nonnumeric fallback zero is fixed. A formally unavailable source remains unknown in validation and poisons both the computation target and a dependent read. The result envelope renders numerically equal integral values canonically (for example an authored scale-2 result may surface as `125`); scale is the operation's static/result contract, not a trailing-zero string guarantee. *(Source-verified in `CheckFeldOpMitParamsImpl`/`CheckRundenOperOpImpl`/`CheckFeldOperOpImpl`/`CheckOpUtils` and `RuntimeController.bereichAlsZahl`; dynamic Groovy + generated Java + interpreter locked by `RangeAsNumberWrapperDiffTest`, JVM/Node by `RangeAsNumberWrapperTest`.)*

Modeled as `FieldValueAsNumber`/`RangeAsNumber` (Calculation, kind NUMBER), the numeric mirror of `FieldValueAsString`/`RangeAsString`; authored via `Dsl.fieldValueAsNumber`/`Dsl.rangeAsNumber`. `FieldValueAsNumber` retains the existing node while its sealed `NumericTextSource` admits a direct field/enum or `CategoryRef`, so `FieldValueAsNumber(Field -> Category)` is typed and read back without admitting arbitrary calculations. *(Locked: `FieldValueAsNumberCategoryReadTest`, `adapter.StringCoercionReadTest.roundTripsNumberCoercions`, BA-corpus `FieldValAsNumber`/`RangeAsNum`, and N7 catalog examples.)*

**Runtime semantics + interpreter status — the `Range*` substrings (2026-06-24).** Source-read `RuntimeController.getSubstring`/`bereichAlsZahl`: the substring is `value[start-1, end)` (1-based inclusive) **but** an empty value OR `end > value.length()` yields **`""`** — never a partial substring (the out-of-range cliff). `RangeAsNumber` then parses its substring **only when it is a pure run of digits (`\d+`)** — anything with a sign, a decimal point, non-digits, or empty → **`0`**. The interpreter reproduces both (`Interpreter.rangeSubstring` + the `\d+` gate). **`RangeAsString` is read and authored** in rulekit's DSL (a string coercion, alongside `RangeAsNumber`/`FieldValue*` — `adapter.StringCoercionReadTest`, `dsl.operators.RangeOperandTest`); the interpreter handles both for evaluation. *(Interpreter-locked: `adapter.laws.RangeCoercionDiffTest`.)*

**Empty-string comparison — generalized (2026-06-24).** `BedingungsOperatorHelper.vergleicheSTRING` returns **false when EITHER operand is the empty string**, for `==` AND `!=` alike — so an empty `RangeAsString` result, or a **literal `""`**, makes the comparison **not fire** (NOT "they differ"). The interpreter previously matched this only for empty string *fields* (which resolve to `Unknown`); it now matches it for *any* empty string operand (`Interpreter.evalCmp` suppresses when either side is an empty `Val.Txt`), fixing a latent `[field] != ""` divergence the `RangeAsString` work surfaced. *(Interpreter-locked: `adapter.laws.RangeCoercionDiffTest.notEqualToEmptyLiteralDoesNotFire`.)*

**Interpreter status — the value coercions `FieldValueAsString`/`FieldValueAsNumber` (completed 2026-07-23).** `feldWertAlsString` returns the field's **RAW stored value** as a string (no re-canonicalization — `"5.00"` stays `"5.00"`), empty → `""`. `feldWertAlsZahl` projects the exact stored/category token and parses it to a number; an **empty** source reads as symmetric-fillable **`0`**, a present value is fixed, and a formally unavailable source stays Unknown in validation and poisons a reached computation read. The interpreter uses the same category projection as ordinary enum-category evaluation, iterates UTF-16 `char` units (Kotlin `Char`) and normalizes each host decimal digit — the same `Character.isDigit(char)` class as the kernel, so a supplementary-plane decimal code point is rejected (its surrogate halves each fail the digit test), not admitted as a Unicode scalar (SPEC-2026-07-23-07) — before its cross-platform decimal parser, and feeds the result through the existing rounding/`Abs` value and fill mechanisms without a wrapper-specific conversion path. *(Tri-engine locked by `FieldValueAsNumberDomainDiffTest`, `FieldValueAsNumberWrapperDiffTest`, and the existing direct-enum `FieldValueCoercionDiffTest`; JVM/Node locked by `FieldValueAsNumberDomainTest` and `FieldValueAsNumberWrapperTest`.)*

## operand-list math: Abs / Min / Max (family 5, 2026-06-07)

> Fold: folded → §2.

The kernel's `IOpListOperation` (`OpListOperationName`) has two groups: the rounding family (`RoundDown`/`RoundUp`/`RoundAccounting`, modeled as `Rounding`, which carry decimal places) and the parameterless **`Abs`/`Min`/`Max`** (DE keywords are the untranslated `Abs`/`Min`/`Max`). These operate on numeric **value expressions**, not field/repetition lists — so they are distinct from the field-list aggregates `MaxValue`/`MinValue` (`EntityListOperation`, modeled as `ListAggregate`): `Max([A], [B])` is the per-row max of two values; `MaxValue(Items*/x)` is the max over a repetition list. `Abs` takes exactly one operand (`Abs([A] - [B])`, equivalent to `AbsValue` when the operand is a bare field); `Min`/`Max` take one or more. Operands render as values (`Min([A], [B])`), not bare entity refs. Modeled as `OpListMath` (Calculation, kind NUMBER); authored via `Dsl.abs`/`Dsl.min`/`Dsl.max`. *(Locked: `adapter.ArithmeticReadTest.roundTripsOperandListMath` + BA-corpus `AbsDiff`/`MinOf`/`MaxOf`; N7 catalog examples for `Abs`/`Min`/`Max`.)*

**Per-call constant budget (SPEC-2026-07-23-06).** Each numeric operand-list `Min`/`Max` call independently admits at most ONE immediate-or-bracketed numeric literal; a second in the same immediate list is `MVK_TOO_MANY_CONST_FOR_CALC_OP`. The kernel counts only immediate constants of one call's operation list: `CheckFeldListenOperOpImpl.checkMaxOneConstant` limits `CheckOpUtils.isConstant` to two hits, and `isConstant` is true only for a `SINGLE_OP` `konstante` or a `BRACKET_OP` (curly `{…}`) that recursively wraps one — so a literal inside arithmetic (`[A] + 1`), inside another value function, or inside a nested `Min`/`Max` call is NOT an immediate constant of the outer list. Curly braces therefore do not hide a literal (`Min([A], {1}, 2)` is rejected, `Min([A], {1})` admitted), while a paren around a literal is a parse error (`MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`, not the grouping token). Consequently `Min(Min([A], 1), 2)` (same selector), `Min(Max([A], 1), 2)` (different selector), `Min([A] + 1, 2)`, and a completed call inside surrounding arithmetic (`Min([A], 1) + 1`) are all admitted, while the flattened `Min([A], 1, 2)` is rejected — nested calls are authored structure and must not be flattened. The static result takes the union/maximum of operand scales and keeps multiplicative-constant capability only when every operand has it; dmkits defers that scale summary (`NumericScaleSummary.from` returns `deferred()` for `OpListMath`) and delegates the constant gate to the kernel, so this is a regression lock on the kernel contract, not a reimplemented check. Runtime folds operands left to right in authored order — order-independent for these commutative extrema, so the separation is observable at admission, not at the folded value. *(Source-verified in `CheckFeldListenOperOpImpl`/`CheckOpUtils.isConstant`; admission locked by `OpListMathConstantLawsTest`, nested-call peer composition by `adapter.laws.InterpreterDiffTest`.)*

Operation-form `Abs` admits a direct numeric field-list aggregate: `Sum`, `MinValue`, `MaxValue`, or numeric `NumberOfDifferentValues`. The aggregate performs its ordinary encounter-ordered fold first; `Abs` then transforms the single folded number and its directional fillability, retaining the aggregate's derived scale. Thus `Abs(Sum(-3.25, 1.00))` is `2.25`, not `4.25`; all-empty Sum/extremum zero changes from bidirectional to grow-only magnitude, while all-empty distinct-count zero was already grow-only and stays so. A formally unavailable aggregate member remains unknown in validation and poisons the wrapped computation and dependent reads. This is the ordinary numeric-child gate and wrapper evaluator, not operand-list `Min`/`Max` syntax or a separate aggregate fold. *(Source-verified in `CheckFeldListenOpImpl`/`CheckFeldListenAnzahlOpImpl`/`CheckFeldOperOpImpl`; dynamic Groovy + generated Java + interpreter locked by `AggregateAbsDiffTest`, JVM/Node by `AggregateAbsTest`.)*

## NumberOfValueInFields (family 6, 2026-06-07)

> Fold: folded → §2.

`NumberOfValueInFields(value IN f1, f2, …)` (DE `AnzahlWertInFeldliste`) — the count of the listed fields whose value equals a constant (the kernel `EntityListOperationName.NumberOfValueInFields`, carried as an **`IEntityListOperationWithValue`**: the constant rides on `getConstantOperation()`, the fields on `getEntityList()`). Returns a number. The constant is type-flexible — a **boolean** (`True IN OptInA, OptInB, OptInC`, an `IBoolConstantOperation`), a **number**, a **string**, or a **date** (an `IConstantOperation` typed by `getType()`). The kernel requires **at least two fields** (or at least one group): a single field draws `MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALIDN`. The `IEntityListOperationWithValue` is a subtype of `IEntityListOperation`, so the converter intercepts it at the top of `convertAggregate` (before the name switch, which would otherwise reject it). Modeled as `NumberOfValueInFields` (Calculation, kind NUMBER) carrying the constant as a literal `Calculation` (BoolLiteral/NumberLiteral/StringLiteral/DateLiteral) + the field operands; the `In` is a grammar literal (like the value-list `In`). Authored via `Dsl.numberOfValueInFields`. *(Locked: `adapter.FilledCountReadTest.roundTripsNumberOfValueInFields` + BA-corpus `NumValueInFields`; N7 catalog example.)*

**Runtime semantics + interpreter status (2026-06-24).** The kernel (`RuntimeController.anzahlWertInFeldlisteIntern`) counts only **filled** fields whose value equals the constant: a field that is **empty** (`isWertAngegeben == FALSE`) is **NOT counted** — even when the constant is `0`, an empty NUMBER does **not** count as `0` (this differs from empty-as-0 in a comparison); it only marks the count growable (omission-typed). A field whose value is **UNKNOWN** (a formal error) makes the whole count non-evaluable → the rule is suppressed. The interpreter reproduces this exactly (`Interpreter.numberOfValueInFields`: skip un-`filled` fields, an `Unknown` operand → `Unknown`), and the parser handles the `In` grammar literal in `readArgs`. *(Interpreter-locked: `adapter.laws.NumberOfValueInFieldsDiffTest` — the empty-not-counted catcher (`>= 2` over one matching + one empty) against the live kernel; the fuzz net adds a `NumberOfValueInFields` rule — `PerfCatalog.fuzz()`.)*

**§12 supplement — the filtered count is growable on any filter, but shrinkable only through a MATCHED filtered cell (source + differential, both strategies, 2026-07-23; SPEC-2026-07-23-12).** The result POLARITY of `anzahlWertInFeldlisteIntern` mirrors the entity-count `containsFilteredValue` rule (§12 supplement / IF114) with a **value-match gate**: over a `Having`-filtered operand every filter makes the count `kannGroesserWerden` unconditionally (`rtIdContainer.getFilter() != null`), plus an empty cell or the omitted declared tail (`setzeEndIndicesAufMaxGesetzt`); but `containsFilteredValue` — the *both-directional* `(kannGroesser=true, kannKleiner=true)` return — is set **only when a cell that MATCHED the constant (`v.equals(wert)`) came through the filter**. So the two separators, both stored BELOW the count: a filtered current **match** is shrinkable → **OMISSION**, while a filtered selected **non-match** leaves a **grow-only zero** → **VALUE** (the count cannot shrink to a lower stored value; only changing the value helps). Stored ABOVE, both are OMISSION (the count can grow). An **unfiltered** star is grow-only via its omitted tail / empty cells and is *never* shrinkable. Number equality is scale-19 (`VkBigDecimal.equals`: `5` matches both `5` and `5.00`); textual families match by exact evaluated/stored projection (`"A"` ≠ `"a"`). The first reached formal unavailability (a kept malformed cell → UNKNOWN, or a non-relevant U-star) suppresses the whole count → the referencing comparison does not fire (the cell's own formal error still surfaces). The scalar authoring matrix (Number/String/Enumeration/Boolean/Confirm) was already correct at its owners; the observed divergence was interpreter-only — the value route rejected a starred operand outright and the polarity had no filter separator (INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF215). *(Locked: `adapter.laws.NumberOfValueInFieldsPolarityDiffTest` — the two separators, unfiltered-star grow-only both directions, filter-drops-then-matches, no-rows tail, exact token identity, and first-formal-cause, tri-checked groovy-dynamic == java-static == interpreter; scale-normalized equality in `adapter.laws.NumberOfValueInFieldsDiffTest`; multiplatform `interpreter … NumberOfValueInFieldsPolarityTest`, JVM + Node.)*

**`ValueAsDate` is implemented (2026-06-07) — the third coercion, the partially-known-date resolver.** `ValueAsDate(field, FirstDay|LastDay)` resolves a **partially known date** to a concrete date (the earliest / latest real date matching the known parts; e.g. `ValueAsDate(00.02.2024, FirstDay) = 01.02.2024`, `LastDay = 29.02.2024`). A *partially known date* is a `DateType` field whose **date precision** is other than `FULL` — the kernel `IDateType.DatePrecision` enum `{FULL, DAY_OPTIONAL, MONTH_OPTIONAL, YEAR_OPTIONAL}`, serialized as the DM-JSON key `datePrecision` on the `DateType` object (omitted for `FULL`). A plain `DateType` and a `DATE_FRAGMENT` both draw `MVK_INVALID_DATE_TYPE_FOR_OP`. The kernel node is an **`IFieldIdOperationWithParameters`** with `getOperatorName() == ValueAsDate` and `isFirstDay()` carrying the day specification (`FirstDay`/`LastDay`, DE `ErsterTag`/`LetzterTag` — localized keywords). Returns a DATE, comparable like any date. To author the fixture, **modelkit gained date-precision support**: `dateField(…, d -> d.datePrecision(DatePrecision.DAY_OPTIONAL))` (a new `modelkit.DatePrecision` enum) emits `datePrecision` in the `DateType` JSON. Modeled as `ValueAsDate` (Calculation, kind DATE); authored via `Dsl.valueAsDateFirstDay`/`valueAsDateLastDay`. *(Locked: `adapter.DateTimeExprReadTest.roundTripsValueAsDate` + BA-corpus `ValueAsDateRule`; N7 catalog examples for `ValueAsDate`/`FirstDay`/`LastDay`. The order fixture gained a `DAY_OPTIONAL` date field `ApproxDate`.)*
- **Date-range bound extraction** — `StartOfDateRange(range)`/`EndOfDateRange(range)` (the kernel's `IDateRangeExtractionOperation`) yield the interval's bound **dates**, which compare or feed a date component extraction (`DayFromDate(EndOfDateRange(range))`). A `DATE_RANGE` field is an interval of two dates; a **valid kernel format** pairs a single date format with a `rangeSeparator` that can't be confused with it (e.g. `dd.MM.yyyy` + `-`; `yyyy-MM-dd` + `-` is ambiguous and rejected). Modeled as `DateRangeBound` (a DATE-valued Calculation) + `DateRangeRef` source (`BoundModel.dateRange`). The **overlap predicates** are modeled too: `DateRangesOverlap(r1, r2, …)` (a field-list predicate, reusing `FieldListPredicate`) and `AtLeastOneDateRangeOverlaps(range IN r1, r2, …)` (a field-vs-field-list predicate, reusing `FieldListEntityListCondition`). All locked by `adapter.DateRangeReadTest`. The `BaseYear` point-in-time as a range source (`StartOfDateRange(BaseYear)` = 01.01, `EndOfDateRange(BaseYear)` = 31.12 of the reference year) is also modeled — `Dsl.startOfBaseYear()`/`endOfBaseYear()`, §6 supplement, `adapter.PointInTimeSourceReadTest`.

## §9 supplement — per-row iteration + aggregation of missing rows (runtime, 2026-06-06)

> Fold: folded → §9.

The first **runtime** (fires/does-not-fire) checks of §9, via the multi-row `RuntimeLaws.Doc` builder:

- **Per-row iteration.** A rule whose error field reaches *through* a repeatable group is evaluated **once per existing row**, and each message attaches to **that row** (the pointer carries the row index). Rows iterate in **document order** — the model-element sort metadata (`IGroup.getSortInfo`) is emitted into the generated META model but consumed **nowhere** in `kernel-core-runtime`/`kernel-core-calc` (source-swept 2026-06-26, INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF22): sort order is an editor/UI concern, inert for evaluation. Verified: rule in `/Order`, error field `Items/Count`, condition `[Items/Count] > 50` over three rows `10/80/90` → fires **twice**, on `/Order/Items/Count [1, 2, 1]` and `[1, 3, 1]` (rows 2 and 3); rows `10/20/30` → fires nowhere.
- **Aggregation of missing rows.** `Sum` substitutes `0` for an empty field (so empties neither error nor suppress the aggregate): `Sum(Items*/Count)` over `40, _, 40` is `80`; over an all-empty list is `0`. `MaxValue` ignores empties: over `40, _, 10` it is `40`.
- **`GroupFilled` = the row exists, not that it has data.** `GroupFilled(Items)` fires for a created Items row whose `Count` is empty, and not when there is no Items row at all.
- **`RepetitionNotUnique`** (no `*` wildcard — the asterisk draws `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED`) fires on each duplicate row of a non-index field: `Count` values `5, 5, 9` → fires on rows 1 and 2, not row 3. The **index field** already carries *built-in* uniqueness — duplicate index values raise the kernel's own `uniqueIndex` formal error regardless of any author rule, so `RepetitionNotUnique` on the index field is redundant.
  - **Placement is exacting (validate-time, verified 2026-06-22, probe F06-1).** The rule must sit at the **PARENT** of the repeated group with the error field **inside** it — `RepetitionNotUnique(Items/Sku)` is accepted only with rule group `/Order` + error field `/Order/Items/Sku` (verified VALID). The CLI's default grouping (rule group = the error field's own parent) puts the rule *at* `/Order/Items`, where the same condition draws **`MVK_INVALID_ENTITY`**; with a non-repeatable error field (rule at `/Order`, field `/Order/CustomerName`) it draws **`MVK_NO_WILDCARD`** — so `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` here is *not* a "missing asterisk" (the operator forbids one) but a wrong-scope signal. Author it with the rule group at the parent — `rule check --group /Order --field /Order/Items/Sku` — and **persist with the same `group` key** (`rule add` / `apply rule add` accept it, mirroring `rule check --group`, so a checked rule round-trips; F06-3, 2026-06-22). `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` is a **shared** code (also CurrentRepetition, aggregates, model-scope `GroupFilled`), so its enriched fix is generic — KERNEL-INTEGRATION-NOTES "Structural-code fix hints" (F06-2). The catalog (`RepetitionNotUnique` gotchas) and `FieldValuesNotUnique(Items*/Sku)` (the asterisked, no-`--group` alternative for plain cross-row uniqueness) are the authoring paths.
- **Parallel iteration** — two repeatable groups keyed by a shared index field are iterated jointly as an **outer join** over the index values; a value present on one side only counts as **"not specified"** on the other. Verified on an own-domain `staffing` fixture (`Demand`/`Supply`, both keyed by `Department`): `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled(Demand/Headcount, Supply/Headcount)` with Demand=`{Sales, Eng}` but Supply=`{Sales}` fires once, on the `Eng` Demand row (its Supply side is not specified); when every Department is matched on both sides it fires nowhere. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.RepetitionLawsTest`, `adapter.laws.RepetitionNotUniqueLawsTest`, `adapter.laws.ParallelIterationLawsTest`; the exact-match interpreter differential is `adapter.laws.ParallelIterationDiffTest`.)*
  - **The iteration value-set is the UNION of the groups' index-field values** — source-confirmed (`ValidationCache.getIterationValuesForGroups` collects each group's `IndexFieldCache` value→repetition map keys into a sorted set). For each value, each group resolves to the row carrying it, or `IIdentifier.UNKNOWN` if absent.
  - **The unmatched side's pointer carries `IIdentifier.UNKNOWN = -5`** as its repetition index — a fixed sentinel, NOT a fresh counter. So an outer-join fire whose *error field's own group* has no row for the value points at e.g. `/Staffing[1]/Demand[-5]/Headcount` (probed; reproduced exactly). Polarity is the ordinary empty-operand rule: an iteration with an unspecified operand types **OMISSION**, all-specified types **VALUE**.
  - **An INVALID index value (duplicate or empty) is a 3VL corner.** A duplicate value still sits in the index cache (last write wins) and the union, but the kernel marks the group's index *invalid* for that parent (`IndexFieldCache.isIndexGroupInvalidForOneRepetition`); references into a group with an invalid index then read as **3-valued UNKNOWN** for unmatched values — which *suppresses* a negative operator like `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled` (it fires only on a definite "not filled"). Probed asymmetry: a clean A-only value (error field filled, sibling missing) fires, but the same shape with an invalid index in the *sibling* group does **not** (the sibling's reference is UNKNOWN, not "empty"). Operator-specific, but fully reproducible — the interpreter models it exactly (`Document.parallelContexts` marks a group invalid for a parent when it has a duplicate/empty index there, and `ParallelRowContext` reads that group's unmatched references as `Val.Unknown` / `presenceUnknown` → the Kleene logic suppresses the negative operator). See INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF19 for the interpreter treatment.
  - **Topology laws (probed 2026-06-25):** non-repeatable **intermediate** groups between the join and the keyed groups are transparent — wrapped and asymmetric-depth shapes are valid, and the kernel renders the transparent level as `[1]` in the pointer; a **repeatable non-indexed FRAME** above exactly ONE joined group is kernel-valid too. The probed **invalid** shapes: a repeatable frame above the non-error joined group → `MVK_NO_WILDCARD`; an **indexed** repeatable intermediate → `MVK_MULTIPLE_INDEX_FIELDS`; a negative operator over a frame → `MVK_NEG_CONDITION_IN_ITERATION`; a joined group **without an index field** is rejected (no key to join on). *(Locked via `adapter.laws.ParallelIterationDiffTest`'s shape matrix; interpreter treatment IF19.)*

## §9 supplement — a rule/computation's ITERATION SCOPE is its set of referenced repeatable fields, not its node placement (corrected + matrix-verified, 2026-06-21)

> Fold: folded → §9.

> **Correction (2026-06-21):** an earlier note here claimed a `field move` *into or out of* a repeatable group is rejected `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_IN_RULEGROUP` and motivated a planned "co-move the bound rule/computation" enhancement. A full directional matrix (authored via `rule add`/`computation add`, validated through the kernel) shows that was **directionally wrong** and the enhancement is a **non-goal**. The verified truth follows.

A rule's **error field** and a computation's **computed field** are stored as a *relative* path (`errorEntityRelPath` / `computedFieldRelPath` = `"../F"`). But the kernel derives a rule/computation's **iteration scope from the repeatable fields it *references*** (the error/computed field **plus** every per-row operand), **not** from where the rule/computation node physically sits. The law: the error/computed field must **share the iteration scope** of the referenced repeatable operands. (Locked by `validate/laws/IterationLawsTest.errorFieldMustShareTheIterationScopeOfTheReferencedRepeatableFields`.)

Consequence for a reference-rewriting **`field move`** of F (matrix-verified, symmetric for rules *and* computations):

| `field move` of F | bound rule/computation (error/computed field = F) | why |
|---|---|---|
| → **into** a repeatable; the rule/comp node is at the outer scope | **accepted** — node stays put, reaches *down* (e.g. `errorEntityRelPath ../Items/DeliveryDate`, `computedFieldRelPath ../Rep/X`); the error/computed field then drives the per-row iteration | the error field inside the repeatable *is* the iteration scope; per-row operands resolve consistently |
| → **out of** a repeatable; the rule/comp references **only** F + outer/constant fields | **accepted** — node stays put, reaches *out* (`../../Qty`); nothing forces per-row iteration any more | no referenced operand is per-row, so error field + operands are all in one (outer) scope |
| → **out of** a repeatable; the rule/comp **also** references another field still **inside** the repeatable | **rejected** `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_IN_RULEGROUP` (or `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` on the operand side) | a genuine **cross-cardinality split**: F is now single-valued while a referenced operand stays per-row |

So a `field move` is rejected **only** when it would genuinely split a rule/computation across two cardinalities — and that case is **not mechanically fixable**: relocating the node ("co-move") leaves the cross-scope operand reference intact, and the *only* valid repair is to **rewrite the rule's logic** (e.g. wrap the per-row operand in an aggregate: `X = [Rep/P] * 2` → `X = Sum(Rep*/P)`), a modeling decision the tool must not invent. The existing reference-rewriting `field move` therefore already accepts every *semantically valid* cross-boundary move and correctly refuses the genuinely-broken ones; **`field move` co-move adds no capability → non-goal** (CLI-SPEC §6; FEATURE-GAPS §4). *(All behavior here is the kernel's; rulekit just lets the kernel's gate refuse the split, with `model check` as backstop.)*

## §9 supplement — the kernel tolerates an index field on a non-repeatable group (probe-observed, 2026-06-21)

> Fold: not-foldable — a structural tolerance we deliberately refuse to consume (prose-only by design); no canonical evaluation claim.

A group's `indexFieldName` (the repetition row-key) is **not** kernel-gated on repeatability: a `repeatability: 1` (single) group carrying an `indexFieldName` that names a direct-child field passes `checkConsistency` (probed via raw DM-JSON + `model check` → `valid: true`).
