But it is a **degenerate** row-key — with no repetitions, the uniqueness check (`RepetitionNotUnique`) it would arm is vacuous and parallel iteration has nothing to iterate. So **modelkit/CLI deliberately forbid it** (`ModelDocument.setIndexField` / `group modify --index-field` require a repeatable group): the index field only earns its keep on a repetition list. **Prose-only by design** (the consumer for the kernel-tolerance fact is nobody — we refuse the degenerate case; *our* behavior is locked by `GroupModifyVerbTest.indexFieldOnANonRepeatableGroupIsRejected`, the G24 surface). This resolves the SME 1184 question (the non-repeatable allowance) — the kernel allows it, we don't, on purpose.

## §9 supplement — field/group predicates are type-directional

> Fold: not-foldable — authoring-tier type-direction codes; the enclosing-group-reference half is canonical in §9's error-field rule.

`GroupFilled(field)` → **`MVK_NO_FIELDS_ALLOWED`** ("Only groups or root groups are allowed"); `FieldFilled(group)` → **`MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED`**. Also re-confirmed experimentally: an enclosing group reference (`GroupFilled(G)`) satisfies the error-field-referenced rule for a field inside `G` — a bare `GroupFilled(ShippingAddress)` condition with error field `ShippingAddress/Street` validates. *(Locked: N7 `GroupFilled`/`FieldFilled` invalid examples + `RuleAuthoringTest.acceptsAnEnclosingGroupReferenceAsErrorFieldReference`.)*

## §9 supplement — repeatability interactions (2026-06-04 experiments)

> Fold: not-foldable — authoring-tier rejection codes; the guarded-negativity and asterisk laws they evidence are canonical in §9/§10.

- **`MVK_NEG_CONDITION_IN_ITERATION` triggers on iteration × unguarded negativity** — reproduced: with the error field under repeatable `Addons` (the rule iterates), both `GroupNotFilled(Addons)` and a bare `FieldNotFilled(Addons/MonthlyFee)` are rejected; the guarded form `GroupFilled(Addons) And FieldNotFilled(Addons/MonthlyFee)` passes. The trigger is the *rule's iteration* (error-field position) combined with a negative condition — not the predicate's group argument per se.
- **A repeatable group referenced from a *non-iterating* rule needs the asterisk** — `FieldFilled(Tier) And GroupNotFilled(Addons)` with error field `Tier` is rejected with **`MVK_NO_WILDCARD`** ("Missing specification of an asterisk for 'Addons'"). A previously unknown law; pre-checkable once the iteration model lands (rulekit knows both the error-field iteration and the group's repeatability).
- **Root groups work with both group predicates** — `GroupFilled(/Subscription)` and `GroupNotFilled(/Subscription)` (non-repeatable root, non-iterating rule) both validate; the kernel parses root-group references into a distinct AST node (`IRootGroupCondition`) but the surface syntax is uniform.

*(Lock status (sweep 2026-06-10, was "no locks yet"): the `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` position law is locked in `validate.laws.IterationLawsTest` (and slice 7f showed a naive missing-asterisk pre-check would false-positive under parallel iteration — deliberately kernel-delegated); the unguarded-negativity case is pre-warned by the linter (`lint.LinterTest`) and stays kernel-delegated per the §"negativity law" note; both codes are classified in `MVK-LEDGER.md`.)*

## §9 supplement — `GroupFilled` splits on repeatability (source-read `GroupFillCache` + differential, 2026-06-23)

> Fold: folded → §9.

`GroupFilled(G)` is **not** one rule — it depends on whether `G` is repeatable (kernel `GroupFillCache` / `ValidationCache`):

- **Repeatable `G`**: a row is filled once it is **instantiated** — creating the row is itself content (the repetition index is meaningful). A *created-but-empty* repeat row → `GroupFilled` **TRUE**. (A filled descendant also instantiates the row, so "row exists" subsumes "descendant filled".)
- **Non-repeatable `G`**: filled only when it has **content** — a filled descendant **field**, or (the 2026-07-06 refinement, the same "content" notion as the §2/§4 supplements) an **instantiated repeatable descendant ROW**: `GroupFilled(Box)` over a non-repeatable `Box` holding three created-but-empty repeatable `Items` rows is **TRUE** (INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF53). Its own structural presence — an explicitly-created empty instance with no content below — is **not** content → `GroupFilled` **FALSE**.

This is why the `relativeToParent` requiredness gotcha (§4) flips by parent repeatability, and it resolves the earlier "GroupFilled on an empty created repeat row" open edge (interpreter was descendant-filled-only; now matches). *(Interpreter parity locked via the requiredness gate: `adapter.laws.RequirednessDiffTest` (the created-empty-repeat-row case fires `mandatoryField`; the non-repeatable empty-parent case does not).)*

## §4/§9 supplement — group content and group error are independent validation dimensions (source + tri-differential, 2026-07-21)

> Fold: folded → §4 + §9.

The validation pipeline admits a field value to the evaluation/group-fill cache only after formal conversion succeeds, while it records rejected values separately as erroneous entities. Consequently a non-repeatable group is not described by one Boolean. Under full relevance the exact matrix is: clean empty → `GroupFilled` FALSE and `GroupNotFilled` TRUE; invalid-only → FALSE and UNKNOWN; valid-only → TRUE and FALSE; valid plus invalid → TRUE and UNKNOWN. Positive group-list quantifiers count admitted content; negative tallies count only clean empty groups, leaving erroneous groups in neither bucket. The plain multi-group `NumberOfFilledGroups` is stricter: any erroneous group makes the count unavailable rather than silently counting only the admitted subset. A duplicate index value is admitted before uniqueness marks it erroneous, so it contributes content and error simultaneously. In partial validation, one relevant descendant makes each ancestor partially relevant: that is enough to prove positive presence, but an empty group proves negative presence only when the whole group is relevant (an explicit group/ancestor selection or complete descendant coverage). A created repeatable row remains structural ancestor content even when over-limit; the over-limit diagnostics and ordinary-row suppression are separate. The same admitted-content cache gates `relativeToParent`: an invalid-only parent does not activate an empty required sibling. *(Locked across dynamic Groovy, generated Java, and the interpreter by `GroupPresenceFormalInvalidDiffTest` + `GroupPresenceAdmissionDiffTest`.)*

## §9 supplement — wildcard slot laws (slice 7a, 2026-06-04 experiments)

> Fold: not-foldable — authoring-tier slot laws (which slots accept a star); the propagation and negativity laws they evidence are canonical in §9/§10.

The `*` suffix is per path segment (grammar `idNameRef: idName '*'?`) and builds a **repetition list**; whether it is legal depends purely on the *slot*:

- **List slots accept starred paths**: the field-list quantifiers (`AtLeastOneFieldFilled(Addons*/Name)` …), shape-B value lists (`…(Addons*/Name In "x")`), and the count/`Sum` aggregates — iterating rule or not, and **mixing plain and starred operands in one predicate is legal**.
- **Scalar slots reject them** with **`MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED`** ("It is not allowed to use an asterisk"): `FieldFilled(Addons*/Name)`, a bracketed value `[Addons*/Fee]`, `GroupFilled(Addons*)`, and shape A's single-field slot.
- A star on a **non-repeatable** group is its own code: **`MVK_INVALID_WILDCARD`** ("Since 'X' is not repeatable…").
- **The negativity law (`MVK_NEG_CONDITION_IN_ITERATION`) is not an operator list** — the kernel's `IterationNotValidVisitor` (~800 lines) classifies each condition as positive/negative *in context*: the same operator flips depending on whether its operands reference the iteration (e.g. `FieldFilled(A)` is "negative" when `A` does *not* iterate but the rule does), and aggregate comparisons against constants are negativity-sensitive by comparison direction (`< / <= / != n`, `== 0`-style forms). rulekit deliberately does **not** replicate this — kernel-delegated to F4.
- **`MVK_NO_WILDCARD` (missing star) is not naively pre-checkable**: under *parallel iteration* (joined repeatable groups via same-name/type index fields) plain references to another repeatable group are legal — the pre-check waits for slice 7e's iteration model.
- **Wildcard propagation down nested repeatables (§10, 2026-06-06):** when a path flattens an outer repeatable with `*`, **every repeatable group below it on the path must also be starred**. Verified on the nested-repeatable `project` fixture (Milestones ▸ Tasks): `Sum(Milestones*/Tasks/Effort)` (outer `*`, plain inner) → **`MVK_NO_WILDCARD`**; `Sum(Milestones*/Tasks*/Effort)` accepted; and a single `*` over the outer repeatable's own field (`Sum(Milestones*/Budget)`) is fine.
- **Keyword-named fields need single-quoting in a path (§10):** a field literally named like a language keyword (the `project` fixture has one named `Date`) is rejected unquoted — `FieldFilled(Date)` → **`MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN`** — and accepted single-quoted: `FieldFilled('Date')`.
- **Relative `..` + `*`: the star may not be on a `..` part (§10).** The kernel message is precise — *"In a relative path containing `..`-notation, the `..` parts may not use the `*` symbol."* So `../Items*/Count` (star on a *named* segment) is fine, but **starring the `..` itself** — `Sum(..*/Budget)` from an inner rule, to reach all repetitions of an ancestor repeatable — is rejected with **`MVK_INVALID_WILDCARD_REL`**; use the absolute form (`/Project/Milestones*/Budget`). (This is why my earlier shapes, with `*` on named segments, all passed.)
- **A bare `[Name]` ref resolves DECLARING-GROUP-FIRST, then falls back to a model-wide UNIQUE field short name — gated by `fieldRefByShortNameAllowed` (§10, source 2026-07-11).** The condition parser's `CheckIdNameRefImpl.getEntityFromConditionText` (`kernel-core-parser`, run at model conversion/expand, per path segment) resolves a bare name in a fixed order: (1) build the candidate `<declaringGroup>/<name>` (or the literal for an absolute `/`-path) and look it up in the entity container — **the declaring group wins**; (2) **only if that misses**, *and the ref is not absolute*, *and `modelConfig.fieldRefByShortNameAllowed` is on*, search `shortName2FullName(name)` — a multimap over **FIELDS only** (`flattenToStream().filter(isField)`) — where **>1 match throws `MVK_FIELDNAME_NOT_UNIQUE`** (it never picks one), exactly **1** resolves, and **0** falls through to `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY`. So the flag enables ONLY the cross-group fallback; a same-group `[Quantity]` resolves via step (1) regardless of the flag (which is why an earlier probe saw `[Quantity]` resolve with the flag off — that was the declaring-group case, not the fallback). Resolution is a load-time BINDING, but the **serialized condition RETAINS the short name** (`FormatterVisitor` re-emits it when the flag is on and the name is unique — `PathNotationConfig.isShortName`), so an expanded model reaching a downstream consumer still carries the bare form and must re-resolve it. Default is **off** (`DocumentModelConfig.fieldRefByShortNameAllowed = false`). *(Interpreter: the parser's `RefResolver` (built by `ParsedModel` from `ModelDef.entityPaths`/`fieldsByShortName` + the flag) reproduces exactly this order; ambiguous/absent stays unresolved → the loud "no kind declared", never a silent wrong value — INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF119, graduated from IG57. Locked `adapter.laws.ShortNameRefDiffTest` (fires/does-not-fire) + `validate.laws.PathLawsTest` (unique accept, flag-off `MVK_INVALID_ENTITY`, ambiguous `MVK_FIELDNAME_NOT_UNIQUE`) + multiplatform `interpreter.ShortNameRefTest`.)*
- **The kernel ships a reference-rewriting engine for rename/move — `MoveSupportDM` (§10, 2026-06-12).** `MoveSupportDM.changePathReferencesForMove(documentModel, Map<oldPath,newPath>)` (internal, `@OnlyForUsage`, `kernel-md-model/a12internal/services`) rewrites every reference to the moved paths — rule conditions, error texts, computations (preconditions/operations/common precondition), the error/computed-field refs, **and a group's index-field and sort-field relative references** — preserving absolute-vs-relative style. (The index/sort-field rewrite is a no-op when a non-repeatable group is merely re-parented — the relative path to its own child is unchanged — but it matters for a repeatable group; the kernel handles it from the same `entityMoves` map either way, one more reason to rewrite references through this engine rather than by hand on the JSON.) It does **not** move the element itself — the caller does that afterward, confirmed by the method's own javadoc (*"the rules and computations themselves are NOT moved … Moving … must be done by the caller afterward"*); rulekit's `ModelDocument.moveField/moveGroup` is that caller-side relocation, kept in our authored-JSON surgery so source fidelity (comments/ordering) survives. **Starred-group references DO follow a group rename:** `Sum(Items* / Amount)` becomes `Sum(Lines* / Amount)` when `/Order/Items` → `/Order/Lines`, via dedicated `starredGroupsNewFullnames` handling — so `MoveSupportInternal`'s blanket javadoc limitation #6 ("References with * are not touched") is **misleading** for the group-rename case (narrower `*` edge cases may still not rewrite; the kernel re-validate is the backstop). rulekit wraps this at the N8 boundary as `adapter.ReferenceRewriter`; it is the engine that turns *refuse-if-referenced* rename into a *reference-preserving* rename/move (FEATURE-GAPS). *(Locked: `adapter.ReferenceRewriteTest`.)*
- **`getDirectlyReferencedEntities` is the canonical "what does this rule reference" set — operands included, not just the error-attached field (§9/§10).** The kernel's `getDirectlyReferencedEntities(IRule, IProblemReporter)` returns **every** field/group a rule's condition reads (the operands), and for a calculation rule it spans all four sites: the computed field, the common precondition, each alternative's precondition, **and each operation expression** — so a field read *only* inside a computation's operation is in the set even though a plain rule scan would miss it. This one primitive is the shared oracle for all three reference queries, which is why they can never disagree: **`where-used`** is its inverse (`whereUsed` over validation rules + `whereUsedInComputations` over calculations — kept as separate kinds because a calculation is also an `IRule` in `getAllRules()`), **`rule explain`/`computation explain`'s `references`** is its forward projection, and the **safe-delete cascade** is its membership test. It needs no rulekit converter coverage (it's the kernel's own answer over the bound rule), so it works even for `Opaque`-to-rulekit conditions. rulekit treatment + the consumer-facing contract (the referrer/referent symmetry, delete-cascade vs rename/move-rewrite, the query verbs): CLI-SPEC §6 "What counts as a reference". *(Locked: `adapter.WhereUsedInComputationsTest` — incl. `operandReferencedOnlyInAComputationIsFoundViaTheComputationLeg`; `cli.WhereUsedVerbTest`, `cli.RemoveVerbTest`.)*

*(Locked: `validate.laws.IterationLawsTest`; rulekit treatment: `FieldOperand` = `FieldRef` | `FieldListRef`, list slots take the operand union, scalar slots take `FieldRef` — `BoundModel.flatten(starPath)` validates star placement.)*

## §9 supplement — `CurrentRepetition` + the index-field/semantic-index cluster (slices 7e/7f, 2026-06-04 experiments; retitled 2026-06-10 — the constant-form semantic index IS supported, only the entity/`$` forms stay kernel-delegated)

> Fold: folded → §10.

`CurrentRepetition` (DE `AktuelleWiederholung`; grammar `groupOp: 'AktuelleWiederholung' '(' entitySpec ')'`) is a **numeric expression** — the current iteration index of a group.

- **Takes a group, not a field** — a field argument → **`MVK_NO_FIELDS_ALLOWED`** ("Only groups or root groups are allowed"). Structural: `Dsl.currentRepetition(GroupRef)`.
- **The rule must iterate over that group** — from a non-iterating rule it draws **`MVK_NO_WILDCARD`** ("Missing specification of an asterisk"). Position-dependent → kernel-delegated (like the RNU position law).
- **A non-repeatable group is tolerated** (its index is constant) — `CurrentRepetition(/Subscription/Billing)` from an iterating rule validates.

**No positional repetition operator — a row is addressed by semantic *key*, never by index (grammar-verified, 2026-06-16).** The grammar exposes exactly one `groupOp` (`groupOpName: 'AktuelleWiederholung'`) and one way to address a specific row — the `Fuer`/`For` **semantic index** (`entitySpec: … ('Fuer' semanticIndex)`, with `semanticIndex: entitySpec | StringLiteral`). So `CurrentRepetition` *yields* a row's index as a **number** (compare it, self-exclude via the `$` correlation), but you **cannot use a number to *select* a row**: there is no `[Field At n]`, no "current + 1" neighbour, no positional offset. `[Field For "key"]` keys on the index *field's value* (a string constant or another entity), not on a repetition position. Consequence for rulekit: a `date.atRepetition(n)`-style positional API is a non-starter — it would invent syntax the kernel lacks; the only repetition-indexed *date* read that could ever ship is the semantic-index `For` form, mirroring `NumberRef.forIndex`. The construct inventory is CI-locked by `adapter.KernelConstructParityTest` (operator set) + `GrammarProductionLedgerTest` (productions, opt-in vs the `../a12-kernel` checkout) — a kernel that later *added* a positional operator would trip them.

**Slice 7f (2026-06-04, with an index-field fixture).** A repeatable group declares an index field via **`"indexFieldName": "<containedField>"`** (own-domain fixtures `logistics.dm.json` — `Lines`/`Picks` keyed on `LineRef`; `order.dm.json` `Items` keyed on `Sku`). With that:
- **Semantic index `[Field For "value"]`** (DE `Fuer`) selects the repetition whose index field equals `value` — **works for a string constant**; the field's group must declare an `indexFieldName` else **`MVK_NO_INDEX_FIELD`**. An *entity* index that itself iterates is rejected (**`MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_INVALID_ITER`**). rulekit supports both key forms — the **constant** key (`forIndex(String)`) and the **field** key (`forIndex(FieldRef)` → `SemanticIndexFieldValue`, DG8) — on **any comparable field kind** (`NumberRef`/`StringRef`/`EnumRef`/`DateRef`/`TimeRef`/`DateTimeRef`; API-FINDINGS finding 55), read by `adapter.SemanticIndexReadTest` (number/field-keyed) + `adapter.SemanticIndexNonNumberReadTest` (string/enum/date). The `$`-correlated form stays kernel-delegated.
- **Parallel iteration needs no construct** — two repeatable groups sharing a same-name/type index field iterate jointly; a rule referencing both (e.g. `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled(Lines/OrderedQty, Picks/PickedQty)`, or a field-vs-field cross-compare) validates with *existing* handles. rulekit supports it by *not interfering*.
- **`MVK_NO_WILDCARD` (missing asterisk) confirmed correctly kernel-delegated**: under parallel iteration a *plain* cross-group reference is **legal** (`FieldFilled(OrderedQty) And FieldFilled(/Shipment/Picks/PickedQty)` validates) — so a naive "repeatable group needs `*`" pre-check would false-positive. The pre-check would require full iteration-model analysis (does the rule parallel-iterate?); deliberately left to F4.

*(Locked: N7 `SemanticIndex` valid + `MVK_NO_INDEX_FIELD` invalid; `dsl.operators.IterationConditionTest` (render) + `validate.laws.IterationLawsTest.parallelIterationOverIndexKeyedGroupsIsSupportedNotBlocked`.)*

**Runtime resolution (2026-06-10, RT-semantics drain; the no-match half CORRECTED by the 2026-06-24 probe):** `[Items/Count For "SKU-1"]` reads the `Count` of exactly the row whose index field (`Sku`) carries `"SKU-1"` — siblings are not read. The drain's original "no matching row ⇒ not evaluated" reading was **under-determined**: its lock compares `> 0`, a threshold where "reads 0" and "not evaluated" coincide; the 2026-06-24 `== 0` probe (the §9 SemanticIndex supplement above) separates them — a no-match reads the kind's **empty value** (empty-as-0 for a number) and fires OMISSION. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.SemanticIndexLawsTest` (row selection) + `SemanticIndexDiffTest` (the no-match empty read). NB `SemanticIndexLawsTest`'s in-code comment still words the no-match case as "not evaluated" — assertion-neutral but misleading; flagged for the next code-touching set.)*

**Operator-context restrictions + a BA-doc correction (source + probe, 2026-06-17).** The semantic index is **accepted inside `NumberOfFilledFields`** but **rejected inside `NumberOfFilledGroups` and `RepetitionNotUnique`** (`MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_NOT_ALLOWED`; source `CheckFeldListenAnzahlOpImpl` calls `checkNoSemanticIndex` in the `AnzahlAngegebenerKontexte` branch only, and `CheckWiederholungBedingungImpl`), and **rejected under a starred enclosing group** (`MVK_SEMANTIC_INDEX_AND_WILDCARD`). This **contradicts the BA reference** (L902, "not supported for `NumberOfFilledFields` and `RepetitionNotUnique`") — a *Felder/Kontexte* slip in the prose: the kernel rejects it for `NumberOfFilledGroups`, not `NumberOfFilledFields`. *(Locked: `validate.laws.SemanticIndexSlotLawsTest`.)*

*(Locked: N7 `CurrentRepetition` valid (iterating) + invalid (non-iterating `MVK_NO_WILDCARD`); the iterating-position law in `validate.laws.IterationLawsTest`. Groups-only is structural.)*

## §9 supplement — parallel iteration forbids a pure-negative condition (probe, 2026-06-17)

> Fold: folded → §9.

In a **parallel iteration** (a rule referencing two repeatable groups that share an index field), a **pure negative** condition is rejected with **`MVK_PARALLEL_ITERATION_INVALID`** — `NoFieldFilled(/Shipment/Lines/OrderedQty, /Shipment/Picks/PickedQty)` over the two index-keyed groups fails (probed on the logistics fixture). BA L919: a negative is allowed in a parallel iteration only **combined with a positive** condition (else it would require a value for every joined iteration) — the positive-bearing `FieldsNotCollectivelyFilled` form *is* accepted (locked alongside). Note the code is the parallel-specific `MVK_PARALLEL_ITERATION_INVALID`, **not** the ordinary-iteration `MVK_NEG_CONDITION_IN_ITERATION`. (Source `ParserService` raises it when `IterationNotValidVisitor.testParallelIteration` returns false — the `IterationType.PARALLEL` traversal whose `erlaubt` flag encodes the positive-required rule; the check is short-circuited for non-parallel conditions.) *(Locked: `validate.laws.IterationLawsTest.aPureNegativeInParallelIterationIsRejected`.)*

## §9 supplement — the `Having` filter and `$` correlation (slice 7d, 2026-06-04 experiments)

> Fold: folded → §9.

`Having` (DE `Mit`; grammar: `'Mit' bedingung` on an `entitySpec`) filters a `*`-repetition list before an aggregate/quantifier consumes it; `$` (`DOLLAR`, language-independent) pins a reference to the rule's outer repetition inside a filter.

- **`Having` requires a starred (flattened) field** — on a plain reference it draws **`MVK_WILDCARDS_REQUIRED`** ("At least one asterisk needs to be specified for the filtered field"). So it lives on `FieldListRef`, not on plain `FieldRef`.
- **It attaches in any list slot** — verified inside `Sum`/aggregates *and* the field-list quantifiers (`AtLeastOneFieldFilled(… Having …)`).
- **Filter-condition exclusions** → **`MVK_DISALLOWED_CONSTRUCT_IN_FILTER_CONDITION`** ("The language feature 'X' is not supported for filter conditions"): verified for `RepetitionNotUnique` and a **nested `Having`**; KERNEL-SEMANTICS §9 also lists `CustomCondition`, a semantic index, and a parallel iteration. rulekit pre-checks the two representable ones (RNU + nested Having); the rest stay kernel-delegated.
- **`$` is filter-only** — outside a filter it draws **`MVK_INVALID_OUTER_ITERATION`** ("The symbol '$' to specify rule iteration is only allowed inside filter conditions"). A valid use correlates inner to outer: `Sum(/S/Addons*/MonthlyFee Having [/S/Addons/Name] == [$/S/Addons/Name]) > 0`. (`$` on the *filtered* side without a non-`$` inner ref → `MVK_NO_ITERATION_FOR_WILDCARD` — the filter must reference a field in a starred level.)
- **Why `$` exists — `MVK_INVALID_ITERATION_IN_FILTER_CONDITION`** (from the kernel-team BA doc `a12-kernel/documentation/_merged/kernel-ba.md`, cross-checked 2026-06-07). Inside a filter you may only iterate over the repetition levels the **filtered field** stars; a reference to a field that iterates over a *different* group is rejected — `Sum(K*/G Having [K/F1] == [L/F2])` errors because `L/F2` iterates over the un-starred group `L`. The **only** fix is the `$`: `[$L/F2]` pins that reference to the outer iteration. So `$` is not optional sugar — it is the sole way a filter references an out-of-scope (outer) field. **Path notation is unrestricted** (any path valid without `$` is valid with it: `$/A/F`, `$L/F`, `$F`).
- **`$` correlation is implemented (2026-06-07, both directions) — was "deferred", now landed.** The marker is an `outer` flag on the AST value nodes (`FieldValue`/`EnumCategoryAccess`/`CurrentRepetition`), set by `.outer()` on the handles, rendered as `[$path]`/`($group)`. It threads through every field-vs-field comparison kind (number/string/date/enum/category), `CurrentRepetition($group)`, **presence predicates** (`NoFieldFilled($a, $b)` via `FieldRef.outer`), **value-list operands** (`FieldListRef.outer`), and filtered field-list quantifiers (`AtLeastOneFieldFilled(… Having …)`) — **every `$` position in Corpus-1 converts (0 `$` opaques)**. **Runtime-verified, not just structural:** the correlation fires on the rows that share an outer-row value and nowhere when distinct; the `CurrentRepetition(G) != CurrentRepetition($G)` self-exclusion is load-bearing (without it every row matches itself); and combined with `CurrentRepetition(G) < CurrentRepetition($G)` it expresses "all predecessors" — the kernel BA doc's reviewed "holidays not in chronological order" example, transferred to a schedule domain. Corpus-1 read reached 95.0% on the back of this.
- **The capture is the complete NAMED outer repetition environment, not one scalar index (source + differential, both strategies, 2026-07-20).** `IterationState` retains the whole repetition-index vector and outer state; a `$`-marked reference resolves the coordinate belonging to its own named group path. In a rule instance at parent row 1 / child row 2, `CurrentRepetition($Parent)` is 1 while `CurrentRepetition($Parent/Child)` is 2. A focused one-ancestor-star filter requires a candidate parent index to equal both captured coordinates: the diagonal child row 1 selects parent candidate 1 and fires, while child row 2 selects none. The fixture also has a marker-bearing parent row 2 but no outer `Probe` there: a faulty one-scalar capture would bind both marked paths to child coordinate 2, select that marker, and falsely fire child 2. Groovy-dynamic, generated Java, and the interpreter agree. This establishes the full-environment rule for named levels without inferring a broader multi-star behavior beyond the tested topology. *(Interpreter: the existing `havingInterp` passes the complete outer `EvalContext`, and `CurrentRepetition` resolves `repsOf(itsOwnPath).last()`; no behavior change. Locked: `adapter.laws.CapturedOuterEnvironmentDiffTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF178.)*

*(Locked: N7 `Having` valid + `MVK_DISALLOWED_CONSTRUCT_IN_FILTER_CONDITION` invalid + `FieldListRef.having` pre-checks — `dsl.operators.IterationConditionTest`. `$` authoring kernel-valid — `adapter.OuterCorrelationAuthoringTest`; read round-trips — `adapter.OuterCorrelationReadTest`/`OuterCorrelationQuantifierReadTest`; the N8 count-check that keeps an un-threaded construct Opaque — `adapter.OuterCorrelationBackstopTest`; the **runtime behavior** — `adapter.laws.CorrelationLawsTest`. `Having`'s starred-field law stays structural (only `FieldListRef` offers it).)*

## §9 supplement — list-aggregate slot laws (slice 7c, 2026-06-04 experiments)

> Fold: not-foldable — authoring-tier slot laws; the missing-row semantics they accompany are canonical in §9.

`Sum`/`MaxValue`/`MinValue` (DE `Summe`/`MaxWert`/`MinWert`; grammar `feldListenOp` — its sibling `SumOfProducts` is now implemented, `adapter.SumOfProductsReadTest`; `FirstFilledValue` is implemented for number/date/string/enum and preserves a kernel-legal ordered list of multiple flattened and plain operands, `adapter.FirstFilledValueReadTest` and `adapter.MultiOperandFirstFilledValueReadTest`) and `NumberOfDifferentValues` (DE `AnzahlVerschiedenerWerte`; third member of the count family):

- **Param law is shared with the count aggregates** — more than one field or a group (`MVK_PARAMSIZE_INVALIDN`); **a single flattened (`*`) operand satisfies it** (it expands to many fields), so `Sum(Items*/Count)` is the canonical shape. Mixed starred + plain operands and mixed scales are legal.
- **Only repeated DIRECT operands are rejected — a wildcarded operand may repeat** (SPEC-2026-07-22-07, kernel-lean; verified 2026-07-22). A repeated direct field `Sum(A, A)` draws **`MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM1`** (fires before the type checks; applies to the whole entity-list family — `Sum`/`MaxValue`/`MinValue`/`FirstFilledValue`/`NumberOfFilledFields`/`NumberOfDifferentValues`). But outside HModel the kernel's `checkDuplicatesInDirectRefs` **continues past `ref.hasWildCards()`**, so a plain or filtered wildcard (`Items*/Count`) may occupy several authored slots — it is expanded and consumed *again* at each position, so `Sum(Items*/Count, Items*/Count)` doubles the total. An indirect group/descendant overlap is still rejected (`MVK_DUPLICATE_PARAM2`, `checkDuplicatesInIndirectRefs`). *(Locked: `AggregateDuplicateWildcardTest` (typed authoring), `validate.laws.AggregateSlotLawsTest` (kernel accept/reject incl. the indirect PARAM2 gate), `adapter.laws.AggregateRepeatedWildcardDiffTest` (the consumed-twice runtime differential).)* The typed aggregate surface admits the repeated wildcard (`FieldListRef` has no dedup identity, mirroring value-list `Dsl.referenceIdentity`); the indirect overlap is not typed-expressible — `FieldOperand` permits only `FieldRef`/`FieldListRef`, no group operand.
- **Type laws**: `Sum` takes numbers only (**`MVK_NO_NUMBER`** — "Only numbers and amounts"); `MaxValue`/`MinValue` take numbers **or dates** (**`MVK_NOT_SORTABLE`** — "Only numbers, amounts or dates"); a **group argument** (`Sum(Group*)`, where the entity is a group not a field) is expanded to every descendant number field recursively and type-checked the same way — valid over a single- or multi-number-field group at any nested depth. **Read makes the expansion explicit while retaining each descendant's complete relative suffix**: `Sum(Group*)` converts to a Sum over every descendant number field's list (single field → one operand; several → `Sum(Group*/A, Group*/Nested/B)`), and that expanded form is itself kernel-valid — a sound rewrite (`adapter.SumOverGroupReadTest`, nested-depth lock `adapter.RecursiveGroupOperandExpansionReadTest`).
- **`NumberOfDifferentValues` is value-comparing**, so its operands must be type-homogeneous: string/enum together (**`MVK_STRING_ENUM_AND_NON_STRING_ENUM`**) or all numbers (**`MVK_NUMBER_AND_NON_NUMBER`**); booleans/confirms never (**`MVK_ONLY_STRING_ENUM_NUMBER_CMP_DATE_ALLOWED`**).
- Missing-row semantics (KERNEL-SEMANTICS §9): `Sum` substitutes 0 for unspecified fields; `MaxValue`/`MinValue` ignore them.

*(Locked: N7 examples of all four operators incl. three invalids; the pre-checks live in `ListAggregate`/`CountAggregate` — `dsl.operators.AggregateConditionTest`. The date variant of Max/Min landed with the date family — `DateListAggregate` via `Dsl.latestOf`/`earliestOf`, locked `adapter.DateAggregateReadTest` (stale "rides item 9" flipped 2026-06-10).)*

## §9 supplement — aggregate uncertainty + `FirstFilledValue` prefix sensitivity (runtime, 2026-06-10, RT-semantics drain)

> Fold: folded → §12.

The aggregate combiners track whether their result *could still change if more fields were filled*, and that uncertainty is observable through the message typing (§12 supplement below). Drained from `Combiner`/`NumberCombiner`/`FirstValueCombiner`/`NumberOfDifferentValuesCombiner`:

- **A starred aggregate over a repeatable group stays omission-typed even when every *instantiated* row is filled** — the un-instantiated remainder of the declared repeatability counts as an omitted, fillable tail. Only **exhausting the repetition** (all declared rows instantiated and filled) makes the fired comparison a value error. *(The probe corrected the naive source-read here, which expected the filled instance to be certain.)*
- **`NumberOfDifferentValues` counts distinct *filled* values only** — an empty operand never bumps the count, but it keeps the count growable (omission-typed).
- **`FirstFilledValue` is prefix-sensitive — operands after the first filled or formally unavailable value are never read.** A formal error *after* the first filled operand is invisible to the rule; *before* it, it suppresses the rule (unknown state); an *empty* operand before it types the fired comparison as an omission error. Each operand's `Having` marker is encountered immediately before that operand is scanned: a reached filter escalates a firing to OMISSION, while a filter on a later unvisited operand is invisible.
- **The date aggregates (`MaxValue`/`MinValue` over a DATE scope) follow the same uncertainty laws** — omitted tail ⇒ fillable, exhausted repetition ⇒ certain, and an empty operand keeps the result fillable. Probe-corrected source-read (second drain, 2026-06-10): the date combiner has *no* per-operand empty tracking, yet the typing comes out like the number combiner's — the not-given flag travels inside the `VkDate` value through `max`/`min`.

*(Locked: `adapter.laws.AggregateUncertaintyLawsTest` + `adapter.laws.FirstFilledValueLawsTest` + `adapter.laws.FirstFilledValueFilterEncounterDiffTest`.)*

**Interpreter status: `FirstFilledValue` is reproduced by one ordered short-circuit scan shared by value and polarity.** It visits operands and star rows in authored order, observes a slot's filter immediately before scanning that slot, and returns at the first filled or formally unavailable value. A later malformed cell or filtered slot is therefore never consulted. *(Interpreter-locked: `adapter.laws.FirstFilledValueDiffTest` + `adapter.laws.FirstFilledValueFilterEncounterDiffTest`; the fuzz net adds a `FirstFilledValue` rule — `PerfCatalog.fuzz()`.)*

## §9 supplement — SumOfProducts same-group requirement (source + probe, 2026-06-17)

> Fold: folded → §9.

`SumOfProducts(A*, B*)` requires its two repeatable number fields to live in the **same group** — fields from two different repeatable groups draw **`MVK_DIFFERENT_GROUPS`** (source `CheckEntityListenUtils.checkAllEntitiesInSameGroup`; probed: `SumOfProducts(Items*/Count, Items*/UnitWeight)` accepts, `SumOfProducts(Lines*/OrderedQty, Picks*/PickedQty)` across logistics' two repeatable groups rejects). BA adds (source-read, not separately probed): both operands must be Number with at least one repeatable layer, and the asterisk may notate only the lowest repeatable layer. *(Locked: `validate.laws.SumOfProductsLawsTest`.)*

**Runtime semantics + interpreter status (2026-06-24).** Source-read `RuntimeController.summeVonProdukten`: it **zips the two fields' row iterators** and sums `aᵢ·bᵢ` per row (`while both hasNext`) — both fields share the group, so the rows align. Each cell is `feldWertAlsZahl`, so an **empty cell reads as 0** (its product is 0). A malformed cell is suppressed by the formal mechanism (the rule references the field). The interpreter reproduces this with a new **row-aligned star accessor** `EvalContext.starAll(path)` (one entry per group row, empty → the kind's empty value, malformed → Unknown) — `Interpreter.sumOfProducts` pairs the two `starAll` lists and yields Unknown if any cell is Unknown. *(Interpreter-locked: `adapter.laws.SumOfProductsDiffTest` — the row-alignment catcher (26 vs a mis-paired 22) + empty-as-0 + malformed-suppression, against the live kernel; the fuzz net adds a `SumOfProducts` rule — `PerfCatalog.fuzz()`.)*

## §9 supplement — `RepetitionNotUnique` slot laws (slice 7b, 2026-06-04 experiments)

> Fold: not-foldable — authoring-tier slot laws (key shapes, placement codes); the runtime semantics are canonical in §9 "RepetitionNotUnique, precisely".

- **The rule sits at the parent**: every key field must have at least one repeatable group **between the rule group and the field** (or sit directly in a repeatable root group) — `RepetitionNotUnique(Addons/Name)` from *inside* `Addons` draws **`MVK_REPEATABLE_GROUP_MISSING`**; the same condition from the parent validates. Position-dependent → kernel-delegated, not pre-checked.
- **Keys are plain fields**: a group argument → `MVK_NO_GROUPS_ALLOWED`; a starred path → `MVK_NO_WILDCARDS_ALLOWED` (the iteration is implicit). **Composite keys** (several key fields) are legal. A key mixing in a non-repeatable field (e.g. `Tier`) → `MVK_REPEATABLE_GROUP_MISSING` for that field.
- **The rule must iterate** — with a non-iterating error field the plain key path draws the ordinary `MVK_NO_WILDCARD` before any RNU-specific check.
- **All keys must lie on a single path** — keys spread across two *parallel* repeatable groups (e.g. `Lines/LineRef, Picks/LineRef` on the logistics fixture) draw **`MVK_NOT_IN_SAME_PATH`**; `RepetitionNotUnique` cannot span a parallel iteration (source `CheckEntityListenUtils.checkAllRepeatableGroupsOnSamePath` — no longest-shared-path across the repeatable refs ⇒ throw; probe-confirmed 2026-06-17).
- **`@From`'s reference group must be repeatable** — `@From /Subscription` (root, non-repeating) draws **`MVK_GROUP_NOT_REPEATABLE`**; and the **keyed fields must descend from that `@From` group** — `RepetitionNotUnique(Lines/LineRef @From Picks)` (key in Lines, scope Picks) draws **`MVK_NOT_IN_GROUP`** (source `CheckEntityListenUtils.checkAllEntitiesInGroup` — each key's parent path must start with the `@From` group path; probe-confirmed 2026-06-17). Note `@From`/`@Von` is a **localized keyword** (lexer-bundle token `VON`), unlike the language-independent `In` separator.
- **`FieldValuesNotUnique` (DE `FeldWerteNichtEindeutig`) is a separate, plain field-list predicate** (the grammar's `feldListenBedingung` family, not `wiederholungBedingung`): cross-field distinctness over plain or starred operands — both shapes kernel-verified.

*(Locked: `validate.laws.IterationLawsTest` + N7 `RepetitionNotUnique`/`FromScope`/`FieldValuesNotUnique` examples; `@From` repeatability pre-checked in `RepetitionNotUniqueCondition.from`.)*

## §11 supplement — computation alternatives and the calculated-field ban

> Fold: folded → §11.

- **Alternatives are all-or-nothing about preconditions**: either a single alternative *without* a precondition, or several that *all* carry one — mixing a precondition-less "otherwise" in is rejected with **`MVK_ERROR_EMPTY_PRECONDITION`**. The default branch must be an explicit complementary precondition (as the demo's `BonusComp` does with `[WeekPay] < 500`).
- The computed field may not be referenced in preconditions **nor in operations** — both directions draw the same **`MVK_ERROR_REFERENCE_TO_CALCULATED_FIELD`** (KERNEL-SEMANTICS §11 states the precondition half). **The ban is on a *direct field* reference, not the field's containing group** (BA-doc cross-check, 2026-06-08): guarding a *repeatable* computed field with `GroupFilled`/`CurrentRepetition` on its **containing group** is kernel-legal — it's exactly the BA doc's current-repetition pattern (`CurrentRepetition(RuleGroup) == 1 → "…"` where the computed field lives in `RuleGroup`). rulekit's pre-check therefore uses **exact field-match** (`ConditionReferences.referencesField`), not the group-prefix `covers()` it uses for the rule error-field check — the earlier `covers()` over-rejected this valid shape.
- **`CurrentRepetition(group)` contributes no computed-value dependency edge** (source + differential, both strategies, 2026-07-21). The kernel's calculation-operand collector excludes the structural group operand: it supplies the current row coordinate, not the values of fields contained by that group. A computation guarded by `CurrentRepetition(Lines) > 0` may therefore coexist with a second computation that reads its target; expanding `Lines` into every computed descendant invents a cycle the kernel does not have. *(Locked: `CurrentRepetitionComputationDependencyDiffTest` tri-checks the independent guard plus the real downstream value dependency; multiplatform `CurrentRepetitionComputationDependencyTest` locks dependency ordering on JVM and Node.)*
- A wrong-kind operation (string expression into a number computed field) is **`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_TO_ENUM_OR_STRING`**.
- Computations require error text like rules do (`MVK_ERROR_MESSAGE_MISSING`).
- **Runtime evaluation (RT-semantics drain, overlap corrected IF175; division corrected IF177):** with **overlapping preconditions, the first declared alternative wins** the computed value, but the generated implicit validation keeps every holding alternative's mismatch. A legal `RoundAccounting(div, 2)` division by zero has no public value — fresh delta absent, stale value CLEARED — yet the numeric invalid sentinel survives `Abs`, `Min`/`Max`, and rounding, marks the target with `berechnungsWertFehler`, and poisons a dependent read. Arithmetic evaluates the one-pass lowered tree left-to-right: domain failure does not skip a later operand, while a thrown read poison aborts before it. A date shift intentionally maps an invalid numeric amount to a valueless date without that formal calculation error. A bare **division has unknown scale** and cannot assign to a scaled computed field (`MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES`, rhs "?") — wrap it in explicit rounding. *(Locked: `ComputationOverlapValidationDiffTest`; `DivisionDomainFailureComputationDiffTest` differentially locks the runtime projections and domain-left/right-poison separator, refutes use of the public formal-error inventory as a first-read trace, and locks generated-Java source order/catch; JVM+Node `DivisionDomainFailureComputationTest` observes the interpreter's selected poison; the earlier delta-only observation remains in `ComputationEvaluationLawsTest`.)*
- **`ICalculationInfo.getRoundingMode` is INERT in 30.8.1** (source-swept + probe 2026-06-26, INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF22): the slot exists (EXACT / RoundUp / RoundDown) but no runtime or codegen consumer reads it, and a non-EXACT mode does **not** relax the assignment scale gate (an unrounded division into a scaled field still draws `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES`) — an explicit `Round*` in the operation is the real mechanism.

*(Locked: `ComputationAuthoringTest`; the BA-doc worked-example shapes — concat, tiered+rounding, division/grouping, common-precondition concat, current-repetition, parallel-iteration — transferred to own domains in `adapter.BaComputationCorpusTest`.)*

## §11/§12 supplement — every computation is ALSO a validation rule (runtime, 2026-06-17)

> Fold: folded → §11.

The kernel auto-generates, per computation, an **implicit validation rule** `FieldFilled(F) And [F] != <term>` — so `validateFull` fires when a document's **stored** computed value disagrees with what the computation would produce. Probed deterministically with `CoverageWindow = DateRange(OrderDate, DeliveryDate)`: a stored `CoverageWindow` differing from the computed range fires; an agreeing one does not. **The fired errorCode is the computation's own name** (`CoverageWindowComp`). This is the mechanism behind the BA's two "Computation Rules as Validation Rules" self-fire traps: computing **`Now` into a field** self-fires (time elapses between the compute and validate steps), and **copying a precise date into a coarser date field** self-fires (the fragment's missing day/month read as `01`, so `[F] != [G]`). Authoring guidance: don't compute `Now` into a field; rebuild a coarse date with `Date("1","1",YearFromDate(G))` rather than copying it. *(Locked: `adapter.laws.ComputationValidationLawsTest`; the `Now`/precise-coarse cases are BA-described instances of the locked general mechanism.)*

**…and the generated rule is ANCHORED AT THE TARGET — an empty declaring container does not silence it (differential, both strategies, 2026-07-10).** The rule-from-computation fires at the **error entity (the computed field)** in the TARGET's scope, independent of the declaring group's instance content: a computation declared in an empty `*_Rules` container root, targeting `/Person/WeeklyWorkhours` in ANOTHER root (a real-workspace shape), still fires `<name>|OMISSION|/Person[1]/WeeklyWorkhours` when the stored value disagrees with the applicable tier's term (groovy-dynamic == java-static). A CROSS-GROUP computation into a **repeatable** target is unreachable — codegen-rejected with `MVK_ERROR_FIELD_NOT_IN_RULEGROUP` ("the error field is not in the rule group, but the condition specifies iteration"), even for a star-free condition — so only non-repeatable cross-group targets reach the runtime. *(Interpreter: the synthetic rule anchors at the target's parent group — the IF89 write-scope rule's validation twin; locked `adapter.laws.ImplicitComputationValidationScopeDiffTest` + multiplatform `interpreter … ImplicitComputationValidationScopeTest`; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF100.)*

**The exact generated condition — a GUARDED / TIERED computation is validated too (source, both strategies, 2026-07-07; overlap discriminator 2026-07-20).** The rule is built by the md-model `ValidationModelConverter.getComputationStatement` → `ComputationAlternativesJoiner.join()`: a **field gate** `FieldFilled(F) And`, then the optional `(commonPrecondition) And`, then the alternatives — a **single** alternative is `[(precondition) And] [F] != <operation>`; **>1** alternatives are the `Or` of each so-gated comparison `( (pre0 And [F] != op0) Or (pre1 And [F] != op1) … )` (plus a `@SuppressWarning(codes)` preamble when the computation declares `errorCodesToSuppress`, and a tolerance `UngleichMitToleranz*` in place of `!=` for a tolerance alternative). So the implicit validation fires when F is filled and **any holding tier** disagrees with the stored value; a false common precondition (or no holding tier) means no comparison holds, so it does not fire (the field is cleared on `compute`, and the stored-vs-cleared mismatch is deliberately NOT flagged). This is **not** computation's first-match selection: with two holding guards and operations `1` then `2`, computation stores `1`, yet a stored `1` still fails implicit validation through the second guarded mismatch; equal operations and a nonholding second guard are silent controls. Both kernel strategies share the md-model-generated rule ⟹ codegen-parity. *(Interpreter: `DmInterpreter.implicitComputationCond` builds this condition AST directly from the parsed alternatives; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF65/IF175. Locked: `adapter.laws.ComputationGuardedValidationDiffTest` + `ComputationOverlapValidationDiffTest`.)*

**Where the implicit rule FIRES — the pointer follows the computed field, even across subtrees and roots (differential, both strategies, 2026-07-07).** The generated rule's error entity is the computed FIELD's absolute location: the kernel resolves the DM-JSON `computedFieldRelPath` from the computation NODE (so `../../header/modelType` reaches a *sibling root*), independent of the computation's iteration group. On a multi-root (`header`+`content`) or sibling-subtree model the message therefore lands at the target's own pointer (e.g. `/header[1]/modelType`), never at a path mangled relative to the declaring group. *(Interpreter: INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF66 — the general relative-path resolution to the absolute target; locked `adapter.laws.MultiRootComputationPointerDiffTest` — cross-root + sibling-subtree + compute pointer + a static-JAVA tri-check.)*

## §12 supplement — every fired message is typed value-vs-omission (runtime, 2026-06-10, RT-semantics drain)

> Fold: folded → §12.

A dimension KERNEL-SEMANTICS doesn't cover (it presents the logic as two-valued): every message `validateFull` raises carries a **`MessageType`** — `OMISSION_ERROR` when the rule could be satisfied by *filling* one or more additional fields, `VALUE_ERROR` when only changing a value can satisfy it. Public surface: `IMessage.getMessageType()` (kernel-md-runtime-api). Internally this is the three-state `ValidierungsErgebnis` (`TRUE_WF` / `TRUE_AF` / `FALSE_OR_UNKNOWN`) whose whole public algebra is `combineUND`/`combineODER` — there is **no negation combinator** on it (the DSL likewise has no generic `Not`, KERNEL-SEMANTICS §1):

| combination | result | intuition |
|---|---|---|
| comparison over filled values | `VALUE_ERROR` | only changing the value helps |
| comparison fired on an empty-field substitution (number→0, confirm→False) | `OMISSION_ERROR` | filling the field could flip it |
| presence predicate (`FieldNotFilled`, …) | `OMISSION_ERROR` | filling satisfies it by definition |
| `And` of a value branch and an omission branch | `OMISSION_ERROR` | **omission wins** — filling could still rescue the whole rule |
| `Or` of a value branch and an omission branch | `VALUE_ERROR` | **value wins** — the value branch alone convicts |

*(Locked: `adapter.laws.ErrorTypeLawsTest`; the aggregate corollary — the omitted-tail typing — in `AggregateUncertaintyLawsTest`, §9 supplement above. Source: `ValidierungsErgebnis.combineUND/combineODER`, `BedingungsOperatorHelper.vergleiche`.)*

**A `Having`-filtered entity COUNT escalates the fired polarity only when the filter COUNTED a value; kept-nothing counts are grow-only (source + differential, both strategies, 2026-07-10).** `RuntimeController.anzahlAngegebenerEntities` (`NumberOfFilledFields`/`NumberOfFilledGroups`) sets `(kannGroesser=true, kannKleiner=true)` only via `containsFilteredValue` — a filtered operand that actually counted a value; a filter keeping NOTHING returns the ordinary flags: grow-only while `anzAllerFelder > anzAngFelder` (any uncounted cell — an empty one, the declared tail, or a FILLED cell the filter dropped), never shrink. A fired kept-nothing mismatch is therefore DIRECTIONAL (stored below the count → VALUE, stored above → OMISSION) — unlike the VALUE combiners, whose `getValue(containsFilter)` escalates both directions unconditionally (the IF104 rule). `NumberOfDifferentValues` follows the value-combiner rule (`kannGroesser/Kleiner = containsFilter || …`). *(Interpreter: `countFill`; locked `adapter.laws.FilteredCountPolarityDiffTest` + multiplatform twin; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF114.)*

**A fired STRING comparison is OMISSION iff an operand carries the NOT-GIVEN flag — and a CONCATENATION ORs the flag across its parts (source + differential, both strategies, 2026-07-10).** `RuntimeController.vergleicheSTRING(op, v1, v2, isNichtAngegeben)` types a fired `==`/`!=` `TRUE_AF` → `OMISSION_ERROR` when either operand is flagged `nichtAngegeben`, else `TRUE_WF` → `VALUE_ERROR`; `VkString.add` (the `+` concat) ORs the flags of its parts, so a concat containing ANY not-given read — an empty check-relevant field, a no-match/empty-cell semantic-index read, a not-given `FieldValueAsString`, a flag-preserving `Substring`/`RangeAsString` — keeps the whole concatenated value "not given": a fired mismatch against it is OMISSION ("filling could still change it"), even though the concatenated string itself is non-empty. A fully-given concat mismatch is VALUE. This drives the implicit computation-validation of concat operations (a stored value vs a computed concat over a no-match indexed read fires OMISSION). *(Interpreter: `arithFill`'s concat arm carries the flag (`concatNotGiven`); locked `adapter.laws.ConcatNotGivenPolarityDiffTest` + multiplatform twin; INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF113.)*

## The pre-`checkConsistency` tiers — the write path's output contract (instrument 4, 2026-06-04)

> Fold: not-foldable — serializer/deserializer-tier gates (the write path's output contract), outside the evaluation scope.

Two structural gates fire *outside* `checkConsistency`, found while scoping the F6 DM-JSON write path:

- **Deserialize tier (Jackson binding).** `serializer.deserialize(reader)` is a Jackson read — malformed JSON, an unknown `"type"` discriminator, an unparseable number/enum, and the **locale-format check** (`en_us` → "Invalid locale format", before any consistency pass) fail here as `IOException`/binding errors, *not* `MVK_*` codes. Not enum-enumerable; documented, not locked.
- **Convert tier (model assembly, found 2026-06-10 while locking the rule-slot laws).** Building the validation model rejects **two same-named sibling elements in one group** with a plain `IllegalArgumentException` ("Cannot have two child elements with the same name …") — *before* `checkConsistency`, so the `MVK_NAME_RULES_DUPLICATE` metamodel rule is shadowed by this tier for the same-group case. *(Locked: `validate.laws.RuleSlotLawsTest`.)*
- **Serialize/write gate — `BasicConsistencyCheck`.** `serialize(model, writer, …)` runs `BasicConsistencyCheck.checkModel` first and **aborts the write on any ERROR** — so this, not `checkConsistency`, is what an F6 `writeTo` must satisfy. Its `BasicDocumentModelConsistencyCategory` enum (16 categories) is the enumerable contract: `ID_EMPTY`, `NAME_EMPTY`, `ERROR_{ENTITY,CODE,CONDITION}_EMPTY`, `COMPUTED_FIELD_EMPTY`, `COMPUTATION_OPERATION_EMPTY`, `ENUM_TYPE_DUPLICATE_{VALUE,CATEGORIES}`, `LOCALIZED_TEXT_{ELEMENT,TYPEDEF}_NULL_PROBLEM`, `INDEX_FIELD_NAME_EMPTY`, `SORT_FIELD_NAME_EMPTY`, `TYPEDEF_{TYPE_REF,ID,NAME}_EMPTY`.

rulekit's authoring builders already satisfy the rule/computation subset (`ERROR_*`, `COMPUTED_FIELD/OPERATION`, `LOCALIZED_TEXT_ELEMENT`) by construction; the rest are model-structure concerns rulekit doesn't author (it only *adds rules/computations to an already-valid model*). *(Locked: `SerializerTierParityTest` — reflective parity over the category enum, so a kernel upgrade adding a category fails loudly.)*

## `formatConditionText` is lossy for a `$` correlation (2026-06-06)

> Fold: not-foldable — a kernel formatter-tool defect record (canonical-text lossiness), not evaluation behavior.

The kernel's condition formatter (`IMVK_Service.formatConditionText`, the parse+format round-trip behind rulekit's `canonicalConditionText`) **truncates a condition that carries a `$` outer-iteration correlation to its first conjunct**. A rule the kernel *accepts* and reports as valid —

```
FieldFilled(CustomerName) And Sum(Items*/Count Having [$/Order/Quantity] == [Items/Count]) > 5
```

— formats back to just `FieldFilled(CustomerName)`. The `$`-bearing `Sum(… Having …)` conjunct is silently dropped. (`hasValidConditionText` still returns `true` for the rule.) So the canonical form is **not a safe verbatim source** for such rules. *(Consequence for rulekit: `ReadResult.Opaque` preserves the **raw stored** `getErrorConditionText()`, not the canonical form — see KERNEL-INTEGRATION-NOTES. Observed while building the `$`-correlation read guard; not separately locked — the dependent behavior is locked by `adapter.OuterCorrelationReadTest`.)*

## The condition formatter knows exactly two languages — `Locale.US` / `Locale.GERMANY`; anything else silently falls back to German (2026-06-13)

> Fold: not-foldable — formatter/tooling locale behavior (condition-text rendering), not evaluation behavior.

The kernel's keyword-language switch for conditions (`formatAndTranslateConditionText(IRule, Locale, IProblemReporter)`, behind rulekit's `canonicalConditionText(rule, ConditionLanguage)`) resolves the target language through `AbstractMultiLanguageHandler`, whose **supported set is exactly two specific locales**:

```java
public static Set<Locale> SUPPORTED_CONDITION_LANGUAGES = Set.of(Locale.GERMANY, Locale.US);   // de_DE, en_US
// constructor: this.languageUsedInConditionText = SUPPORTED.contains(lang) ? lang : Locale.GERMANY;
```

So the language is keyed by **country**, not just language: `Locale.US` (en_US) → English keywords (`And`/`Or`/`If`); `Locale.GERMANY` (de_DE) → German (`Und`/`Oder`/`Wenn`). **Any other locale — including bare `Locale.ENGLISH` ("en") and `Locale.GERMAN` ("de") — is not in the set and silently defaults to `Locale.GERMANY`** (German), with no error and no diagnostic. Passing `Locale.ENGLISH` to translate an English model therefore returns *German* — the trap that surfaced this. (Source: `kernel-core-parser` `AbstractMultiLanguageHandler`/`ConditionMultiLanguageHandler`; the German branch is literally `Locale.GERMANY.equals(languageUsedInConditionText)`.)

*Consequence for rulekit:* the adapter's translate seam takes rulekit's **`ConditionLanguage`** (EN/DE), not a raw `Locale`, and maps `EN → Locale.US` / `DE → Locale.GERMANY` internally — the silent-German footgun is made unrepresentable at the N8 boundary. *(Locked: `adapter.KernelAdapterFormatTest` — EN must yield English keywords, DE German; a kernel change to the supported-locale set would surface as a German fallback the EN assertion catches.)*

## A model's condition language is independent of its display locale — and English is the convention (2026-06-20)

> Fold: not-foldable — model-config/serialization fact (which keyword language is parsed and stored), outside the evaluation scope.

The model header carries **two distinct, unrelated** language settings — conflating them is a trap:

| setting | where | governs |
|---|---|---|
| **display locale(s)** | header `locales[]` | labels + messages — a rule must carry a message for every declared locale (§13) |
| **condition language** | `modelConfig.conditionLanguage.code` | the keyword language the kernel **parses and stores** *all* DSL in — rule conditions, computation **operations**, and **preconditions** (`precondition`/`commonPrecondition`) alike: `FieldFilled`/`And` (en_US) vs `FeldAngegeben`/`Und` (de_DE); one of the two `SUPPORTED_CONDITION_LANGUAGES` (§above) |

Verified (experiment + the **a12-kernel-mm** corpus):

- **The kernel parses a candidate condition in the model's `conditionLanguage`** — a keyword in the *other* language is rejected `MVK_UNEXPECTED_TOKEN` ("Unexpected found 'FieldFilled'"). (Already noted, "model condition-language gates parsing", §CustomCondition.)
- **`conditionLanguage` need NOT be a declared display locale.** A model declaring only `de_DE` with `conditionLanguage=en_US` and English-keyword conditions is kernel-valid (`checkConsistency` passes — confirmed by a hand-flip experiment and `DocumentModelDraftTest`). The condition language is a free choice between the two supported, not constrained to `locales[]`.
- **English is the corpus convention.** All 59 a12-kernel-mm models — which declare `de_DE` as their *first* display locale — set `conditionLanguage=en_US` and author English-keyword rules (`FieldFilled`/`And`/`PatternViolated`/`RepetitionNotUnique`). German-keyword conditions are the rare, explicit case.

*Consequence for rulekit:* a from-scratch model defaults `conditionLanguage` to **en_US regardless of the display locale** (it used to derive it from the first display locale — breaking English conditions on a `de_DE` model; surfaced by probe `01-de`). Treatment + the single-source guard in [`KERNEL-INTEGRATION-NOTES.md`](KERNEL-INTEGRATION-NOTES.md). *(Locked: `DocumentModelDraftTest.conditionLanguageDefaultsToEnglishRegardlessOfDisplayLocale` — a de_DE draft keeps en_US and the kernel accepts it; `ModelNewVerbTest.aGermanLocaleModelStillAcceptsEnglishDslKeywords` — English DSL validates end-to-end on a de_DE model; `ModelNewVerbTest.aGermanLocaleModelAlsoAcceptsEnglishKeywordComputationsAndPreconditions` — the sibling lock proving the model-level fix covers computation operations + preconditions, not just rule conditions.)*

## A mount group's local elements SHADOW the include — the kernel does not expand it, and stays valid (2026-06-20)

> Fold: not-foldable — include/expansion mechanics, explicitly outside the canonical doc's scope.

A group that mounts an include carries `Group.modelAlias`. If that same group **also** has its own `Group.elements`, the kernel uses the **local elements** and does **not** expand the include — and the model is still **kernel-valid** (no error, no warning). Experiment (`field add` into a mount group): a mount of `address` resolving `/Person/Addr/{Street,City}`, after adding a local `Extra`, resolves **only** `/Person/Addr/Extra` — `Street`/`City` silently gone — and `model check` returns `valid:true`. So local content on a mount is a *silent override*, not an extension.

*Consequence for rulekit:* a routine `field add`/`group add` whose target group is a mount used to write a local element and report `applied`, silently discarding the entire mounted include. rulekit now **refuses** an element-add into a mount group (`RK_MOUNT_NOT_EDITABLE`) — see KERNEL-INTEGRATION-NOTES; the kernel's silent-override behavior itself is the finding here. *(Surfaced by probe `01-de`; locked by `MountEditRefusalTest`.)*

## An include's locales must be a superset of the base's — not the reverse (2026-06-13)

> Fold: not-foldable — include/expansion locale constraint, explicitly outside the canonical doc's scope.

The kernel's include locale-compatibility rule, pinned by experiment, runs in the direction that's easy to get backwards: **the included model must declare a superset of the base model's locales** — it must *cover* every locale the base declares. Extra locales in the include are harmless.

- base `{en_US}` includes `{en_US, de_DE}` → **loads** (the include over-covers; the extra `de_DE` is ignored).
- base `{en_US, de_DE}` includes `{en_US}` → **rejected on expand** (the include can't cover `de_DE`).

So a one-line "locale superset" note is ambiguous about direction; the constraint is *include ⊇ base*, enforced by the kernel on expand (not at the modelkit build tier). *(Locked: `modelkit.IncludeConstraintsTest`.)* Cycles and the single-root rule are likewise the kernel's to enforce on expand — modelkit doesn't re-implement them.

## A group's index field auto-generates its uniqueness + mandatory rules (2026-06-13)

> Fold: folded → §4.

Declaring `indexFieldName` is all the authoring needed — the kernel auto-generates the uniqueness and mandatory checks (canonical §4 "The index field's auto-generated checks"); modelkit's `indexField(name)` must **not** also emit the pair (the kernel would double them). *(BA §"Index Fields", runtime-confirmed; locked: `adapter.laws.IndexFieldLawsTest` — differential, so it doesn't depend on the generated rules' kernel-internal error codes.)*

## The DM-JSON deserializer ignores unknown field-config keys — a round-trip must read the value *back* (2026-06-13)

> Fold: not-foldable — a deserializer-tier tolerance (ignore-unknown keys), outside the evaluation scope.

The kernel's DM-JSON `JsonMapper` is annotated **`@JsonIgnoreProperties(ignoreUnknown = true)`** (`kernel-md-serializer`). So a misspelled or unsupported field-type key (e.g. `maxFractionDigits` for the real `maxFractionalDigits`, or a guessed `unit`) is **silently dropped** — the model still loads and validates clean, but the slot has *no effect*. Consequence for verifying a new field-config slot (FEATURE-GAPS): **"emit + load-valid" proves nothing about whether the engine honored the key** — only reading the value back through the kernel's typed field-type API (`INumberType.isZeroNotAllowed()`, `IStringType.getHintList()`, `IEnumerationType.getErrorMessage()`, …) proves it. rulekit's field-config round-trip tests therefore read back via `ModelIndex.fieldType(path)` rather than trusting a clean load. *(Locked: `modelkit.FieldConfigExtrasTest` — every field-config slot asserted via the kernel getter, not the emitted JSON.)*

## The number `trait`/unit slot is a clean lowercase field attribute — `AmountWithCent` is a *separate* derived category (2026-06-13, corrects an earlier deferral)

> Fold: not-foldable — a field-attribute/serialization fact; its one evaluation-adjacent consequence (the trait does not interpolate) is canonical in §13.

A number field's **unit** is the DM-JSON `trait` slot, whose allowed values are the **lowercase** tokens **`amount` / `percent` / `permille`** — the authoritative client contract (`kernel-md-facade` `api.ts`: *"allowed values are: 'amount' | 'percent' | 'permille'"*). It round-trips cleanly: emit `trait`, read it back via `INumberType.getTrait()` (`Optional<String>`); the runtime treats `amount` specially via `getTrait().equals("amount")` (`TypeConverter`).

This **corrects an earlier (same-day) deferral** that called the trait "inconsistent across tiers." The confusion was conflating two distinct things:

- the field's own **`trait`** slot — lowercase `amount`/`percent`/`permille`, what we model;
- the metamodel's **`AmountWithCent` / `AmountWithoutCent`** enum (in `CT_FIELD_TYPE_ATTRIBUTES` / `MM_SMM_FieldTypeChecks`, with **no** kernel-java references) — a **derived** category used only in *cross-field* type-compatibility checks (source-vs-target decimal places in a copy/computation), not a competing representation of the field's own trait.

So there is no inconsistency for the slot itself; modeling it is just `trait:"amount|percent|permille"`. The 0-or-2-decimal "with/without cent" distinction is the kernel's *derived cross-field* concern, not a field-level constraint we pre-check. **The trait does NOT render into message interpolation** (probe 2026-06-26, INTERPRETER-FINDINGS IF22): `$field.value$` of a `trait:"percent"` field interpolates the bare `5` (not `5%`), an `amount` field the bare `5.00` (no currency symbol) — the unit is a UI concern, invisible to §13 interpolation. *(Locked: `modelkit.FieldConfigExtrasTest.numberUnitTraitRoundTripsThroughTheKernel`.)* Modeled via `NumberFieldBuilder.unit(NumberTrait)`.

## `modelConfig.supportedCharacters` is enforced at runtime, not just authored — out-of-set values raise `ZeichenNichtImZeichensatz` (2026-06-13)

> Fold: folded → §3.

The model-config **allowed-character set** (`content.modelConfig.supportedCharacters`, a `List<String>` whose entries can be a singleton, a range such as `" -~"` = space-through-tilde, or an atomic combined sequence; empty = the full BMP except surrogate code units — `IDocumentModelConfig.getSupportedCharacters()`) is a **real runtime constraint**, not codegen-only metadata. The interpreted `IDocumentRtService.validateFull` checks string/enum/custom field **values** against that charset: a value carrying any character or sequence outside the set raises the formal error **`ZeichenNichtImZeichensatz`** ("character not in the character set" — a German-named code, not `MVK_*`-prefixed); a value drawn wholly from the set is clean. Empirically confirmed (a model restricted to `A-Z`: value `"abc"` fires, `"ABC"` does not). So this slot can be both authored *and* behaviorally locked. *(Locked: structural round-trip `modelkit.SupportedCharactersTest` via `IDocumentModelConfig.getSupportedCharacters()`; runtime fires/does-not-fire `adapter.laws.SupportedCharactersLawsTest`.)* Modeled via `DocumentModelDraft.Builder.supportedCharacters(...)` / `ConfigEditor.supportedCharacters(...)` / CLI `config modify --supported-character`.

**The source mechanism — the slot IS the §3 `legalCodepoints` (pinned from source 2026-07-11, definition boundary completed 2026-07-19 for IF151).** The model slot and the §3 project/codegen charset are **one effective charset**, not two composed ones: `ExternalDocumentModelService.createCodeGeneratorParameters` passes `getDocumentModelConfig().getSupportedCharacters()` straight into `CodeGeneratorParameters`, the codegen bakes it into the generated meta-model's `getLegalCodepoints()`, and `FormalChecker` is the single check that raises `ZeichenNichtImZeichensatz`; singleton/range membership uses `LegalCharTester.rangeSearch`, while combined entries use the trie-backed path. In the dynamic-codegen path the slot is the sole source of the legal charset — `DocumentProcessingConfig` carries no charset override. `CharsetUtil.parse` classifies an entry as a range iff `length()==3 && charAt(1)=='-'`, a singleton iff `length()==1`, else a combined sequence. `KernelUtils.validateCharset` rejects an empty entry, a surrogate-bearing entry, a reversed range, a plain multi-character string with no combined grapheme, a combined entry over three UTF-16 units, and its specific ambiguous-overlap class. Non-ambiguous shared prefixes and a complete sequence that is also a terminal prefix of a longer entry are legal. Validation happens before either evaluation strategy runs; both strategies then use the same prepared trie behavior. **Empty list ⇒ BMP**: `LegalCharTester.noCharsetDefined()` makes a value legal iff it carries no surrogate. **Locks:** `SupportedCharactersDefinitionDiffTest` pins the structural accept/reject boundary; `SupportedCharactersGraphemeDiffTest` tri-checks the legal shared/terminal-prefix runtime cases; `LegalCharsetDefinitionTest` and `LegalCharsetGrowthTest` lock the interpreter boundary and bounded matcher on JVM and Node.

## An include mount can exclude the included model's rules/computations — `excludeRules`/`excludeComputations`, applied at expand (2026-06-13)

> Fold: not-foldable — include/expansion mechanics, explicitly outside the canonical doc's scope.

A mount group (one carrying `modelAlias`) may set the booleans **`excludeRules`** and **`excludeComputations`** in its `Group` body, beside `modelAlias`. They are honored at **expand**: with `excludeRules: true`, the kernel does **not** carry the included model's own rules into the expanded model (its *fields* still mount — only the rules are dropped); `excludeComputations` does the same for computations. Empirically confirmed: a price-list model with rule `LIST_PRICE_NEG`, mounted plainly, has that code in the expanded model; mounted with `excludeRules`, it does not, while the mounted `ListPrice` field remains.

The kernel ties the flag to a mount — metamodel rule **`EXCLUDE_RULES_INVALID`** (`MM_Group_1`): `FieldFilled(excludeRules) And FieldNotFilled(modelAlias)` is an error ("excludeRules filled and modelAlias not filled"), with a twin `EXCLUDE_COMPUTATIONS_INVALID`. So the slot is meaningful only where there's a `modelAlias`; our API only emits it on a mount, so that refusal is structurally unreachable from rulekit. *(Locked: `edit.IncludeExcludeRulesTest` — plain mount keeps the rule, `excludeRules` drops it but keeps the fields, via the kernel's own expand.)* Modeled via `MountOptions.single().excludingRules()` → `ModelDocument.mount(...)` / `IncludeEditor.mount(...)` / CLI `include add --exclude-rules`.

## A type-def-only model is just a root-less `document` model; a consumer imports it with `purpose: "typeDefinitions"` (2026-06-13)

> Fold: not-foldable — model-reference/import mechanics, outside the evaluation scope.

There is **no separate model discriminator** for a type-definition library. A "type-def-only" model is a normal `modelType: "document"` model that carries `content.typeDefinitions` and **no root groups** — the kernel accepts it standalone (empirically: a model with an empty `modelRoot.rootGroups`, *and* one with no `modelRoot` at all, both load + `checkConsistency`-pass). So authoring one is just emitting type-defs with an empty root.

A consumer pulls those types in via a header `modelReferences` entry whose **`purpose` is `"typeDefinitions"`** — the kernel constant **`PURPOSE_4_ONLY_TYPE_DEFS = "typeDefinitions"`** (`DocumentModelExpandSanityChecks`), **not** `"import"`. At expand, `DocumentModelExpandImportService` walks the referenced model and merges **all** its type-defs into the consumer's namespace **by id** (`addUniqueTypeDef` / `getImportedTypeDefsById`) — so a consuming field references an imported type by a **plain `typeDefinitionId`** (no alias prefix; the entry's `alias` is just a label). This differs from an `include` (`purpose: "include"`, which mounts *structure* under a group via `modelAlias`). The kernel checks `purpose == "typeDefinitions"` before expanding an import (`checkTypeDefPurpose`) and that the imported model's locales cover the consumer's.

Consequence for authoring: a consumer's `typedefField` may reference an **imported** id that isn't locally declared, so rulekit's draft-time local-typedef-ref check defers to the kernel when an import is present (the kernel resolves it at expand). *(Locked: `modelkit.TypeDefModelImportTest` — type-def-only standalone valid; consumer-from-scratch import + imported-typed fields; the edit path.)* Modeled via `DocumentModelDraft.typeDefModel(id)` (provider), `Builder.importTypeDefs(ref)` / `ModelDocument.addImport(ref)` / `EditSession.addImport(ref)` / CLI `typedef import --reference` (consumer).

## Attachment & Multi-Select are `usageType` group templates with kernel-validated canonical compositions (2026-06-13, source-verified)

> Fold: not-foldable — model-structure templates validated at consistency time; no evaluation behavior beyond the ordinary rules they mandate.

A12 has two **group templates**, flagged by a group's **`usageType`** slot (`IGroup.getUsageType()` → `Optional<String>`; the enum is defined in the `MM_Group_1` metamodel). Setting `usageType` flips the kernel into validating a strict canonical composition — both checks report `Severity.ERROR`, so a malformed template fails `checkConsistency` (our `validate()` gate is the oracle). The same marker drives the UI widget (upload control / checkbox list). Sources: `AttachmentGroupRule` / `MultiSelectGroupRule` in `kernel-md-model/.../internal/service/consistency/rules`, and `DocumentModelUtils.ATTACHMENT_NAME = "attachment"` (multi-select id is the literal `"multi-select"`).

**`usageType: "attachment"`** — `AttachmentGroupRule` requires, on that group:

| Requirement | Detail | Violation category |
|---|---|---|
| 8 fields, by exact name | `original_filename`, `internal_filename`, `content`, `attachment_id`, `size`, `mime_type`, `category`, `description` (extras tolerated — the rule only checks the set is *present*; order not checked) | `DOCUMENT_MODEL_ATTACHMENT_MISSING_FIELD` |
| 4 rules, matched by exact `errorCondition` string in the model's condition language | see below | `DOCUMENT_MODEL_ATTACHMENT_REQUIRED_RULES` |
| `content` field config | a `StringType` with `noValueValidation: true` **and** `lineBreaksPermitted: true` | `DOCUMENT_MODEL_ATTACHMENT_CONTENT_NO_VALUE_VALIDATION` |

The 4 required `errorCondition`s (compared after the kernel re-formats them, so emit the canonical form verbatim; `RuleGroup`/`RegelKontext` is the rule's own group context):

| en_US (`Locale.US`) | de_DE (`Locale.GERMANY`) |
|---|---|
| `GroupFilled(RuleGroup) and NotExactlyOneFieldFilled(attachment_id, content)` | `KontextAngegeben(RegelKontext) und NichtGenauEinFeldAngegeben(attachment_id, content)` |
| `FieldFilled(content) and FieldNotFilled(size)` | `FeldAngegeben(content) und FeldNichtAngegeben(size)` |
| `GroupFilled(RuleGroup) and FieldNotFilled(internal_filename)` | `KontextAngegeben(RegelKontext) und FeldNichtAngegeben(internal_filename)` |
| `GroupFilled(RuleGroup) and FieldNotFilled(mime_type)` | `KontextAngegeben(RegelKontext) und FeldNichtAngegeben(mime_type)` |

`AttachmentGroupRule.RULES_REQUIRED` is keyed by `Locale.US`/`Locale.GERMANY` **only**, so an attachment group is well-defined only for an `en_US`/`de_DE` condition-language model (it lines up with the formatter's two-locale limit, above). A wrong field type for the other 7 fields is *not* checked by this rule (only `content` is type-checked) — but the 4 conditions must still bind (`size` must be a field, etc.), so the conventional types are `size` = Number, the rest = String.

**`usageType: "multi-select"`** — `MultiSelectGroupRule` requires, on that group:

| Requirement | Violation category |
|---|---|
| `repeatability > 1` (a repeatable group) | `DOCUMENT_MODEL_MULTI_SELECT_REPEATABLE_GROUP` |
| exactly one `Field` element | `DOCUMENT_MODEL_MULTI_SELECT_REQUIRED_FIELD` |
| that field is an `EnumerationType` or `StringType` | `DOCUMENT_MODEL_MULTI_SELECT_FIELD_FIELDTYPE` |
| that field has a `requirednessConfig` (not empty) | `DOCUMENT_MODEL_MULTI_SELECT_FIELD_REQUIRED_FLAG` |
| that field is **not** the group's index field | `DOCUMENT_MODEL_MULTI_SELECT_FIELD_INDEX_FIELD` |
| only `Field` + `Rule` elements — no sub-groups | `DOCUMENT_MODEL_MULTI_SELECT_OTHER_ELEMENTS` |

So **attachment is a heavy fixed template** (no user input beyond the group name/parent — all 8 fields + 4 rules + the `content` config are mandated boilerplate); **multi-select is light but parameterized** (the single enum/string field, its values, and its requiredness are the user's). *(Source-verified by reading the two rule classes + `MM_Group_1`, then **locked by the generators**: `modelkit.MultiSelectTemplateTest` / `modelkit.AttachmentTemplateTest` each build the template, assert the kernel **accepts** it (the attachment accept-test proves the 4 emitted condition strings round-trip byte-for-byte), and assert a **tampered** template is rejected with the right `DOCUMENT_MODEL_*` category. The generator refuses a non-`en_US`/`de_DE` model rather than emit rules the kernel can't validate — which would also NPE its `RULES_REQUIRED.get(locale)`.)*

Removal: a composite group's internal rules live inside its subtree, so `group remove` takes them with it — the safe-delete gate **excludes in-subtree referrers** so it doesn't demand `--cascade` for rules the author never wrote (CLI-SPEC §6; `cli.GroupAttachmentVerbTest`).

## Descriptions + annotations are element-wide (`IElement`/`IFieldTypeDefinition` are `IDescribed`+`IAnnotated`); the model carries a `comment` (2026-06-13)

> Fold: not-foldable — element-metadata/serialization structure, outside the evaluation scope.

Every element and type-def carries the same documentation metadata, not just fields: **`IElement extends IIdNamed, IDescribed, IAnnotated`** and **`IFieldTypeDefinition extends IIdNamed, IDescribed, IAnnotated`**. So a field, group, *and* rule (all `IElement`) and a type definition each have:

- **`internalDescription` / `externalDescription`** (`IDescribed`) — per-locale text (`ILocalizedTextMap extends Map<Locale,String>`), serialized **element-level** as `[{locale, text}]` (alongside `annotations`, beside the `Field`/`Group`/`Rule` body — uniform across kinds). Distinct from a field's `label` and its `helperText` (`IField`-only).
- **`annotations`** (`IAnnotated`) — `[{name, value}]`, element-level.

Separately, the model has a free-text **`comment`** at `content.modelInfo.comment` (`IDocumentModelInfo.getComment()` → `Optional<String>`) — distinct from the header `labels`/`annotations`. (The other "comment" is the inline `;;` concrete-syntax comment inside a condition/computation — a different mechanism, carried in the rulekit DSL.)
